  100 ms, or 390 ms on a phone-like CPU, for a first set of questions). Releasing takes 80–200 ms.

### Loading positions of the pelicandromes: the job `pelican/import` (SOG-0083, alpha.5)

The user's file `pelicandromes_positions.yaml` (schema `pelicandromes` v1, WGS84; per site `nom`, `oaci`, `categorie`, `latitude`, `longitude`, `cap` (optional: the orientation of the berthing, an integer from 0 to 359; absent or empty = unknown; SOG-0521), `statut`, `source`; « position de chargement uniquement si vérifiée manuellement, ne pas utiliser pour la navigation réelle ») is imported from Downloads into **`data/pelicandromes/<country>.json`**, published in the data repository (one commit by the page's author, only the compiled file; the YAML is not copied). Called like every job: `launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'pelican', verb: 'import', args })`.

`args` (closed): `{ dry?: true (default), country?: 'fr' (2 letters, default 'fr'), file?: { name, size } }`. The file is found **by the pattern** `pelicandromes_positions*.yaml` in Downloads (the latest; a `.yaml.pdf` copy does not match), never by a path from the page. The plan (`dry`) reads and checks it and answers without writing; the take needs the `file` the plan showed (`not-shown`, `changed` otherwise).

Result: `{ ok, dry, file: { name, size, sha256 } | null, sites, categories: { <category>: n }, rejected: [{ index, error }], written, commit, reason?, message? }`; `reason`: `no-file` (with `expected`: the pattern), `invalid` (not a readable pelicandromes file: `message` says why), `not-shown`, `changed`, `failed`. On this workstation's file: 32 sites, 26 `principal`, 2 `temporaire`, 1 `mobile`, 1 `projet`, 1 `outre-mer`, 1 `à confirmer`, none rejected.

**The compiled file**: `{ schema: 'pelicandromes', version: 1, country, datum: 'WGS84', note, source: { name, size, sha256 }, sites: [{ name, icao, category, lat, lon, cap?, status, source, country }] }` (`cap`: an integer from 0 to 359, **the key is missing when it is unknown**, so a file without cap compiles to the same bytes as before; a cap that is present but is not an integer from 0 to 359 — 360, -1, 270.5, a text, a boolean — refuses that site, with the reason « cap hors de 0 à 359 (un entier) » in `rejected`). `country` is on the file **and on each site** (the next countries come as `<country>.json` with the same shape). Nothing is timestamped: the same file gives the same bytes, a second take writes nothing and makes no commit. `category` and `status` are free short texts (the categories of the file are not a closed list); a site is rejected, with its index, when its name is missing, its position is missing, not a number or out of range, or its OACI code is not 2–4 letters or digits.

**The file is the user's, so untrusted like everything in `data/`**: at most 256 kB (before it is read), UTF-8 text, at most 500 sites; a reduced YAML grammar of the standard library (one document, scalars, **one list of flat records**: anchors, tags, `{ }` / `[ ]`, `|` / `>` blocks, tabs, nesting and duplicate keys are refused with the line); numbers finite and bounded (latitude ±90, longitude ±180, 7 decimals), booleans and `NaN` are not numbers; texts without control characters, **without `<` or `>`**, blanks collapsed, 200 signs at most. **The consumer sets the texts with `textContent`, never as HTML.** The positions are loading positions checked by hand: the note travels with the file, to be shown wherever they are.

### The pelican sites: `pelicandromes()` (SOG-0521)

**`await aerodb.pelicandromes({ country?, near?, limit? })`** is the one reader of `data/pelicandromes/<country>.json` (declared as a `reference` read: prefix `pelicandromes/`). A module, Création's workshop included, never reads the file itself.

- **Query** (closed; any other key is ignored): `country` — two lower-case letters, default `fr` (else `reason: 'country'`, nothing read); `near` — `{ lat, lon, radius_nm? }`, `lat` ±90 and `lon` ±180 as finite numbers (a text is refused: `reason: 'near'`), `radius_nm` 0–5 000 (default 100); `limit` — an integer, 100 by default, 500 at most.
- **Answer**: `{ ok: true, country, datum: 'WGS84', note, total, count, rejected, radiusNm?, sites }`; each site `{ name, icao, category, lat, lon, cap, status, country }` (`cap`: the orientation of the berthing, an integer from 0 to 359, **`null` when unknown** — optional in the file, always present in the answer; a value that is not such an integer comes out `null`) and, with `near`, `distanceNm` (great circle, 0.1 NM), the nearest first; without `near`, by name. `lat` / `lon` are the **loading positions** verified by hand; the `note` of the file says so and travels with the answer: show it wherever they are shown, never for real navigation. Frozen (the answer and each site).
- **Refusals**: `{ ok: false, reason }`: `country`, `near`, `absent` (no file: not published or not read; not remembered, the next question reads again), `too-large` (more than 256 kB), `invalid` (not `schema: 'pelicandromes'` version 1, no list of sites, or the file's own `country` is another country).
- **The file is untrusted** (a pilot can push to the data repository): the known fields only (`name`, `icao`, `category`, `lat`, `lon`, `status`; the `source` of a site and any key the file adds do not come out; `__proto__` / `constructor` are never read: keys are taken as OWN keys), finite numbers in their bounds (a text `'45'` is not a number), texts without control signs, `<` `>` or the invisible format characters of `frame/lib/untrusted.js` (bidi marks and isolates, the Arabic mark, zero-width signs; the joiners are kept as there), blanks folded, 200 signs at most (60 for a category or a status, 4 for a code: a code that is not 2–4 letters or digits comes out empty); at most 500 sites (the others counted in `rejected`); a site without a name or a position is dropped and counted. **The consumer sets the texts with `textContent`.** The guards are the frame's (`frame/lib/untrusted.js`, § 12c of `frame/INTEGRATION.md`) written again inside `aerodb.js`, which does not load frame files: they move to the shared file when aerodb may depend on it.
- **Memory**: the cleaned file is kept per country while the revision the reader gives is the same; a changed revision is read again.

Case K398 (browser; an invented file and hostile ones).

The Map layer (beta.1) is BUGS SOG's and may keep reading the file through the data repository; the other consumers ask this function.

### The state per zone and « Mettre à jour »: the verbs `base/status` and `base/update` (SOG-0071, V3 § 10.44, alpha.5)

Both are launcher jobs like the others (`launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'base', verb, args })`, `job.result`). The launcher holds ONE job at a time: **`base/status` is refused (409) while a build runs**; the state `building` is the page's (it reads `job.data.zones` of the running job), never in this answer.

**`base/status`** `{ zones?: [ids] (1–60; default: the zones the base publishes), personal?: [sources] }` — one call, nothing written, under a second:
`{ ok, current (the cycle in force), parserVersion (what the build page builds with), counts: { <state>: n }, zones: { <zone>: { state, reason, reasons, buildable, builtCycle, expectedCycle, parserVersion } } }`.

- `state`: `up-to-date` | `stale` | `never-built` | `failed`. A zone with no folder in `data/sia/base/<zone>/` is `never-built` (a zone the pilot ticked and never built).
- `reasons`: `[{ code, … }]` in this order, `reason` = the first (`null` when up to date); `buildable`: a build would change something. The codes and their parameters:

| code | parameters | buildable | meaning (the page's words, four languages below) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `never-built` | — | yes | no built folder |
| `failed` | `error` | yes | the last build of this zone failed (kept until the next one succeeds) |
| `pickup-waiting` | `source`, `set?`, `cycle?`, `zip` | **no** | a file of Downloads is not taken yet (`base/pickup` takes it, the plan first): NATS one set at a time (AIP, OBS, UAS), the SIA zip whose cycle is newer than the one held |
| `source-newer` | `source`, `cycle`, `built` | yes | the dated source (SIA, NATS, ENAIRE) is of a cycle newer than the build |
| `inputs-changed` | `count`, `names` (3 first) | yes | a source file of the zone changed (by hash) since the build read it |
| `parser-old` | `built`, `current` | yes | built by an older parser (`parserVersion` of its `meta.json` under the build page's) |
| `files-missing` | `count` | yes | files missing in the built folder |
| `source-old` | `source`, `cycle`, `current` | **no** | the dated source is older than the cycle in force: a newer file is to be fetched or dropped |

A zone **without a dated source is never stale by the cycle** (openAIP-only countries: an AIRAC change is nothing for them). `up-to-date` has no reason at all. On this workstation (10 Nov): 45 zones built, all `parser-old` (built at parser 21, current 22: the direction of the airways); gb `pickup-waiting` (AIP and OBS of 29 Oct) then `source-old`.

**What the build read** is kept apart: **`data/local/build/<zone>.json`** (local to the workstation, never published, written by `base/build` after each zone): `{ zone, cycle, builtAt, parserVersion, personal, inputs: { <name>: { size, mtime, sha256 } }, failed?: { error, at } }`. `inputs-changed` compares hashes (a file is hashed again only when its size or date moved), so a file rewritten with the same content is not a change. A zone built **before** this record exists is judged on its cycles, parser and files only (no `inputs-changed` until its next build).

**`base/update`** `{ zones, personal?, fetch?: true }` — « Mettre à jour »: (1) `base/fetch` of the zones (only what changed), (2) the state, (3) `base/build` of **the zones that are not up to date and buildable**, (4) the state again. Result: `{ ok, fetched: { ok, fetched, unchanged, failed, manual } | null, before, upToDate: [zones], waiting: [{ zone, reasons }], notBuildable: [zones], built, failed, build: { ok, reason?, cycle, cycles, folders, commits, seconds }, after, nothing, reason? }`. **Idempotent**: when nothing moved `nothing` is `true` and no browser starts. **The pickup is not done by the update**: a file in Downloads is shown (`base/pickup` plan) then taken by the pilot's answer, so the file shown is the file taken (Sécurisation's rule); a zone that waits for it is in `waiting`. The job's `phase` (`fetch`, `status`, `build`, `done`) is in `job.data.phase`; the build's per-zone progress is in `job.data.zones` as for `base/build`. **« Tout recalculer »** stays `base/build` on the whole selection.

**The reasons in the pilot's words** (the page writes them with `K.t`; `{…}` are the parameters, dates written as « 29 OCT »):

| code | fr | en | de | es |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `never-built` | Jamais construite | Never built | Noch nie erstellt | Nunca construida |
| `failed` | Dernière construction en échec | Last build failed | Letzter Build fehlgeschlagen | La última construcción falló |
| `pickup-waiting` | {set} {cycle} dans Téléchargements, non repris | {set} {cycle} in Downloads, not taken yet | {set} {cycle} in Downloads, noch nicht übernommen | {set} {cycle} en Descargas, sin tomar |
| `source-newer` | Source {source} du {cycle} plus récente que la construction du {built} | {source} source of {cycle} newer than the build of {built} | Quelle {source} vom {cycle} neuer als der Build vom {built} | Fuente {source} del {cycle} más reciente que la construcción del {built} |
| `inputs-changed` | {count} fichier(s) source modifié(s) depuis la construction | {count} source file(s) changed since the build | {count} Quelldatei(en) seit dem Build geändert | {count} archivo(s) fuente modificado(s) desde la construcción |
| `parser-old` | Construite avec un ancien analyseur (v{built}, actuel v{current}) | Built with an older parser (v{built}, current v{current}) | Mit einem älteren Parser erstellt (v{built}, aktuell v{current}) | Construida con un analizador antiguo (v{built}, actual v{current}) |
| `files-missing` | {count} fichier(s) manquant(s) | {count} file(s) missing | {count} Datei(en) fehlen | faltan {count} archivo(s) |
| `source-old` | Source {source} du {cycle}, cycle en vigueur {current} : un fichier plus récent est à déposer | {source} source of {cycle}, cycle in force {current}: a newer file is to be dropped | Quelle {source} vom {cycle}, gültiger Zyklus {current}: neuere Datei ablegen | Fuente {source} del {cycle}, ciclo vigente {current}: falta un archivo más reciente |

### The ground read after the procedures (SOG-0135) and the world forecast job `weather/world` (SOG-0138)

**Ground after the procedures** (10 Nov 2026): « on ne va pas créer un bouton en plus: si on décolle ou qu'on se pose, on va devoir rouler ». When `AeroDb.fetchProcedures(icao)` succeeds, the ground layer of the same aerodrome is read **right after**, in the background (`fetchGround`, the same OpenStreetMap reading, about 30 s), **only if** `proc/ground/<ICAO>.json` is missing or dated before the cycle in force. The procedures' answer does not wait for it and is never changed by it: a ground read that fails (launcher busy, too soon, nothing in OpenStreetMap) is silent. The end of the ground read is announced as before, `on('change', { ground: icao })`; `ground(icao)` then returns it. `fetchProcedures(icao, { ground: false })` skips it. The panel's own ground button is not needed any more (BUGS SOG removes it); `fetchGround(icao)` stays for a direct gesture.

**`weather/world`** `{ action?: 'build' (default) | 'status' }` (launcher job, `launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'weather', verb: 'world', args })`): the world synoptic forecast of the Atmosphere module is made on the workstation. Until now `tools/build_world_weather.py` ran only in the Pages workflow at deployment: on the workstation nothing ran it, so the map had no world layer.
- **What the tool reads**: NOAA GFS 1° through NOMADS (the GRIB filter, no key, no quota), decoded by `tools/grib2_lite.py`, brought to a 5° mesh (2 376 places), one file per 3-hour step from the current step to +72 h (25 steps) plus a second file of the pressure levels per step. **Weight of a publication: 17.1 MB, 51 files** (an index of 2.9 kB, 25 step files and 25 pressure-level files), measured on 9 Nov 2026 (run 06Z).
- **Where**: **`data/weather/world/`** (`index.json` and the step files, same layout as the Pages publication): in `data/`, **git-ignored** (`weather/` in `data/.gitignore`, put there by the verb when it is missing, and in `.git/info/exclude`), **outside the launcher's tree** (`/__setup/changes`, `FRAME_SKIP`: never offered to the synchronisation, never copied to the devices' cache), served as `/data/weather/world/` by the launcher (SOG-0138: `data/local/` is never served, `hidden()`, audit of 7 Oct; the first versions wrote there and no real launcher could serve it). Moved once from `data/local/weather/world/` by the verb. `weatherStore.world()` reads `<root>/weather/world/` (the Pages publication), then `<root>/data/weather/world/`.
- **`build`**: runs the tool in a work folder under `data/weather/`, **checks** what it wrote (an index of schema 1 and model `gfs`, at most 25 steps, file names of the tool's own pattern only, no sub-folder nor link, every file the index names present, at most 51 files and 40 MB), then replaces the publication in one move; the previous publication is given to the tool (`--keep`): **the same GFS run is not asked of the NOAA again** (second call on the same run: 13 s instead of 128 s). 15 minutes at most. One start per ten minutes (`too-soon`, 429).
  Result `{ ok, kept (the previous publication was reused), exists, run (ISO, the GFS run), built, step, times: [first, last step], steps, files, bytes, age, seconds }`; `reason`: `no-run` (no run published or the NOAA unreachable: the old publication stays), `invalid` (the folder is not a valid forecast: nothing replaced), `timeout`, `cancelled`, `failed`.
- **`status`**: the same fields from the publication in place (`exists: false` without one); no network.
- **For Données** (the line « Synoptique monde (GFS) »): `status` for the run, the age and the weight, `build` for the gesture. The map shows the layer as soon as a publication exists.

### Country packs and thematic filters: `AeroDb.packs()` (SOG-0065, V3 § 10.42, alpha.5)

`frame/aerodb/packs.json` (11 kB, made by `python tools/build_aero_packs.py`, from Natural Earth v5.1.2, public domain, and hand lists) and **`await aerodb.packs()`**, read once, frozen: `{ generated, source, regions: [pack], themes: [pack], packs: [pack] }` — **`packs` is the contract with Réglages' rows: one list, regions then themes, `kind: 'region' | 'theme'`, `date` and `source` on each pack** (`regions` and `themes` are the same packs by kind) — with `pack = { id, kind, date, source, names: [fr, en, de, es], zones: [zone ids], partial?: [zone ids], empty?: 'no-zone-no-source', basis }`. Zones the list of zones does not know are dropped, names cleaned (no control sign, no `<` `>`), 60 packs and 250 zones each at most; `null` when the file cannot be read (the next call tries again). Nothing is built or published by a pack.

- **Two levels, never mixed.** **A pack is a list of zone ids**, static, many-to-many (Turkey is in `middle-east` and `mediterranean`; `fr`, which carries its overseas territories, is in several). **A theme is a filter**: the zones that touch an object; it shows which zones to add and adds none.
- **The 22 packs (`regions`)**: 18 from Natural Earth's `SUBREGION` (`north-america` 5 zones, `central-america` 26 with the Caribbean, `south-america`, `europe-north`, `europe-west`, `europe-south`, `europe-east`, `africa-north`, `africa-west`, `africa-central`, `africa-east`, `africa-south`, `middle-east` 19, `asia-central`, `asia-south`, `asia-east`, `asia-southeast`, `oceania`), 3 hand lists (`mediterranean`, the riparian states; `australia-indonesia`; `arctic`, whose `partial` says the zones that are whole countries only partly inside the circle: ca fi no ru se us) and **`antarctica`, empty** with `empty: 'no-zone-no-source'` (no zone and no data source today: Données lists it as empty with its reason). Every zone is in at least one pack except the oceanic `nat`.
- **The 6 themes**: `islands` (37 zones: the island states, a hand list), `himalaya` (bt cn in np: the zones that Natural Earth's HIMALAYAS polygon touches), `deserts-steppes` (46: the Natural Earth « Desert » regions of scale rank 3 and above, without PUNJAB and CAATINGAS which it classes as deserts but are not, plus the Kazakh and Pontic steppes), `forests-jungles` (23: sets of countries by hand: Amazon basin, Congo basin, Borneo and Sumatra, boreal belt: Natural Earth has no forest layer), `lakes-rivers` (44: the 24 largest lakes and the 13 main rivers of Natural Earth 110 m crossed with the zone outlines), `megacities` (29: the cities of the base's `cities_major.json` of 5 million and more, **the city without its agglomeration** (Tokyo 9.7 M, Cairo 9.6 M), so 5 M is what gives the agglomerations of more than ten million; Kinshasa is given to `cd` by hand, the two capitals face to face on the river overlap in the simplified outlines). **Each list says its `basis`**; what is a desert or a jungle is the choice of that file, to be reviewed by the user once.
- **For Données**: the rows (a pack: its name, its size = the sum of the published sizes of its zones, « Ajouter tout » = `addZone()` on each id, then the ordinary download and build, a zone already added is said and never duplicated; a theme: a filter that ticks the zones) are Réglages'. Regenerate the file after a change of the zone list (`tools/build_aero_zones.py`) or of the base's cities.

### Airways by country: what is published, from which source, and who has none (SOG-0077, answer of 10 Nov 2026)

The question (the user): « a-t-on les AWY pour d'autres pays que la France et l'UK ? Si non, il faut trouver une source ». **Measured** on this workstation's `data/sia/base/<zone>/<cycle>/airways.json` (218 zones known, 45 built):

| zone | source | segments / routes | cycle of the folder | state |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fr` | SIA, AIXM 4.5 (`Rsg`) | 1 493 / 425 | 2026-10-29 (and 2026-10-01) | published, with the direction `dir` after the next build |
| `gb` | NATS, AIXM 5.1 (`EG_AIP_DS`) | 1 217 / 220 | **2026-10-01 only**: the 2026-11-26 folder has 0 (built without the NATS file declared « personnel ») | needs the AIP of 29 Oct taken from Downloads (SOG-0126) and a build with « personnel » |
| `es` | ENAIRE, AIXM 5.1 (`LE_…_AIP_DS_FULL_EnRoute.xml`) | **903 / 153** | **2026-10-01 only**: the 2026-10-29 folder has 0 | published once; the file is not on the workstation any more, so the current cycle has none |
| `ie` | AirNav Ireland, eAIP ENR 3.3 (geojson, `tools/airnav_eaip.py`) | 0 | — | the reader exists (K70, K71), no airways built yet |
| the other 41 built zones | — | 0 | — | **no airways source is read for them** |

So **yes, Spain** (903 segments, 153 airways, built on 1 Oct) as well as France and the UK; Ireland is readable but not built; **nowhere else**. Readers exist for four dated sources (SIA, NATS, ENAIRE, AirNav) and for X-Plane's `earth_awy.dat` (global, read by the page only: the X-Plane folder is a handle held by the page, and the launcher build refuses it). openAIP and the FAA files the suite reads carry no airways.

**Candidate sources for the countries that have none** (research of 9 Nov 2026; « opened » = fetched; what could not be opened is said unverified, never assumed):
- **United States**: the FAA **NASR 28-day subscription** (cycle 2026-10-29 opened: `AWY.zip`, `ATS.zip`, the CSV set with `AWY_BASE.csv` and `AWY_SEG_ALT.csv`, and `aixm5.1.zip` with the airways, under `nfdc.faa.gov/webContent/28DaySub/`; the zips answer, I did not unzip them). No licence wording on the page (US government data: public domain is an inference to confirm). A CSV reader is small. **Best candidate.**
- **Germany**: **DFS « AIP Datasets »** (`aip.dfs.de/datasets`), dataset **ED Routes** in **AIXM 5.1** (Route, RouteSegment, StandardLevelColumn), **CC BY 4.0** for DFS's own data (credit DFS; free; the terms PDF opened). The AIXM 5.1 reader that reads NATS and ENAIRE should read it with little change; the download path is in DFS's REST documentation (not decoded). **Second candidate.**
- **Norway**: Avinor announces an AIXM 5.1 service with ATS routes; today the eAIP HTML is open, the AIXM data is for subscribers (not live). **Italy** (ENAV, eAIP after a free registration; an AIXM 5.1.1 WFS is mentioned in a search result only) and **Turkey** (DHMI: AIXM 5.1 datasets mentioned in a third-party presentation): **unverified**.
- **eAIP HTML or PDF with the route pages (ENR 3) opened, no machine-readable file**: Portugal (HTML; copying and redistribution only by prior agreement with NAV Portugal), Thailand (HTML), Denmark (PDF, not read), Iceland, Canada (NAV CANADA AIP Part 2 ENR is a PDF; the ENR file of the page answered 404 today), Nepal, Jordan: a table reader per State (the suite reads the SIA eAIP pages for aerodromes only).
- **Unverified or nothing found**: Belgium, Netherlands, Sweden, Switzerland, Morocco, Algeria, Philippines (portals that blocked the fetch or did not resolve), Cyprus, Egypt, Lebanon, Tunisia, Israel, Indonesia, Fiji, Cambodia, Myanmar, Pakistan, Vietnam, India (no ENR 3 page located).
- **Consolidated or default datasets**: EUROCONTROL's route network data is free for R&D only, after an account and its terms (no redistribution); ICAO: nothing found; **X-Plane `earth_awy.dat`** is a global airway file (the page already reads it) but its redistribution terms for X-Plane 12 are unconfirmed (the legacy format specification speaks of a GPL freeware header derived from DAFIF, for X-Plane 10 and earlier): read the first line of the file for its source and copyright before publishing anything derived from it.

**Proposal** (to be decided by the ergonomics session and the user): (1) the **DFS** AIXM 5.1 « ED Routes » (CC BY, the reader exists) and the **FAA NASR** CSV (a small reader) as new sources of the catalogue, in that order: they cover Germany and the United States, the two largest airway networks after France and the UK; (2) rebuild `gb` and `es` for the current cycle (the NATS AIP of 29 Oct is in Downloads: SOG-0126; the ENAIRE file has to be downloaded again); (3) AirNav Ireland: build `ie` (the reader exists); (4) for the rest, the global X-Plane file as a **personal local** source only, until its terms are read.

### DFS « ED Routes »: the airways of Germany (SOG-0077, source of the catalogue `dfs`, 10 Nov 2026)

The first new source of airways after France, the UK and Spain (decision of the ergonomics session; the FAA NASR comes next, once its licence is confirmed in writing).
- **Source**: DFS « AIP Datasets » (`aip.dfs.de/datasets`, REST API v1.2), dataset **ED Routes** in AIXM 5.1 (Route, RouteSegment, StandardLevelColumn: the routes more than 50 % inside the German AOR and some others), **CC BY 4.0, credit DFS**, free. Catalogue id `dfs` (zone `de`, group `base`, `fetch.provider: 'dfs'`, built by the AIXM 5.1 reader), in the launcher's mirror (`base_sources.SOURCES['dfs']`, `EXTRA['de']`, folder `sia/zones/de`) and in `shared/aero_zones.js` (K52 compares the two).
- **Fetch** (`aero_fetch.dfs`, called by `base/fetch`, nothing to do by hand): reads the metadata `https://aip.dfs.de/datasets/rest/`, takes the **amendment in force (n° 0)** release `AIXM 5.1` of the leaf « ED Routes », then `…/rest/0/<file name>` (14.9 MB). The **SHA-512 of the metadata is checked**: a download that does not match writes nothing; a file name that is not `ED_Routes_<date>_<date>_<type>.xml` is refused. The file is written under a **stable name, `data/sia/zones/de/dfs_ED_Routes.xml`** (one file: the next amendment replaces it); when the SHA-512 of the file in place is the metadata's, **nothing is downloaded** (0.4 s). Measured on 9 Nov 2026: 0.9 s for the first fetch, then unchanged.
- **Reader** (`AeroSources.aixm5`, `shared/aero_sources.js`): this file has **no point** (no DesignatedPoint, no Navaid): each end of a segment is a reference with its name (`xlink:title`); the reader now names the end by that title and **places it at the end of the segment's curve** (`curveExtent`, `posList`, lat lon for EPSG:4326) when the point is not in the file (NATS and ENAIRE, which carry their points, are read as before). Measured on the real file: **1 797 segments, 399 routes**, every end named and placed, no segment over 500 NM; the direction comes from the availabilities (689 forward only, 578 backward only, 530 both ways) and the levels from `upperLimit` / `lowerLimit`.
- **Parser version 23** (`shared/sia_base.js` VERSION, `AeroCatalogue.PARSER_VERSION`): the builds before it are shown « à reconstruire » (all the zones are already, since the direction of the airways, parser 22): one « Mettre à jour » rebuilds them, `de` included, once it has the file (`base/fetch` on the zone `de`).
- **Not done**: the airspaces, the points and the navaids of Germany (other DFS datasets: `ED Airspaces`, `ED Waypoints`… are in the same metadata, not read); the credit « DFS, CC BY 4.0 » is in the catalogue's licence line, to be shown where the data are shown; no end-to-end build of `de` on a real browser was run here (the reader is checked on the real file, the build page uses the same reader).

**DFS lot 1: the points and the navaids** (SOG-0462 follow-up, 19 Nov 2026; case K113). `aero_fetch.dfs` now takes THREE datasets of the amendment in force: `ED Routes`, `ED Waypoints` and `ED Navaids` (`DFS_KINDS`), each release `AIXM 5.1` with the SHA-512 of the metadata, each under a stable name of `sia/zones/de/` (`dfs_ED_<Kind>.xml`); the file name given by the server must have the form `ED_<Kind>_<date>_<date>_<type>.xml` FOR ITS OWN SET (a name of another set, a path, a bad sum: refused, nothing is fetched under it). A set that fails is in `errors` and the others are still written; `ok` is true only when there is no error. `AeroSources.aixm5` reads both new files as it is (DesignatedPoint with its type, the VOR / NDB / DME / TACAN / ILS components of the navaids); the builder keeps the points of type `ICAO` (`sia_base.js`), which name 1 218 of the route ends; the 33 others are navaids. Measured on the real files: Waypoints 10.4 MB read in 227 ms, Navaids 4.5 MB in 141 ms (browser). Not read: `ED Airspace` (lot 2), `ED AirportHeliport`, `ED Runway`, `ED Procedure` (Q-0111, the user's answer is awaited). The catalogue label and `gives` of `dfs` (four languages) say the three sets; `content` is `points, navaids, airways` (JS and the Python mirror, K52).

**DFS lot 2: the airspaces** (19 Nov 2026; case K113). `aero_fetch.DFS_SETS` maps the four sets to their metadata leaf and file name; `Airspace` is the leaf « ED Airspace StrokedBorders » (`ED_Airspace_StrokedBorders_<date>_<date>_<type>.xml`, one stable file `dfs_ED_Airspace.xml`). `AeroSources.aixm5(text, { source: 'dfs' })` (the build passes the source of the file, `AeroZones.fileSource`) reads the spaces through `dfsType(type, localType)`: CTR, CTR_P → CTR; CLASS → TMA (with `cls`); R, OTHER:R_AMC → R; OTHER:D_AMC, D_OTHER → D; RAS + RMZ / TMZ / ATZ → the same; R and D are named `ED-R32A <name>`; `src` is `dfs`; the part carries `layered: true`. `AeroSources.merge` does not compare the spaces of a layered part with each other, only with those already in the base: the order of the sources (aixm5 before geojson before openaip) makes the DFS come first and openAIP the fallback. Left out and why: see the CHANGELOG. Parser version 30.

**DFS lot 3, first part: the aerodromes and the runways** (19 Nov 2026; case K363). `DFS_SETS` gains `AirportHeliport` and `Runway` (`dfs_ED_AirportHeliport.xml`, `dfs_ED_Runway.xml`, same checks). `AeroSources.aixm5(text, { source: 'dfs', shared })`: for the DFS an `AirportHeliport` is kept only when `dfsAerodrome` says so (type AD, HP or LS AND a four-sign `locationIndicatorICAO`; an LS is read as an AD); `shared` is one object per build and per source (`sia_base.js`, `dfsShared`) that holds the table of the aerodromes and the one of the runways from one file to the next: the files are read in the alphabetical order of their names, AirportHeliport before Runway. The runway file has no aerodrome of its own. 483 of the 1 576 features of the real file have an indicator. Not done: the ILS of the DFS (the Navaids file comes before Runway), the thresholds (TouchDownLiftOff), the declared distances.

**DFS lot 3, second part: the procedures** (19 Nov 2026; cases K367, K368). Verb `dfsproc/build {}` (`tools/regen/dfsproc.py`) → { ok, aerodromes, procedures, kept (aerodromes whose file of another source is kept), effective, counts { holds, fixless, unresolved, rf, missing, points } } or { ok: false, reason }, reason: `points` (the files « ED Waypoints » and « ED Navaids » that base/fetch takes are absent), `fetch`, `missing`, `too-big`, `doctype`, `unreadable`, `too-many`, `too-slow`, `nothing`. `aero_fetch.dfs_procedure(data)` takes the set (`DFS_PROC_LIMIT` = 120 MB, passed to `get(limit=…)`). `dfs_proc.read(path, names)` → ({ uuid: leg }, [procedure]) in one pass (`iterparse`, `hasMember` and the root cleared as they are read); `dfs_proc.build` → ({ ICAO: { procedures, runways, warnings } }, counts); `dfs_proc.write` writes `<out>/<date>/<ICAO>.json` atomically and merges the index. A transition of type RWY gives the record of its runway (a STAR: the common part first, then the runway part; a SID: the runway part first), a type EN_ROUTE a record with its name (`RWALL.<name>`), an approach: FINAL then MISSED (phase `missed`) and one record per APPROACH transition. Doubts: `unresolved` (a leg whose point is not given, or that a transition cites and the file lacks), `rf-arc` (an RF kept without its centre). The holds are not read.

**`base/fetch` and the procedures of Germany** (19 Nov 2026; case K369): at the end of `base_sources.fetch`, when `de` is among `zones` and the job is not cancelled, `_dfs_procedures(job, ctx)` runs the verb `dfsproc/build {}` with a quiet job (its steps are not the progress of the fetch) and `res['procedures'] = { 'de': … }` (absent otherwise). The journal `base/last` (`regen_jobs.Jobs`, `LAST_VERBS`) keeps the result trimmed as every result of the fetch. `base_state.update` copies it to `res['fetched']['procedures']`.

#### More reasons for `base/status` and a notice (SOG-0143, 10 Nov 2026)

Four codes join the reasons of `base/status`, and the answer gains `notices` per zone (information that does **not** make the zone stale):

| code | where | parameters | buildable | meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `index-orphan` | reasons (first) | `cycle`, `count` | yes | `sia/base/index.json` declares a cycle for the zone whose folder lacks files (`count`: files absent among `meta` and the 12 kinds): the app would say « aucune base installée »; rebuild |
| `source-unbuilt` | reasons | `source` | yes | the zone was built **without points or airways**, its dated source (ENAIRE, NATS, AirNav, DFS, SIA) has its file on the workstation and is readable (declared « personnel » when the licence asks): built before the file: rebuild |
| `source-unread` | reasons | `source`, `why: 'personal'` | **no** | the same, but the licence is personal use and the **« personnel » box does not declare it**: the file is there and was not read; declare it (`personal: ['enaire']`) |
| `source-missing` | reasons | `source` | **no** | the same, and **no file of that source on the workstation**: to download or drop |
| `personal-undeclared` | reasons (before `inputs-changed`) | `source` (one of `nats`, `enaire`, `airnav`, `lfv`) | **no** | (SOG-0226) the zone was built with a source under a personal licence that the **« personnel » box no longer declares**: the files of that source are not counted as `inputs-changed`; to declare the box again, a build without it would drop the source; K160 |
| `no-points` | **notices** | `source: 'openaip'` | — | the zone was built without points or airways **and none of its sources carries any** (openAIP gives airspaces, aerodromes, navaids and VFR points only): not stale, said so that an unknown point (NOSLI, MOKNI, ADVOP in Sweden, Denmark) is understood |

The four-language sentences (the page writes them with `K.t`):

| code | fr | en | de | es |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `index-orphan` | Fichier du cycle {cycle} déclaré mais absent ({count}) : reconstruire | File of cycle {cycle} declared but absent ({count}): rebuild | Datei des Zyklus {cycle} angegeben, aber nicht vorhanden ({count}): neu erstellen | Archivo del ciclo {cycle} declarado pero ausente ({count}): reconstruir |
| `source-unbuilt` | Construite sans points ni voies, le fichier {source} est là : reconstruire | Built without points or airways, the {source} file is there: rebuild | Ohne Punkte und Strecken erstellt, die {source}-Datei ist da: neu erstellen | Construida sin puntos ni aerovías, el archivo {source} está: reconstruir |
| `source-unread` | Construite sans points ni voies : le fichier {source} est là mais la case « personnel » ne le déclare pas | Built without points or airways: the {source} file is there but the “personal” box does not declare it | Ohne Punkte und Strecken erstellt: die {source}-Datei ist da, das Feld „persönlich“ nennt sie nicht | Construida sin puntos ni aerovías: el archivo {source} está pero la casilla «personal» no lo declara |
| `source-missing` | Construite sans points ni voies : le fichier {source} manque | Built without points or airways: the {source} file is missing | Ohne Punkte und Strecken erstellt: die {source}-Datei fehlt | Construida sin puntos ni aerovías: falta el archivo {source} |
| `no-points` (notice) | Zone sans points ni voies : la source ne les porte pas (openAIP) | Zone without points or airways: the source does not carry them (openAIP) | Zone ohne Punkte und Strecken: die Quelle enthält sie nicht (openAIP) | Zona sin puntos ni aerovías: la fuente no los incluye (openAIP) |

**The build never writes an index that points at an absent file**: `base.install` checks that every file of the zone's folder is on the disk just before it writes `sia/base/index.json`; otherwise it raises (`write-failed` for the zone, nothing published, the index untouched).

### Homonyms: `coords(ident, { near })` and `whereIs(ident, { near })` (SOG-0142, 10 Nov 2026)

A name of a point is not unique in the world (PAM: Pamplona and Panama City; ALS: Alès and a Moroccan beacon). The route `ESSB DCT NOSLI N850 MOKNI DCT ADVOP Z703 ALS N872 PAM …` went through the American PAM and the Moroccan ALS because `coords(ident)` took **the first place found**. With a neighbourhood it takes the nearest:

- **`coordsAll(ident, { near: [lat, lon], all? })`** → `[{ kind, item, zone, distanceNm }]`, **nearest first**. The zones are opened from the nearest (the frame of the zone) to the farthest and the search **stops when the next zone's frame is farther than the best place found** (no zone left can have a nearer one: the other side of the world is not opened). `all: true` searches every published zone (slower) to list **all** the homonyms. Without `near`, nothing changes (first place found, `[{ kind, item }]`).
- **`coords(ident, { near, all? })`** → `{ lat, lon, name, kind, zone, distanceNm, homonyms: [{ lat, lon, name, kind, zone, distanceNm }] }` (5 at most; **the homonyms set aside**; with the early stop only those of the zones that were opened, with `all: true` every one); `null` when the name is nowhere. A bad `near` is ignored (the old answer).
- **`whereIs(ident, { near, all? })`** → `{ found: <coords> }`, or `{ found: null, unknown: { reason, zone?, via?, position? } }`. A name that is **not among the aerodromes, beacons, points and VFR points of the published zones** is looked for as **the end of a published airway** (an airway names it and gives its position): `unknown.reason` is **`'zone-not-built'`** (the zone of the country where the point lies, `unknown.zone`, is not built: it is the zone to add), **`'no-points'`** (the zone is built but no source gives it points: openAIP carries none, SOG-0143) or **`'not-found'`** (no airway names it, no `zone`); `via` is the airway that names it and `position` its position **according to that airway**. Example on the route above, with the airways of Germany (DFS): NOSLI (Sweden: `se`), ADVOP (Denmark: `dk`) are known by their airways and said « zone se construite sans points », « zone dk non construite ».
- **For Carburant's route reader** (Features SOG): resolve each name with `near` = the previous resolved point (or the segment of the route); show the chosen place and, when `homonyms` is not empty, say it; for an unknown name show `unknown` as it is.

**The text of a failure** (SOG-0144, 10 Nov 2026): `data/local/build/<zone>.json` keeps, with `failed: { error, at }`, the **`message`** the build gave for the zone (for a failure of the page itself, the text the page sent: « AIXM4.5_all_FR_OM_2026-10-01.xml : HTTP 404 »), and the job's log says it (`page : …`); `base/status` gives it in the reason: `{ code: 'failed', error, message? }`. The page says « Dernière construction en échec : {message} » (fr), « Last build failed: {message} », « Letzter Build fehlgeschlagen: {message} », « La última construcción falló: {message} ».

**A refused request names its reason** (SOG-0144, 10 Nov 2026): the answer to a refused `launcher.job('regen/job', …)` (HTTP 400) is no longer « invalid arguments » for the verbs of the set `base`: `{ ok: false, status: 400, reason, message, …parameters }`, `message` in English, the page translates from `reason` and the parameters:

| `reason` | parameters | message |
|---|---|---|
| `too-many-zones` | `max` (60), `count` | `60 zones at most per request, 78 given` |
| `unknown-zone` | `zone` | `unknown zone: xx` |
| `duplicate-zone` | `zone` | `zone given twice: fr` |
| `no-zone`, `zones-missing`, `bad-zone` | — | no zone given / a list of zone identifiers is needed / identifiers are texts |
| `bad-source` | `source?` | `source not admitted: yy` (a `personal` source that is not an identifier of the catalogue; 10 at most) |
| `unknown-field` | `field` | `unknown field: path` |
| `bad-field` | `field` (`fetch`, `dry`, `what`) | `fetch: true or false`… |
| `not-an-object` | — | the arguments are an object |

(the verbs `base/build`, `status`, `update`, `fetch`, `purge`, `pickup`; the other sets keep « invalid arguments »). `base/build` and the others take **60 zones at most**: the page cuts a larger selection into slices of 60.

**`tools/.regen_last.json`** now has **`work`**: the last job that is not a simple reading (`base/status`, `route/compute` are read-only), next to `job` (the very last): a `base/status` run right after a build no longer erases the build, its result and its failure text.

**Where the build page reads the moved SIA files** (SOG-0144, decision of the ergonomics session, 10 Nov 2026): the raw SIA XML moved to `data/local/sources/sia/<cycle>/` stay **hidden to the rest of the world** (`data/local/` is never served: the rule of the audit of 7 Oct is untouched); the build page reads them through **`GET /__setup/regen/job/input?job=<id>&source=<source>&cycle=<cycle>&name=<name>`**, a route of the running job: header `X-SOG-Setup: 1` and **`X-SOG-Job-Token`: the token of its claim**; 403 without a valid token, 409 if the job is not the running one, **400 for any file the job did not offer** in its `sources` (the list of the offer is the only list; « .. », a path or a name outside `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*` are refused), 404 if the file is absent; the file is served **as a stream** (`regen_jobs.Jobs.input_file` gives the path, `launch.py` streams it). The page uses the route for the names of `sources` (moved files), and `/data/sia/<name>` as before for the others. Once the job is over the token opens nothing. Measured on the real launcher with a copy of the user's inputs: the 46 zones of the failed job build in 17 s (26 s with the « personnel » box declared).

### The time of a synchronisation: `timings` (SOG-0149, 10 Nov 2026)

The report of a synchronisation pass (`data_sync.run`, kept in `data/local/sync.json` and given as `last` by the sync status) carries **`timings: { steps: { settle, commit, prepare, receive, untrack, push } (ms each), totalMs, gitCalls, gitMs, sentBytes }`**: how long each step took, the total, **how many git commands the pass ran and the time they took**, and **the bytes the push carried** (compressed objects of what the remote branches do not have; `0` when nothing is sent, `null` when it was not counted: more than 20 000 objects). Only numbers: never a path nor a content. A pass that is refused before it starts (not a repository, a rebase in progress) has none. What was measured on the workstation on 9 Nov (read-only, DECISIONS 10 Nov): local git is negligible, an SSH handshake 1.1 s, the LFS pre-push hook 2.75 s even when nothing is sent, the LFS post-commit hook 0.2 s per commit, a day sends 0.3 to 8.3 MB; the two minutes of « the first synchronisation of the day » are not found by a cold measure: **the next one will say where they go**.

**RouteSim of 9 Nov 17 h 05** (ported as it is, same rule; `engine.py`, `fra.py`, `fra_recovery.py`, `recovery.py`, `rad_flight.py` and the new **`nav_data.py`**, which the engine imports): the engine now **reads the base through `nav_data`**: a zone whose newest dataset is **empty** falls back to the newest older non-empty snapshot (Spain at 29 Oct, built without its points and airways, is read from its 1 Oct folder), legacy supplements only when they declare the same AIRAC date. The plan: a route that has **data gaps** (`primary.data_gaps`: an airway absent from the loaded segments, a portion not covered) is **rejected** like an error (alternatives are searched, the fallback proposed), and the way a typed word is taken as a mandatory passage is the engine's (`route_connector`). **For the screen**: the shape does not change; **each alternative carries `incomplete: true`** when its control is incomplete for missing data (its reasons are in the `issues` as « revue »: show it as « contrôle incomplet », never as a checked route). Measured on the workstation: LFPO → EIDW 3.5 s, LFPG → LEMD 3.7 s (via Y129 · T430), LFPG → LFMN 3.2 s. `vfr.py`, `server.py` and the tests stay out.

**`base/pickup`: what is already in place, and what is there but not read** (SOG-0150, 10 Nov 2026). « Rien à prendre dans Téléchargements — Royaume-Uni (openAIP…): gb_apt.xml n'est pas lu » hid the real situation of gb: the three NATS zips **were already taken** (`taken` is empty then) and NATS, a personal-use source, was **not read because the « personnel » box does not declare it**. The answer now carries, next to `taken`: **`inPlace: [{ zone, source, sets: [AIP, OBS, UAS] }]`** (the sets already in place as they are: nothing written) and, when the page gives the box in the new argument **`personal: [sources]`** (the sources of the box; optional), **`unread: [{ zone, source: 'nats', why: 'personal', cycle, sets }]`**: the files are there (in place or taken now) and the box does not declare the source, so the build ignores them (the same state as `source-unread` of `base/status`, which gb's line shows). **Declared** (`personal: ['nats']`): nothing is unread, the next build reads them (`source-unbuilt`). Without `personal` the pickup does not guess. The page says: « {n} jeux NATS présents dans Téléchargements, déjà pris : la case « personnel » ne déclare pas NATS » (fr), « {n} NATS sets in place: the “personal” box does not declare NATS » (en), « {n} NATS-Datensätze vorhanden: das Feld „persönlich“ nennt NATS nicht » (de), « {n} conjuntos NATS presentes: la casilla «personal» no declara NATS » (es).

**Homonyms, the rule on kinds** (SOG-0154): `coordsAll` / `coords` with `near` count as **one place** the items of the same identifier **within 1 NM of each other** whatever their kind (a beacon and a fix of the same name at the same position; the SIA publishes the reference point of an aerodrome as a point of its ICAO code: « LFLL : 2 homonymes » was the aerodrome and its point), and **a four-letter identifier that is an aerodrome resolves to the aerodrome**, with no homonym of another kind; a homonym is **another place** (PAM of Pamplona and of Panama City).

**The cause of a failed publication** (SOG-0155): the report of `data_sync.run` (route `POST /__setup/sync`, kept in `data/local/sync.json`) carries, when `ok` is false, **`cause`** — `remote-refused` (the server's hook or protected branch: its text is in `message`), `push-rejected` (non-fast-forward), `lfs-hook` (Git LFS), `auth`, `network`, `conflict`, `busy` (a rebase or a lock), `timeout`, `launcher-error` (an exception of the launcher, said instead of a bare 500) or `other` —, **`step`** (where it stopped: `settle`, `commit`, `prepare`, `receive`, `untrack`, `push`) and **`message`** (the text of git, cleaned of addresses and paths, 800 signs). A success has none, and `sync.json` keeps the failure until the next pass. The frame's `exchange` keeps `message` and `code` of the report; **`cause` and `step` are to be passed on by `frame/store/frame_data.js`** for the Données panel to say « La publication a échoué : {cause} » (the frame's).

**The FRA points of EUROCONTROL as a local layer** (SOG-0153, 10 Nov 2026, decision of the user). When **no published zone** has an identifier, `AeroDb.coords` / `coordsAll` / `whereIs` answer from the EUROCONTROL FRA book of **this workstation**: `{ kind: 'point', zone: 'fra', local: true, source: 'eurocontrol-fra', fir, cycle }` (`coords` gives `local`, `source` and `fir` next to `lat`, `lon`, `name`, `kind`; with `near`, `distanceNm` too). A place of a published zone always wins; the layer is read only after the zones found nothing. **The page shows « point FRA EUROCONTROL (lecture locale) »** whenever `local` is true. How it is read: the read-only job **`route/fra-points`** (`{}` or `{ date }`) of the launcher reads `data/route/fra.json.gz` and answers `{ ok, cycle, count, _points: [[id, lat, lon, FIR]] }` for the cycle in force on the date (the next cycle is not used before its start; the most recent one that precedes the date, else the oldest, when none is in force); a book missing gives `{ ok: false, reason: 'no-fra' }`. The `_points` key is **for the page only**: `tools/.regen_last.json` never holds it, and the job does not replace the last job that wrote (`READ_ONLY`). The page keeps the points **in memory** (one job per page load; the job is not asked again within 30 s after a failure), never writes them, never exports them; a device of the local network (no launcher) and a page without a launcher have no such layer. **These points never enter `data/sia/base`** (the book is EUROCONTROL's, for the company's use and its private data repository: INTEGRATION « Consolidated or default datasets »). The route engine already holds the FRA points as navigation nodes (`route_engine/fra.py` adds them to the graph): its answers are unchanged by this layer.

**The LFV eAIP reader** (`tools/lfv_eaip.py`, SOG-0153 b, 10 Nov 2026). Input: the offline eAIP of Sweden that LFV publishes (`AIP_OFFLINE.zip`, 988 MB, 3 299 entries: 1 542 HTML pages, 1 309 PDF charts). Only three kinds of entry are opened, out of the archive, never unpacked: the cover page (`ES-Cover Page-en-GB.html`: « SWEDEN » and « Effective date : 29 OCT 2026 », which is the edition), `eAIP/ES-ENR 4.4-en-GB.html` (the designated points: identifier, `590422N 0171529E`, the routes and procedures that use it; 331 rows) and every `eAIP/ES-ENR 3.2 <route>-en-GB.html` (one page per RNAV route, 123 of them: the points in order with their position, and, after each, the upper and the lower limit of the segment that leaves it). Output: `lfv_se_enr_<date>.geojson`, **the shape of the Irish file** (`sog: 'point'` and `sog: 'airway'` features, read by `shared/aero_sources.js`): 345 points and 459 segments for the 29 Oct 2026 edition (NOSLI, MOKNI and the other points of N850, T31, Z703… with their limits; a beacon is named by the code between parentheses: OSS; a point on a FIR boundary by its name: MISBI). **Not read**: the direction of the segments (the eAIP gives the direction of the cruising levels, not a one-way: `dir` stays absent, which means unknown), the SID, STAR and approach waypoints (AD 2: charts in PDF only: AGOPA, ARKIP, IDRID are named on no HTML page of the archive), the FRA connecting routes of ENR 3.3 (220 rows: « ESSB SID final waypoint → FRA departure connecting point → flight plan »: names and route strings, no position; next for the route engine). Bounds: a page of 8 MB at most, 400 route pages, 60 000 features; an archive without the cover page or ENR 4.4, a cover without « SWEDEN » or the date, a route page that speaks of another route, a layout that gives no point or no segment: refused with a message. **Licence**: the AIP of Sweden is published by authority of the Swedish Transport Agency, produced by LFV (ES-GEN 0.1); the archive carries **no reuse condition** (no copyright section) and none was found on aro.lfv.se: the source is treated like AirNav Ireland, a **personal-use licence** (read only if the « personnel » box declares it; compiled data in the private data repository only) until aip@lfv.se answers. K122.

**Hours and remarks of an airspace** (SOG-0163, parser version 24). A record of `airspaces.json` may carry, **both optional and only when the source publishes something**, **`hours`** (the activity hours as published) and **`rem`** (the conditions and remarks of the AIP): arrays of strings, **as the AIP words them** (never reworded, never interpreted), at most 12 lines of 400 signs. SIA (AIXM 4.5): `hours` = the lines of `txtRmkWorkHr` (the « # » of the source separates lines), then one line per `<Timsh>` (« MON-FRI 0800-1700 UTC (01-10 → 31-03) »), at last the code `H24` / `HJ` / `HN` / `HO` when nothing else is said (`HX`, « activation by NOTAM or AUP », says nothing by itself); `rem` = the lines of `txtRmk` (administrator, « Activity known on… », AZBA, conditions). AIXM 5.1 (NATS, ENAIRE): `hours` = the notes of the airspace that bear on the time interval (`propertyName` timeInterval: « Activated by NOTAM. »), `rem` = its other notes. **Read, not yet carried** (a reader to write, for the next lot): openAIP (`hoursOfOperation.operatingHours` per day, `byNotam`, `onRequest`, `onDemand`, `specialAgreement`, `activity`, some `frequencies`), the FAA (`TIMESOFUSE` already in `info`), the LFV and Naviair eAIP (ENR 2.1 / 5.1 text). DFS (« ED Routes »): routes only, no airspace. **A zone built before has neither field: the parser version moves from 23 to 24 and `base/status` says `parser-old` until the zone is rebuilt**; a sheet that finds none says « horaires publiés : non relevés dans cette base ». K124.

**Route engine: a gap between two parts of the network** (SOG-0476; case K358). `Nav.bridge_gap(g, a, b, fl, steps)` → `(NM, from, to)` or None: breadth-first without altitude over the edges usable at the level (an edge whose `upper` is below `fl` is not, unless the steps are allowed; the NAT edges never), from the departure forward and from the arrival backward; if the arrival is not reachable, the shortest direct (900 NM at most, `NO_NETWORK_MAX_NM`) between a point of each set (a grid of one degree on the larger set; the boxes of the two sets first: farther than the bound, nothing is walked; only the border of each ring; a budget of `MAX_BRIDGE_VISITS` = 2 000 000) is added to the graph as an edge `{ route: 'DCT', connector: true, no_network: 'gap' }`. In the answer: `legs[].no_network === 'gap'` and a REVUE « la base n'a pas de liaison entre … et … » (the other value, `no_network: true`, is SOG-0474: no network near the aerodrome). `dct_limits.check` ignores both.

**Heliports as departure and arrival: the pattern of the code** (SOG-0466, lot 2; case K357). The pattern of an aerodrome code of a field or a request is `^(?:[A-Z0-9]{4}|[A-Z]{2}[0-9]{3})$` (four signs, or the LIST code of a heliport of the SIA: two letters and three digits). The route engine accepts it for `dep` and `arr` (`plan.validate`, `engine.assess`: three to four signs, or the list code; the runway fields keep four signs); `AeroDb.suggest(text)` proposes the heliports by code and by name, `AeroDb.coords(code)` and `AeroDb.aerodromes({ icao })` find them (the code is `icao || id` of the light index); `procedures`, `runways`, `chartsUrl`… keep their own pattern: a heliport has none. The common field of the aerodromes (Features SOG, SOG-0465) takes the same pattern.

**Heliports: the twins and the completion** (SOG-0466, lot 1, 18 Nov 2026; parser version 29; case K351). `AeroSources.merge`: an aerodrome of kind `HP` with no code (openAIP: `id` = name) that is the SAME as a coded one of the base — within 100 m, or within 300 m with the same normalised name — is not added: the coded one keeps its `id` (« LF418 »: a code of the list, not an OACI), gets the other name in `aka` (3 at most, 80 signs) and the source in `also` (`['openaip']`); `icao` stays empty. In the other order (openAIP read first) the coded one replaces the uncoded one and takes its name. Two heliports that fail the rule stay two (a roof and a ground pad). `aeroindex.json` rows carry every heliport with a list code (`[A-Z]{2}\d{3}`) under that code in the column `icao`; `aerodb.suggest` accepts it (five signs) and proposes it by code and by name; a heliport without any code is not in the index. Base of 29 Oct: 562 heliports → 340 (222 merged of the 287 openAIP ones). The cards of the heliports (VACH) wait for the answer to Q-0105: the codes of the cards are « Hxxx » for 208 of the 443 local extractions, the list of the SIA gives the correspondence.

**ENR 5.1 of the AIP of France: the nature of the danger** (SOG-0462, lot B, 18 Nov 2026; case K352, K353). New source `enr51` (zone `fr`, provider `enr51` of `tools/aero_fetch.py`; the verb `base/fetch` takes it like the others): ONE page per cycle, `https://www.sia.aviation-civile.gouv.fr/media/dvd/eAIP_<DD>_<MON>_<YYYY>/FRANCE/AIRAC-<YYYY>-<MM>-<DD>/html/eAIP/FR-ENR-5.1-fr-FR.html` (computed from the cycle in force, never taken from a page; ≤ 16 MB), parsed by `tools/sia_enr51.py` (two rows per zone: the identification and the usual name, then limits, hours, TYPE OF RESTRICTION, authority and conditions), written as `data/sia/zones/fr/enr51_fr_<cycle>.json` `{ schema: 1, zone: 'fr', cycle, source, zones: { 'LFR12': { name, hours, nat, auth } } }`. The builder (`sia_base.js`, file name `enr51_<zone>_<date>.json`) joins it to the airspaces of type R / D / P by the key and adds to the record: `nat` (the nature of the danger, as the AIP writes it, French then English), `auth` (authority and penetration conditions), `ed` (the cycle of the text); `hours` only if the AIXM gave none; `uname` only if the XML_SIA gave none. A field the table does not give is ABSENT. The window of a zone (BUGS SOG) shows `nat` under « Nature du danger » and `auth` under « Organisme et conditions de pénétration », with `src` and `ed` as the source; ALL these texts are external: escape them on display. The raw table is not kept in the base.

**Zones R / D / P: what a record carries for the sheet of a zone** (SOG-0462, lot A, 18 Nov 2026; parser version 29; case K350). Contract for the window of a zone (BUGS SOG reads it): `uname` (usual name as published, ≤ 80 signs, France: « MONT SAINT MICHEL » for R 12; shown beside the code: « R 12 — Mont-Saint-Michel »; it is NOT the nature of the danger), `admin` (the manager: France `admin` 514 of 874, FAA and NAV CANADA the controlling agency), `hours` and `rem` (arrays of ≤ 12 lines of ≤ 400 signs, as the source words them, never translated, never reworded: `hours` the activity hours, `rem` the conditions and remarks), `act` (the way it is activated: `activable`, `NOTAM`, `sur demande`; not a nature), `info` (kept for the map windows: contact, frequency or hours depending on the source; FAA and NAV CANADA hours are also in `hours`), `src` (`sia`, `dircam`, `aixm5`, `geojson` = FAA / NAV CANADA, `openaip` = community-maintained: say « openAIP, communautaire »), and the edition = the cycle of the zone (`meta.json`). `nat` (the nature of the danger) does NOT exist until the ENR 5.1 is read (lot B). **A field the source does not give is ABSENT: never a default, never « H24 » invented** (openAIP writes « 00:00-00:00 » on every day when nothing is published: not read as hours). Counts on the base of 29 Oct: hours / remarks / manager / usual name per source: FR 871 / 853 / 514 / 781; ES (ENAIRE) 0 / 167 / 0 / 0; US 393 / 0 / 381 / 0 (now `hours` = `info`); CA 151 / 0 / 151 / 0; the 56 other States (openAIP only) none before this lot.

**Route computation: the control by leg and by layer** (SOG-0161). Every result of the engine (`primary`, each `alternatives[i]`, each `recovery.proposals[i]` of `route/compute`) carries **`control`** and each leg carries **`control`**, **`layer`**, **`control_reason`**. `control` of a leg: `controlled` (a published procedure; an airway segment of the published network, levels compatible, no missing data; a DCT that the RAD's annex 3B DCT publishes available, without condition, at all hours), `incomplete` (a link between FRA points of the EUROCONTROL book: roles E / X / I and levels checked, volumes, NPZ and complex conditions not in the lists of points; an airway segment with a data gap), `fallback` (the DCT connector of a simulation when no SID / STAR was read for the aerodrome, or a DCT that the RAD does not publish: « non contrôlé »), `error` (the engine's check found a level, direction or RAD error on it). `layer`: `SID`, `LOW` (the segment's ceiling does not exceed FL245), `HIGH`, `FRA`, `STAR`, `APP`. The result's **`control`**: `{ controlled_pct, incomplete_pct, fallback_pct, error_pct, total_nm, sections: [{ layer, control, from, to, nm, legs: [first, last], reason }] }` (neighbouring legs of the same layer and class form a section). **The screen's form** (`action` compute / check, `route_engine/view.py`) gains, **optional** and nothing removed: on each alternative `control: { controlledPct, incompletePct, fallbackPct, errorPct, sections: [{ layer, control, from, to, nm, reason }] }`; on each segment `control`, `layer`, `controlReason`. A page that ignores them keeps working; `state` (`ok` / `error`) and `incomplete` are unchanged. **Terminal connectors**: with no SID / STAR read for an aerodrome, the engine joins it to up to six FRA gates that the book names for it (« D » / « A » with the aerodrome) beyond the 40 NM radius, when the climb to the floor of the area can reach them; the connector is a DCT of simulation, `fallback`.

**`AeroDb.suggest(text, { n = 7, lang = 1 })` — proposals for an aerodrome input, across every published zone** (SOG-0158, parser version 25). Answers a code, the start of a name or of **a word of the name** (« stockholm » finds « STOCKHOLM / BROMMA »: the town is in the name), or **a country** by its four names (`zones()[].names`), its endonym or its initials (`zones()[].aka`: « Sverige », « Deutschland », « usa », « uk »…), which gives its **main aerodromes, the longest hard runway first**. It reads only the light index of each published zone (`aeroindex.json`: 438 KB for the 71 zones, a tenth of `aerodromes.json`), once, kept in memory for five minutes; no zone is opened whole. Returns `[{ code, name, lat, lon, zone, country, hardLen, rank }]` (`rank`: 0 exact code, 1 start of a code, 2 country, 3 start of the name, 4 start of a word, 5 contains; `country` in the language `lang`, an index of `names`; at most `n`, 50 at the most). A zone that is not published gives nothing. **`aeroindex` gains the column `hardLen`** (the longest hard runway in metres, null when the source does not say: openAIP gives it, the SIA does not for the French aerodromes) and **`zones()` gains `aka`**; the parser version moves from 24 to 25. Not covered: **a town that is in no aerodrome's name** (the town list of each zone is 3.7 MB for all zones, not a light index: `find()` and the page's `cities()` keep serving them). K126.

**The LFV source wired** (SOG-0153 b). `lfv` is a **manual, personal-use** source of zone `se` (catalogue `lfv`, `shared/aero_zones.js`, `tools/regen/base_sources.py`, mirrored by `modules/data` `PERSONAL`): « Mettre à jour » takes **`AIP_OFFLINE*.zip`** from Downloads (« (1) » of a second download included) **by conversion**: the 988 MB archive is opened for its cover page, ENR 4.4 and the ENR 3.2 pages only (`tools/lfv_eaip.py`), never unpacked, and `sia/zones/se/lfv_se_enr_<edition>.geojson` (160 KB) is the only file written (the plan, `dry`, converts too and writes nothing; its `taken` entry adds `points`, `segments`, `routes`). The file is of zone `se`, source `lfv`, cycle of its edition (`aero_cycles`: `CYCLED`, `PERSONAL_CYCLED`); zone `se` is therefore **`source-missing` until the archive is taken** (it had only openAIP, with no point or airway). The licence line of the catalogue, in four languages: « AIP publié sous l'autorité de l'Agence suédoise des transports, produit par LFV ; aucune condition de réutilisation donnée (ES-GEN 0.1) : usage personnel, lu sur ce poste ». K127.

**The ground layer never fails in silence** (SOG-0046 / SOG-0135, refused at the acceptance of 10 Nov). **`AeroDb.groundStatus(icao)`** → `{ state: 'none' | 'reading' | 'ok' | 'failed', reason?, message?, at?, wait? }`: `failed` keeps the **reason** as the last try said it (`busy`: the launcher was doing something else or sending; `too-soon`: the 30 s between two readings; `failed`: Overpass busy or unreachable, with the server's text in `message`; `no-data`: OpenStreetMap has nothing on this aerodrome) and the **text** of the launcher; `none` is « nothing read, nothing tried ». The failure is **announced**: `on('change', { ground, groundFailed: true })` (success: `{ ground }` as before). It survives a reload: the launcher keeps the last result of each aerodrome in `data/local/ground_state.json` (never committed) and the read-only job **`ground/state`** gives it (`{ ok, aerodromes: { <ICAO>: { at, ok, reason?, message? } } }`; the page asks it once a minute at most, when asked for an aerodrome it knows nothing of). **The panels say it**: the Map's Ground panel and the line of the procedures, « Sol non lu : <raison> · Réessayer » (Réessayer = `fetchGround`), written by BUGS SOG and Features SOG. `ground/fetch` **retries** a busy or unreachable Overpass (HTTP 429, 502, 503, 504, a time-out, a refused connection) twice, after 8 s then 20 s, before saying the failure, and says each retry in its log. The read that follows the procedures (`fetchProcedures`) is still in the background and still does not change its answer; it is no longer lost.

**FIR and UIR outside France** (SOG-0169, parser version 26). The readers of **openAIP** (type 10 `FIR`, type 11 `UIR`) and **AIXM 5.1** (NATS, ENAIRE: type `FIR`, `UIR`) now keep them as airspaces of type `FIR` / `UIR` (before: only the SIA's, for France). **Their rings are simplified to 0.01°** (Douglas-Peucker, `simplifyRing` of `shared/aero_sources.js`; only these two types): on the 126 FIR of the user's openAIP exports (32 countries) 93 381 points become 14 626, about 230 KB in all, and the extent and area hold (case K130). They are **reference lines of the map, never volumes to cross**: the vertical profile ignores the `asFir` family (`modules/maps/profile_view.js`). A zone built before parser 26 has none: `base/status` says `parser-old` until it is rebuilt. The UIR of openAIP are absent from the user's exports (type 11: none); the FIR of France come from the SIA and from openAIP: the merge keeps one by `type|name` and by shape.

**FAA NASR: points and airways of the zone `us`** (SOG-0153 d, 10 Nov 2026, decision of the user: « FAA : free of use and redistribute »). The source `faa` (already: special use airspace, military routes) takes a third layer, **`nasr`**: `tools/aero_fetch.py` reads the cycle in force on the subscription page (`faa.gov/…/NASR_Subscription/`, « Current Subscription effective … »), downloads the three CSV sets of that cycle (`https://nfdc.faa.gov/webContent/28DaySub/extra/<DD_Mon_YYYY>_<FIX|AWY|NAV>_CSV.zip`, the addresses computed by `tools/faa_nasr.py`, never taken from the page) and writes **`sia/geojson/faa_nasr.geojson`** (the shape of the Irish and Swedish files: `sog: 'point'` and `sog: 'airway'` features; replaced only if it changes). Measured on the real cycle of 29 Oct 2026 (0.4 s): **10 936 points** (the points that an airway names plus every navaid) and **17 624 segments** (V 8 575, T 3 688, J 1 706, Q 1 592, colour airways), 4.9 MB of GeoJSON. The limits: the minimum en-route altitude is the lower limit, the maximum authorised altitude the upper one; a segment with none gets its family's (V, T and colour: 0 to 17 999 ft; J, Q: 18 000 to 60 000 ft), the route engine refusing a segment without limits. **Not read**: the direction (the NASR gives it airway by airway; `dir` stays absent), the procedures (`STARDP`, `DP` sets), the gap segments (`AWY_SEG_GAP_FLAG` Y, dropped). Public domain: no personal-use box. K131, K54.

**Naviair (Denmark, Greenland, the Faroe Islands): the reader is not written** (SOG-0153 c). The Preface of the Danish AIP (GEN 0.1, section 6) says that **no part may be reproduced without the written permission of the Danish CAA**, « all types of media » included; the AIP is **PDF only** and the link of the user's note is the AIRAC 06/24 edition. The text of the PDFs can be read with the standard library (glyph positions give the points, coordinates and limits of ENR 3.2), so the reader is a matter of days, but **the terms are the DFS's**: a draft of the request to Naviair and the Danish CAA is in `frame/aerodb/NAVIAIR_LICENCE_REQUEST.md` (to send by the owner).

**`aeroindex` carries the kind of the aerodrome, and AIXM 5.1 aerodromes their runway** (SOG-0158, Features SOG's three asks; parser version 27). (1) **`aeroindex` rows gain the column `type`** (after `hardLen`): `AD` (aerodrome), `AH`, `HP` (heliport), `MIL`; a missing or unknown type is `AD`. **`AeroDb.suggest` returns `type` and, at equal `rank`, puts the civil aerodromes (`AD`) before the heliports and the military bases**, each group by `hardLen` (a military base with the longest runway no longer comes first). (2) **A base built with parser 24, 25 or 26 is `parser-old` for `base/status`** (the parser version of the build is compared with the one of `shared/sia_base.js`): « Mettre à jour » rebuilds it once and its index then has `hardLen` and `type`; until then `suggest` falls back to the alphabetical order for a country (no runway length in the index). (3) **NATS and ENAIRE (AIXM 5.1) do give the runways**: 230 `Runway` features with `nominalLength` and `composition` in the NATS file of 29 Oct; the reader now links each runway to its aerodrome (`associatedAirportHeliport`) and gives the aerodrome `hardLen`, the nominal length in metres of its longest **hard** runway (a composition of grass, gravel, sand, earth, water, snow… counts for nothing; feet are converted). K132.

**Route computation: the NAT layer** (SOG-0263, 13 Nov 2026). `tools/route_engine/nat_layer.py`, called by `Nav.__init__` after the airways, adds the tracks of `data/sia/zones/nat/nat_tracks.json` (FAA NMS, written by the launcher) to the graph, **taken even when stale** (the user: « on essaie tout de même de les utiliser, on raffine après »). Each track is a chain of edges `route: 'NAT<id>'`, one way (the order of the message is the flow; both ways only if the message gives east AND west levels), levels = the lowest and the highest of its levels × 100 ft, `nat: { track, direction: 'west' | 'east' | 'both', from, to (ISO), stale, issued: 'JJ/MM' }`; `stale`: the track ended before the date of the flight. A named fix that the base knows SHARES its node (it already touches the airways and the FRA graph); several homonyms: the nearest to the track's coordinates; an unknown fix without coordinates is left out (the chain starts at the first placed point, said in `skipped`); a NAT coordinate (`5530/20`, `57/30`: north, west) is read by the NAT convention. The ENTRY and the EXIT that touch no high airway (ceiling ≥ 24 500 ft) are joined by DCT of simulation to the 3 nearest high-network nodes within 900 NM (the coast is far from the first oceanic fix, and the base has no Canadian points; the nearest is the cheapest) (entry: network → track; exit: track → network), edges marked `nat_link: 'entry' | 'exit'`. **The file is an input read, not trusted**: at most 2 MB, 60 tracks of 40 points, an identifier `[A-Z]{1,2}`, a fix `[A-Z][A-Z0-9]{1,6}`, finite coordinates inside the globe, integer levels 100–600, ISO dates; what is outside is left out and said (`nav.nat.skipped`), a file that cannot be read is no file, and a failing layer never stops the calculation. **What the answer carries** (`view.py`): on each segment of a track `nat: { track, direction, from, to, stale, issued }` (and `link: 'entry' | 'exit'` on a DCT join); on each alternative that flies one `nat: { tracks: ['A'], issued: '13/11', stale: true | false }`; an issue `{ level: 'review', kind: 'NAT', text: 'Routes NAT du 13/11, périmées : NATA. Prises quand même… autorisation océanique non vérifiée.' }` (a review, never an error). **Added the same day** (ergonomics session): a flight that crosses the Atlantic (departure west of 35°W, arrival east of 15°W, or the reverse) when NO track of the message goes its way says it plainly — an issue « Aucune route NAT vers l'est dans le message du JJ/MM. » on a route that was found, and the same sentence appended to the error of a plan that failed; and in the control by leg (SOG-0161) a NAT track leg is `incomplete` (« track NAT du jour[, périmée] : autorisation océanique non vérifiée »: not a published airway segment) and a DCT join `fallback`, never `controlled`. Not done: the message in force at the time of the flight is not read (only the file of the day), and the tracks are not rated by their levels against the requested FL beyond the edge band. Case K237.

**Route computation: the context is kept** (SOG-0296, 13 Nov 2026). `plan.context` keeps the loaded base, the initialised engine and the RAD (`Nav`, `Rad`) from one calculation to the next, under the key (root, data folder, RAD, FRA, date of the flight), as long as the **fingerprint** of the files it read (path, modification time and size of `sia/base`, `proc` without `ground`, `sia/zones/nat`, `rad.json.gz` and `fra.json.gz`; ≤ 200 000 files, 11 ms on the workstation) does not change: a rebuilt base, a procedure read again, an imported book or a new NAT message gives another fingerprint and the context is rebuilt before the calculation. Bounds: `MAX_CONTEXTS` = 2 contexts (one per flight date and file set, the least recent leaves), `IDLE_S` = 3 600 s without use; `plan.drop_contexts()` empties them; `plan.CONTEXT_STATS` counts built / reused. Safe because the calculations run one at a time (`SOLVER_LOCK`); the engine's own graph caches (one routing graph, one FRA graph, bounded by the engine) stay too, their key holding everything the graph depends on. Measured on the workstation (LFPO → EIDW FL350, today's data): cold 2.4 s, the same pair warm 0.35 s, another pair warm 1.4 s. Case K238.

**VFR circuits** (SOG-0253, 13 Nov 2026): `vfr/circuits {}` (read only) reads `data/local/upload/vfr_circuits.xml` — the XML `VfrCircuits` of the user's extractor, deposited by the page (`tools/base_upload.py` recognises it by its root, source `vfr_circuits`, kept under that name, never committed or published) — with `tools/vfr_circuits.py` (bounds, no entity, finite numbers; format in `tools/report-vfr/FORMAT-DONNEES.md`, « VfrCircuits ») and returns the circuits per aerodrome with their hand, deduced from the order of the points, and their state against the compilation (`current`, `unchecked`, `obsolete`). Case K244.

**Rank, rhythm and content of the sources** (SOG-0369, 16 Nov 2026; case K280). Survey for France: SIA gives the spaces, aerodromes, navaids, points, VFR points, airways and obstacles; DIRCAM the refuelling tracks ALONE; openAIP the aerodromes, spaces, navaids and VFR points as a second source; X-Plane and MORA are local. « DIRCAM pas à jour » was `base/cycle` comparing the date of the FILE taken with the start of the AIRAC cycle in force (DIRCAM has no cycle, and its publisher no longer answers), and the red of openAIP the same comparison. **Now** three facts per source, in `shared/aero_zones.js` and its mirror `tools/regen/base_sources.py` (K52 compares them; `frame/aerodb/catalogue.js` carries the `rhythm` of the sources it shares, K280 compares): `rank` (`primary`: the official publisher | `secondary`: community or derived), `rhythm` (`airac`: a file per 28-day cycle | `editions`: published by editions, never late for the cycle alone | `daily`: the NAT tracks | `none`: no known rhythm) and `content` (the vocabulary `CONTENTS`: airspace, aerodromes, navaids, points, vfr_points, airways, obstacles, aar_tracks, mil_routes, nat_tracks, mora). `base/cycle`: a source with `rhythm: 'none'` is never `late` (its line is `done`, and `steps.download.ageDays` says how old the newest file is); the other rules are unchanged (the held cycle for a dated source, the file date for the others). With `zone`, `zoneView.contents` = the state PER CONTENT (`contents_of`): from the PRIMARY sources of the zone (`done` | `late` | `waiting` | `unavailable` | `missing`), the secondary sources taken listed apart (`secondary`) and never making a content `done`, `secondary-only` when no primary gives it. Not done: the screen (Réglages) does not read the new fields yet; the FAA layers, DFS, Canada and the NAT tracks keep the old rule where the file date is compared with the cycle (`airac` / `editions` / `daily` are declared, only `none` changes the state).

**UK SIDs read in part from the eAIP package of the user** (SOG-0374, 16 Nov 2026; case K282). Survey: the UK package (NATS, « AIRAC 11-2026 », 2 766 files, 881 MB, no AIXM; 116 aerodrome pages `html/eAIP/EG-AD-2.<ICAO>-en-GB.html`, 2 093 charts `graphics/<number>.pdf`) carries for EGLL six SID charts (« AD 2.EGLL-6-1 … 6-6 », « STANDARD DEPARTURE CHART - INSTRUMENT (SID) … (RNAV SUBSTITUTION ONLY) ») that are DRAWN but whose PDF has a text layer (~3 400 signs of scattered labels, no table of legs); no SID coding table for EGLL (ten other aerodromes have « STANDARD INSTRUMENT DEPARTURE CODING TABLES »: a later extension of `uk-star`). **Decided** (ergonomics session): read the SID charts by their text layer and say it; the package is recognised by its content; only the useful PDFs are copied into `data/local/upload/` (the allowed root of `pdf_guard` is NOT widened); personal-use box, never published; diff reviewed by Sécurisation before the push. **Done**: `tools/uk_package.py` (the package found by its FORM — `html/eAIP/EG-AD-2.*-en-GB.html` and `graphics/` — under three levels of folders of the launcher's download folders whose name evokes an AIP, names only; the chart index of an aerodrome — the title is the row BEFORE the link row; the SID charts; the copy under canonical names `ukpkg_<ICAO>_<part>-<number>.pdf`, ≤ 4 MB, `%PDF-` checked, ≤ 12 charts), `tools/proc_uk_sid.py` (from the title of the chart the SIDs — the NAME is the exit point, `J K F G` = 09R 09L 27R 27L at Heathrow, checked on the text — and from the text layer, per SID, the runway (the last `RWY` before the paragraph that ends « Straight ahead to MODMI 1J » / « to intercept MAXIT 1F » / « … to be established on UMLAT 1F »), the level at the exit (« 6000 at MODMI »: a minimum) and the position (the coordinate printed next to the name); one point, status `check`, `partial: { read, unread: [path], note }`), the verb `proc/ukpkg-fetch` of `tools/regen/proc.py` (source `ukpkg`, aerodromes `EG…`, `merge_other`: the file of the aerodrome keeps its STARs and approaches read in EAD Basic, the SIDs are added by identifier, a second reading replaces the first of the same source), `proc_manual.install` counts `partial` in the index entry, `aerodb.fetchProcedures(icao, { source: 'ukpkg' })` (reason `no-package` when no package is found). Real package, six charts: 24 SIDs read (exit point, runway and position for all; the level at the exit for MODMI, MAXIT and ULTIB: the other charts phrase it differently, « to 6000 8 at D CPT »: unread, said). **Engine**: a SID with `partial` needs one point (`procedures.prepare`), counts as `SID_partial` and not `SID_usable` (the connector stays free for the check), is ADDED to the search graph without replacing the DCT connector of the aerodrome (`used` does not count it: with only partial SIDs the search found nothing within its bound: 24 SID exits and no route from them), its legs carry `procedure_partial`, the control classifies them `incomplete` (« SID lue en partie : point de sortie et niveaux ; tracé non lu, voir la carte ») and a review says so.
