EGLL → KJFK on the real base: the route is unchanged (2.6 s): the free connector is still the shorter way. **Security review** (Sécurisation, 16 Nov, accepted after three requests): (1) the NAME of the user's folder goes nowhere (not the journal, not the answer, not `source.ref` of `proc/manual/<ICAO>.json`, which is committed and synchronised): `ref` is « ukpkg »; the `cycle` of the SIDs is the date read in the folder name (`uk_package.package_date`: a real date from 2000 to today), else UNKNOWN (`1970-01-01` with `cycle_unknown: true` on the source: the reader serves them « outdated »; never the date of the day), and the most recent package is chosen by that date, not by the sort of the names; (2) the verb is refused with the reason `not-personal` unless the personal-use box of the LAUNCHER (`data/local/personal_use.json`, SOG-0238) is ticked, as `proc/lnm` (NATS is `personal: 'licence'`: these points would reach `proc/manual/` and the commit of `data/`, so the group); (3) the PDF library is checked (`pdf_guard.require`) BEFORE `copy_charts` puts anything in `data/local/upload/`; (4) `proc_manual.merge`: a procedure read IN PART (`partial`) never replaces a complete one of the same identifier, whatever its source, and a document that brings only partial procedures leaves the header of the file (`formats`, `notice`, `cycle`, unread pages) as it was. Notes: `NAME_HINT` is the WHOLE word « uk »; a TOTAL of 3 000 entries walked per search (`MAX_TOTAL`); links AND Windows junctions are not followed (`uk_package._link`: `os.path.isjunction` from Python 3.12, this workstation has 3.14.7; before, the real path is compared with the given one); the chart rows are cut by a linear scan (no backtracking expression on 8 MB). Not done: the departure coding tables of the ten other aerodromes (extend `uk-star`), the arrivals and approaches of EGLL by the text layer, a button on the Fuel / Data screen (the page calls `fetchProcedures` with `source: 'ukpkg'`), the user manuals (no button yet); `pdf_guard` asks pypdf ≥ 6.21.4 (6.21.3 is installed on this workstation: `pdf/status` says the command). **Cycle** (lot A, case K343): the cycle of the SIDs is the effective date read in the aerodrome page, the `EM.effectiveDateStart` meta tag of its header (`uk_package.page_date`: strict ISO form, a real date up to today); the folder name (`package_date`) is the fallback, and a package with neither is read with the unknown cycle. **Online charts** (lot B, case K344; Q-0106, the user's choice: the online NATS chart, as for the SIA): with a known cycle the aerodrome file carries `charts { base, items }` (the NATS online base composed from the cycle, every chart of the page by its digits and kind) and each SID its `source.chart`; `chartUrl`, `chartsUrl` and `chartOnly` compose the « Carte » address from that base (NATS host whitelisted, `NATS_EAIP_BASE`) and a listed path; the links on the Carburant lines are lot C.

**Route check: no variants, round trip, positions** (SOG-0371 follow-up and SOG-0372 c, 16 Nov 2026; case K281). Features SOG found that the user's « SOG route check EGLL → KJFK · launcher 39685 ms » is the CHECK (« Contrôler »: one request, the text of the field), not « Calculer A → B »: the route taken carried « NATG » and « PARCH4 », the engine refused it then searched variants of its own (`automatic_alternatives = rejected`, the 25 s of a refused plan). Measured at 19c95941f on the route of the capture (real base, NAT of the day, cold): **24 s**, refused. **Decided** (ergonomics session) and done: (a) in CHECK (`mode: validate`, no `alternatives` asked) the plan gives no variants and no fallback proposals (`check_only` in `plan.compute`): the answer says what is refused, 1.1 s on the same route; with `alternatives: true` the search is still there; (b) ROUND TRIP: a route the engine wrote is read back without refusal — the check takes `level_strategy: adapt` by default like the search (before, `natural`: « N14 BIBPE–OKTAD : profil minimum 14550 ft sous le plancher 15500 ft »), and its DCT connector from an aerodrome with no SID read (to one with no STAR read) is free as in the search (`free_departure` / `free_arrival` of `flight_profile`, only when the catalogue holds no usable SID / STAR for the aerodrome: a typed « LFPG DCT … » keeps its rigour); the names of the NAT track (« NATE ») and of the procedure (« PARCH4 ») are read by the same layers as in the search and a name the file of the day no longer holds (the capture's « NATG »: the file read at 08:29 has A–E and S–Z) is still « point inconnu »; (c) each itinerary of the answer carries `points`: [{ id, lat, lon }] in order, without duplicates, at most 600 (`view.points_of`), for « Prendre » (Features SOG reads them: NICSO, added by the NAT layer, is in no zone the browser reads). Side effect: the first alternative search of a plan with alternatives (FL as asked, adapted levels) is now the primary itself on a base with no SID, so it is no longer listed twice (K267's baseline: four searches, three alternatives).

**Route computation: the hard bounds of every phase** (SOG-0371, 16 Nov 2026; the user: « 40" sur le lanceur pour un calcul nats »). Measured on the real base with the NAT of the day (EGLL → KJFK FL370, cold, cache off): the head route is COMPUTED and accepted in 2.5 to 3.0 s (the context 1.0 s, the NAT layer 0.2 s, the search 1.7 s; 34 % controlled, 53 % incomplete — the ocean is « autorisation non vérifiée » —, 13 % fallback); the rest was the ALTERNATIVES that the Fuel screen asks (`alternatives: true`): 28.5 s with a budget of 25 s, because the searches under way finished 3 to 5 s past it; 11.6 s with the budget of 8 s of 14 Nov (a launcher that was not restarted still ran the old code); the 1.6 s of 13 Nov were measured WITHOUT alternatives. **Now**: `engine.DEADLINE` (`time.perf_counter`, set by `plan()` for each phase and reset at the end) bounds EVERY search, the one under way too (`Nav.search` stops at `min(its own 6 s, DEADLINE)`; `Nav.generate` does not build a graph — 1 to 3 s — for a search that would have no time: « Budget de recherche atteint »; the proposals of recovery stop refining their anchors past it). The phases: the head route and its RAD retries **`HEAD_BUDGET_S` = 5 s** after the start of the calculation (the loading of the context included); the alternatives **`ALT_BUDGET_S` = 8 s** (`alternatives_budget`) from the end of the head route; the proposals of a refused head route the whole **`TOTAL_BUDGET_S` = 15 s**; what did not finish is said (`status: 'proposal'`, the sentence of the previous change). Measured after: EGLL → KJFK 11.8 s with the alternatives and 2.5 s without; EIDW → KBOS (head route refused by its budget) 14 to 15 s as a proposition. A head route that needs more than 5 s cold (ESSB → LEMO took 8 s) now comes back as a proposition: the price of the bound. Case K278.

**Iceland (Avians): the real addresses, one page per route, the hand fallback** (SOG-0368, 16 Nov 2026; replaces the profile of SOG-0240). The profile `is` had GUESSED addresses (`/html/eAIP/`, `AAAA-MM-JJ`, `BI-ENR-4.4-en-GB.html`) and a title check « AIP for ICELAND » that no page of the site has; the real forms, read on the site: the editions page (https://eaip.avians.is/) links each edition by an ABSOLUTE address on either host (`eaip.isavia.is`, `eaip.avians.is`) with the date in the segment as **AAAA_MM_JJ** (`A_10-2026_2026_10_29`); the pages are under `<edition>/eAIP/`, the summary is `menu.html` (5 MB, ~6 800 links, two languages); the names carry **spaces** (`BI-ENR 4.4-en-GB.html`, `%20` in the address); ENR 3.1, 3.3 and 3.4 are NIL; **ENR 3.2 is ONE PAGE PER ROUTE** (`BI-ENR 3.2 UT587-en-GB.html`, 31 pages); the pages have **no `<title>`** and their tables have none of the Portuguese `Table-row-type` classes (ENR 4.4: one row `<tr id="ABETUENR44">` per point; a route page: for each point a point row — a nested table with « ∆ NAME 665803N 0505630W », a beacon as « NAME NDB 'SF' » — then a leg row: tracks, distance « 30.5 NM », limits « FL 285 FL 195 », upper first). `tools/avians_eaip.py` reads them (and recognises a page by its CONTENT), `tools/avians_site.py` takes them, `aero_fetch.avians` writes `zones/is/avians_is_enr_<AAAAMMJJ>.geojson`. **Rules**: the edition is READ on the editions page (the most recent not after today; the base is its segment under `https://eaip.avians.is/…/eAIP/`), never built; the list of route pages is READ in `menu.html` (strict name `BI-ENR 3.2 <route>-en-GB.html`, ≤ 100 pages: more is refused); ONE request at a time, 0.5 s pause (`aero_fetch._pause`), the launcher's agent, a size bound per request (`get(url, limit=…)`: 1 MB the editions page, 16 MB the summary, 4 MB a page), **stop at the first refusal of the host** (429, 403, 5xx, timeout: never worked around); a page that is absent (404 / 410) is said with its address and the others are kept; the count is written — `pages: { read, of, missing? }` in the file and in the entry (`stopped` when the host refused); a COMPLETE edition already there is not read again (one request), an incomplete one is read again in full. **The hand fallback** (the rule for every source « taken alone by the launcher »): when the taking fails and writes nothing, `aero_fetch.avians` reads the pages SAVED BY HAND in `data/local/upload/` (this workstation only, never published): `base_upload.recognise` takes an `.html` of ≤ 4 MB (a byte-order mark allowed) by its CONTENT — the ENR 4.4 or a route page — and stores it under `avians_is_enr_4.4.html` / `avians_is_enr_3.2_<route>.html`; the edition of such a file set is the DATE OF THE FILES (`datedBy: 'file'`: the pages carry no date), `from: 'pages déposées'`; the catalogue (`avians`) gives the steps and `pick` for « Choisir le fichier… ». The personal-use box stays required (the licence of Avians is not read). **Tried on a FAKE network that serves the real forms of the addresses with invented points** (case K243); the four real pages (editions, summary, ENR 4.4, UT587) were opened in the browser pane to check the forms; the launcher itself was NOT run against the real network by the session: the first « Mettre à jour » of the user is the real test. **What is written** (Sécurisation, 16 Nov 2026; case K279): a deposit by hand has NO edition (the pages do not say theirs; its date is the date of the DEPOSIT, capped at today; `datedBy: 'file'`, `deposited`), is written as `avians_is_enr_dep_AAAAMMJJ.geojson` — not an edition name, so `aero_cycles` reads no cycle and the source is never « à jour » — and never replaces a file taken from the site (said; the pages stay in `data/local/upload/`); a taking cut by a refusal in the middle (`read` < `of`) is written only when nothing better is there (the same edition more complete, or another file, stays) and the previous editions are removed only by a COMPLETE taking, never one more recent than it; the digits of a designator, an edition segment or a deposit name are 0-9 (`re.ASCII`); a deposit holds 100 routes at most, as the summary; the `have` file is read for its page count under 16 MiB. The zone holds ONE file of this source (the builder reads every file of the folder). **The card of a failed taking** (Réglages' request, 16 Nov 2026): a failed source carries `message` (the sentence with the address tried), `pages` { read, of, missing? } and `stopped` in `zones[zone].sources[source]` AND in `failed[]` (`aero_fetch.Failure` / `avians_site.Said`: a sentence that carries them; `classify` reads them by attribute); the view also keeps `pages` and `stopped` of a taking that succeeded in part. `message` and `stopped` go through `base_sources.said`: `clean_error`, THEN the control characters (Cc: a tab or a line break becomes a space) and the format marks (Cf: U+202E and the other bidirectional marks, U+200B…) are removed, THEN 240 characters at most (Sécurisation: a third-party host must not invert or hide a part of the screen). `builtCycle` of a source published by EDITIONS (NAV Portugal): `aero_cycles.report` raises the cycle of an edition held to the cycle IN FORCE (« une édition d'État en vigueur n'est pas un cycle passé »): « cycle 2611 » there means « the edition taken is in force at cycle 2611 », not that the data is of 29 Oct (its date is `edition` / the file name, 1 Oct for the edition of 2026). **In the catalogue and the cycle list** (16 Nov 2026, case K283): the field `hand` of each source (`shared/aero_zones.js`, mirrored by `tools/regen/base_sources.HANDS`, compared by K52): `only` = the launcher tries nothing, the file is taken by hand (sia, nats, lfv, dircam and NAV Portugal — SOG-0318: its disk is taken by hand, its `kind` being `download` for the launcher's own attempts at the site); `fallback` = taken alone by the launcher, the hand deposit is only a fallback after a failure (avians, ukmil, openaip, faa); `none` = nothing by hand (faaclass, enaire, airnav, dfs, navcan, nat, xplane, mora). `base/cycle` renders it on each line as `handMode` (the key `hand` of a line already holds the pick-up detail `{ link, expected, inDownloads }`), next to `regime`. A first decision (10:00, `fetch.pick.fallback` in the catalogue of `frame/aerodb`) was REPLACED at 10:02 by this field and removed. `base/cycle`: `steps.download.deposited` (the date of the deposit) and `datedBy: 'file'` for a hand deposit of Iceland, derived from the NAME of the zone file (`base_cycle.DEPOSIT_FILE`), with no memory to keep: the entry of `base/fetch` alone lost them at a reload. **Amended** (audit 217): a file from the site whose `pages` count says `read` < `of` does not block a COMPLETE deposit: the deposit replaces it (the zone still holds ONE file, so the builder reads the deposit only); a COMPLETE file from the site still blocks it.

**Iceland: the summary of the real site (apostrophes)** (SOG-0368, refused by the user on 16 Nov 2026). I had written the expressions from the PAGE as displayed, not from its source: the real `menu.html` writes `href='BI-ENR 3.2 UT587-en-GB.html#ENR-3.2-UT587'`. `_ROUTE_HREF` (route pages), `_ANY_HREF` (the editions page) and `_TABLE_CLASS` (recognition of a saved route page by the class of its table) accept both quotes, the closing one being the opening one; the other expressions of the module read parsed text (HTMLParser) or file names, not raw HTML. Not read by this change, same supposition possible: the ENAIRE list of datasets in `tools/aero_fetch.py` (`href="contenido_Datasets/…"`), to be checked on its real source before a next taking. A deposited PDF of the Avians eAIP is refused with a sentence that says what to deposit (the page). Catalogue: `verified: true` for the Iceland path. Case K243.

**A slow or held read of a zone: `readStatus()` and the `change` of the arrival** (SOG-0349, 14 Nov 2026; contract of V3 Frame: a file read is shared, patient and in order). Before, the bound of `readWait` (10 s) called `reader.abandon(path)` then answered `null`: a large file (the 14.5 MB of the airspaces of a zone) on a mobile network was cut at 10 s and restarted from zero at every question, never arriving; a read refused by the host's limit looked like a missing file (zero data on the Map). **Now**: the bound only stops the WAITING. The read CONTINUES (one per path: the next questions attach to it; `abandon` is no longer called by the bound), the part of the zone (and the `meta` of a zone) is **pending** — an empty answer for that question, never `missing` — and on arrival the part is kept, its indexes redone and `change` announced (`{ zone, cycle, part }`): the Map repaints. A read the frame HOLDS (`detail: true` answers `{ unreadable, why: 'limit' | 'queued' | 'offline' | 'error', retryIn }` after 1.5 s) is reported at once, without waiting for the bound. **`AeroDb.readStatus()`** (sync, reads nothing): `{ pending: n, unreadable: n, entries: [{ zone, kind, state: 'reading' | 'queued' | 'limit' | 'offline' | 'error', retryIn (s or null) }], gen }`; `state()` is `loading` (with `pending`) while a part is pending. **What the Map does** (BUGS SOG): an answer given while `pending` > 0 is PARTIAL — it keeps what it shows (a pending or unreadable part never replaces it), says « n zones n'ont pas pu être lues : nouvelle tentative dans … » (with `unreadable` and the smallest `retryIn`) and repaints on `change`; ticking and unticking a layer asks aerodb again and restarts nothing (parts and zones are kept; one read per path). The other bounded reads (procedures, VFR, cities, aircraft…) keep their `null` at the bound, without `abandon`. The cleaning of the manifest's cells stops after 181 valid rows and 2 000 entries (review note of Sécurisation). Case K275.

**The build says a published file that is getting too big for the frame** (SOG-0358, audit 214). The frame caps every file read of the repository (`frame/store/transport_git.js` `MAX_READ`). `base/build` reads that value WHERE THE FRAME DECLARES IT (`frame_read_cap()` parses `const MAX_READ = <product of numbers>;`; never copied, never guessed: unreadable gives no cap, no alert, and a line in the log that says so) and, for every zone it writes, reports each file above HALF of the cap (`size_report`): in the log « zone us, espaces : 14,5 Mo — plus de la moitié du plafond de lecture du cadre (32 Mo) ; à découper », on the zone (`zones[zone].big`) and in the result: `big: [{ file, bytes, cap, message }]`, `readCap`. `ok` is not changed (a big file is a warning, not a failure). Not covered: the page's own publication (`SiaBase.publish`, the V2 path). No test reads `data/`: K276 uses made-up sizes.

**Route computation: low airways and fallback proposals** (rests of SOG-0161, 14 Nov 2026). (1) **A LOW airway is not taken away from the climb and the descent.** On the real base LFOE → KTPA at FL370 left the high network at JFK, went down to 17 500 ft and followed V and T airways for 700 NM (cruise FL364 until NM 3 311, then 17 500 ft on V44, V139, T307, T303, V1, T211). In `Nav.search` an airway edge whose ceiling is more than 1 000 ft under the requested level costs ×3 (`LOW_PENALTY`) unless its start is within the climb distance of the departure or its end within the descent distance of the arrival (the distance each needs from the level, the rate and the speed of the request × 1.3: `LOW_ALLOW`); procedures, connectors, DCT and FRA edges are not concerned; **never forbidden** (a low link can be the only one; the search stays admissible: a cost only grows). Real base after: LFOE → KTPA 34 NM of low airway (the arrival), 4 274 NM against 4 256. Case K273. (2) **A fallback proposal says what it is.** The screen's answer gains, **optional, nothing removed**, `status`: `computed` (a route computed and accepted) | `proposal` (no route: fallback proposals, partial routes to examine, each `proposal: true, incomplete: true`) | `none`; when `proposal`, `message` says « Aucune route calculée : <the reason>. Propositions de repli à examiner : routes partielles, une part n'est pas contrôlée. » (`ok` stays true: the answer is valid). A head route refused because its SEARCH reached its budget (« Budget de recherche atteint (6 s / 100 000 états) ») no longer searches the other levels (each reached the same budget, ~13 s): the failures say « Alternatives non cherchées… » and the proposals come at once — EIDW → KBOS 35 s → 15 s (the 6-7 s of the head route and the ~9 s of the proposals remain). A head route refused for another reason searches the levels as before. Case K274. The Fuel screen should show `status: 'proposal'` as « proposition », not as a route (Features SOG).

**Which zones a query reads: the manifest's cells and the date line** (SOG-0356, 14 Nov 2026). `zonesFor(box)` retains a published zone when the box touches one of its **cells** (`sia/base/index.json`, `cells: { "lat": [[lon0, lon1], …] }`, degrees). Measured for LFPO → EIDW (the survey of SOG-0349): 4 zones, 26 files, 30.4 MB, 23.4 MB of them the zone `us`, because its cells said `[-180, 180]` on the latitudes 51 and 52 and `[-180, 67]` on 53 — an Aleutian contour that crosses the date line, counted across the whole width (the builder walked the ring without cutting it). **Two gestures.** (1) **At the reading** (`aerodb.js`, so that a phone gains without the base being published again): the manifest is an UNTRUSTED input (a file of the data repository), its cells are cleaned (`cleanCells`: integer latitudes −90 to 90, ranges of two finite numbers from −180 to 180 with lon0 ≤ lon1, at most 181 lines of 400 ranges; before, a malformed row made `(cells[y] || []).some` throw and every query fail); **a range of more than 180° is not a sure coverage**, it is kept apart (`wide`) and retains the zone only if the box also touches one of the country's frames of the zones table (`AERODB_ZONES`, widened by the usual margin; a zone with no frame known keeps its old behaviour); a zone with no usable cell falls back on its frames, as a zone with no cells always did. Paris → Dublin asks fr, gb and ie (3 zones); Anchorage and the Aleutians still ask `us`. (2) **At the builder** (`shared/sia_base.js` `coverage`): a ring with a jump of more than 180° between two vertices is carried by +360 on its negative longitudes and its cells are wrapped back into [−180, 180[; an airway segment across the date line is sampled the short way round. **Until the zone `us` is built and published again, only gesture (1) works** (the published manifest keeps the wide rows; the reading ignores them). Case K272.

**French registry: the reduced file in the private data repository** (SOG-0341, 16 Nov 2026; Q-0060: yes, the user knowing that the licence of the export is NOT confirmed — public by law, no licence of reuse). The upload of the registry export (`base/upload`, recognised by its header) still derives the five-column file under `data/local/registry/fr_immat.json` (SOG-0270, k-anonymity of the maker) and now ALSO publishes it: `registry_fr.publish` writes `data/registry/fr_immat.json` — `{ schema: 1, source, date, columns: [5], rows: [[registration, maker, type, serial, home aerodrome]] }`, the date being the date of the export — REBUILT from the derived file (exactly five text cells per row, bounded to 80 characters and cleaned of control characters, a registration of the expected form, a type present; any other key or cell dropped; the maker of fewer than 10 aircraft recounted at this boundary and left empty), atomic, one file. Gates: `data/` a git repository (else `published: false, notPublished: 'no-repo'`), no merge waiting (`'merge-pending'`: nothing written into `data/`), else the code of a refusal (`read`, `headers`, `rows`, `empty`, `size`; never a content). The answer of the upload carries `published` and `notPublished`. **Synchronisation**: `registry` is in the pool (`data_sync.POOL`) and in `GESTURE_ONLY` (only « Synchroniser » commits a ~3 MB file; a background pass never does); on a conflict (`merge_raw`) the version of THIS workstation wins, no field of the file decides (a date would be written by the pilot who wants to win), no copy in `_conflits/` (a whole table). LIMIT: a doctored file pushed WITHOUT a conflict is taken by everyone until the next export of an honest workstation. `publish` writes nothing through a link: neither the folder, nor the destination, nor the `.tmp` is a link or a junction and the real folder stays under the real `data/` (else `notPublished: 'write'`); the `.tmp` is erased in a `finally`. **For the readers** (the phone, the site in GitHub mode: Features SOG / Réglages): the file comes from a repository every pilot can write to — read it as an UNTRUSTED input (type by content, `columns` equal to the five names, `rows` a list of five-text lists, numbers bounded, text escaped, never trusted for HTML); it is read under the policy `reference` (cap of the frame, 32 MB). The page that says « Registre français non déposé sur ce poste » and its notice « ce poste seulement » are Réglages'. Case K285.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) defect 4: the RAD « compulsory » finding** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K303). `rad_flight.evaluate`: a violation of `COMPULSORY FOR TFC VIA Y` of a point rule (Annex 2B, point X in column 7) with no affected segment adds two entries to `search_exclusions`: `{ kind: 'compel', point: X, before: Y, id, sheet, row }` (pass by X next to Y) and `{ kind: 'point', point: Y, fallback: true, … }` (set Y aside). `plan.plan`: the `fallback` entries are used only once the `compel` of the same rule has been tried (second retry); a retry whose entries are all already known is not played (`rad_search_attempts`: « reprise RAD non faite : elle n'exclurait rien de neuf »); when a point has been set aside the primary carries a REVUE remark with the detour and both distances. `Nav.routing_graph`: `graph.rad_compel = { Y: X }` and `graph.rad_avoid` (the `point` entries); the `compel` entries do not block the graph. `free_route.solve`: an entry Y with `rad_compel` is reached BY X (the European search goes to X, then a created DCT X → Y: `lead`/`eff`), an exit Y is left FOR X (Y → X, then the European search leaves from X); the points of the answer list X and Y; the points in `rad_avoid` are neither entries nor exits (`NO_AVOID` when none is left); `sections._solve` sets aside a track whose entry or exit is avoided (`rejected` code `rad`). Measured on the real base, read only (LEMD → KBOS, FL370, 16 Nov 13:00): via REGHI 3 219.3 NM with two errors (LE2666 and LF2096); passing by ETIKI first 3 267.2 NM (KORUL DCT ETIKI DCT REGHI, 30 NM west of REGHI), no error, 3 attempts (the second retry is LE2666's), 7.4 s (10.9 s before); with REGHI set aside (the repli, forced) the next entry NASBA 4 812.3 NM (+1 593 NM), said.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) defect 3: the tries of the free route** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K302). `free_route.solve(…, report)`: the pairs (entry reached, exit) of each pack of entries are tried in the order of their bound (the real European cost + the rest estimated), with no fixed number (`TRIES` is gone); a pack is searched only if `ratio × (the best bound of its first entry)` is below the best cost found; a pair is not tried when its bound is at least the best cost; the loop stops when `FREE_S` (3 s) or `engine.DEADLINE` is used up (the reason is then `NO_TIME`); an exit whose section failed is dead for the next pairs (`dead.add(o)`). `report` receives `{ tried, stopped }` and `sections.free` carries them (`tried`, `stopped` = `bound` | `time` | `end`). Cause measured: the exits ETARI, PIKIL, VENER and RESNO (first points of the west tracks at 55-57 N, 15-20 W) link to MIMKU, DONIB and RUBEX (Scotland, Northern Ireland: N562, N552, FL195-255 only): the search from the exit stops after 4 states; DOGAL, the fifth, works.

**Route matrix, option `--failed`** (SOG-0354 L5 4 (c); case K301): `tools/route_matrix.py --failed` replays only the requests of `detail.jsonl` that did not return a computed route (`failed_ids`: none, proposal, error) into `replay.jsonl` and `progress.txt` (« REJEU des demandes en échec »); `--failed --summary` writes `table_replay.md` and the summary of the replay. The first pass is not touched. The whole matrix is run once at the end, on the word of the ergonomics session.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) defect 2: the retries at other levels** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K300). `plan.level_matters(primary, info)` → `(bool, reason)`; used by `plan.plan` when the head route is rejected in calculation: `level_ok` gates the stepped route (`nav.generate(..., allow_steps)`), the variants by penalty (kept when a route was found with an error that is not a level matter), and the loop over the default lower levels (the levels the request names itself are still tried). A level matter: an ERREUR of the control whose text matches `LEVEL_WORDS` (FL…, RFL, niveau, altitude, plancher, plafond, profil); without a route: `sections.level_retry` (a track set aside for `level` that `nat_layer.check` finds valid, direction and hour at the passage of its entry, at the first level it offers: `sections.level_tracks`), or, with no sections, « à ce niveau » in the error. The answer carries `level_retry: { tried, matters, why, levels: [{ fl, seconds, found }] }` (calculation, head route rejected; None otherwise) and `timing.retries`. `sections` / `timing.sections` / `graph_s` are those of the requested level: `plan.plan` keeps the `sections_info` of the head route (and its RAD retries) and resets `nav.sections_info` at the start of each plan (the context is kept from a request to the next). Measured on the real base: CYYZ → EDDF « out » 4.2 s (was 14.5), KLAX → LFPG « out » 4.2 s (was 13.5), the first one cold (context 0.9 s counted); LEMD → KBOS stays 10.9 s: a route with a RAD error that is not a level matter still has its RAD retries (3.5 s), the forced FRA search and the variants — defect 4, not touched here.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) defect 1: the elevation** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K299). `flight_profile.elevation(icao, value)`: None → 0.0, a number from `ELEV_MIN_FT = -1500` to `ELEV_MAX_FT = 30000` → float, anything else → `ValueError` « Élévation de EHAM : … ft refusée (de -1 500 à 30 000 ft). » (used by `Nav.routing_graph`, the terminal search, the control of a route, `fra_recovery`: the answer of the request carries the sentence in `primary.error`). The ground of a flight is `min(0, departure_ft, arrival_ft)`: `flight_profile.build` and `Nav.search` read a published floor of exactly 0 as that ground, so a flight from or to an aerodrome below the sea (EHAM -10 ft) is feasible. The defect touched EVERY flight that has such an aerodrome as an end, not only the ocean: EHAM → LFPG on the real base gave a route and the ERREUR « Profil vertical impossible… », EGLL → EHAM gave no route. After: EHAM → LFPG 236.6 NM, EGLL → EHAM 252.4 NM, EHAM → KJFK 3 407.8 NM, KJFK → EHAM 3 547.5 NM, all without error; EIDW → KJFK 3 022.7 NM and EGLL → LFPG 197.8 NM unchanged.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) d1 bis: the shortest free route** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K306). `free_route.solve`: the pairs are ordered by `nm_of(path of the European section) + lead + ocean + tail` (a lower bound in NM: the real European section, the great circle for the rest) and the chains are compared by `total_nm = nm_of(path)` of the answer; a pair is not tried when its bound is at least the best `total_nm`; after the pairs of a search, the loop goes on to a search to the entries that remain (`remaining`) while `pack_bound(remaining[0])` (the great circle from the departure to the entry + the free route + the great circle from the exit) is below the best `total_nm`; each extra search is capped at `EXTRA_SEARCH_S` (0.15 s) and the whole at `FREE_S` (3 s) or `engine.DEADLINE`; the stop is `bound`, `time` or `end`; `sections.free.searches` counts the searches. Measured on the real base, read only, the 17 requests of the second run over 5 s: 17 computed, max 4.66 s (LEMD → KORD « in » 3.97, LEMD → CYYZ « in » 3.90, LEMD → KBOS « in » 3.74, KBOS → EDDF « out » 4.66, EIDW « out » 1.3 to 1.7, EDDF → KSFO « out » 2.30, LFPG 2.2 to 2.6); distances: 13 identical to the second run, LFPG → KLAX 5 293.8 (5 312.1 before), LFPG → KORD 3 787.7 (3 806.0), KBOS → EDDF 3 244.6 (3 415.2 with d1 alone). Tried and not kept: stopping by the cost bound × the inflation of the best chain (routes 5 % longer in NM); `FREE_S` 1 s (KBOS → EDDF 3 291.3 NM).

**NAT window judged at the 30°W** (SOG-0403, 17 Nov 2026; case K318). NAT Doc 007 V.2026-1: § 2.1.1 « peak westbound traffic crossing the 30W longitude between 1130 UTC and 1900 UTC and peak eastbound traffic crossing the 30W longitude between 0100 UTC and 0800 UTC »; § 2.1.3 « The hours of validity of the two Organised Track Systems (OTS) are as follows: (Westbound) Day-time OTS 1130 UTC to 1900 UTC at 30°W; (Eastbound) Night-time OTS 0100 UTC to 0800 UTC at 30°W » and « the specific hours of validity for each OTS are indicated in the NAT track message »; § 4.1.6 also reads « cross 30°W ». `sections._solve` has `passage(o, to_entry)`: `to_entry` (great circle from the departure at the first estimate, the real distance of the section of the departure side at the second) + the distance along the legs of the chain `o` up to its crossing of `nat_layer.MERIDIAN` (−30°), `(distance, '30W')`; a chain that does not cross it gives `(to_entry, 'entry')`. The hour is `departure + distance / speed`. `nat_layer.check(meta, fl, east, eta)` is unchanged: `eta` is now the hour at the 30°W. `nat_layer.time_fields(meta, eta, entry, point, hour)`: `at` (the hour at the point), `point` (`30W` | `entry`), `entry` (the entry point), `from`, `to`, and, when the refusal is by at most `NEAR_MIN` = 60 minutes (a route not stale), `near` = `{ minutes, side: 'late', depart_until }` (the passage is after the end: leave at or before) or `{ minutes, side: 'early', depart_from }` (the passage is before the start: leave at or after). `sections.entry` (the hour at the entry of the ocean of the route TAKEN) is the entry, not the 30°W. Measured on the bench of K237 (KJFK → EGLL FL370, track A through 51/40 and 5130/30): the entry ENTRA is passed at 07:05Z at 04:30Z departure, the 30°W at 08:44Z: a window 08:00-20:00Z takes the route (it was refused at the entry), a window 01:30-08:00Z refuses it (`near`: 44 minutes late, leave at or before 03:45Z; at 03:40Z it is taken). `tools/route_matrix.py` dates its departures at the 30°W (53°N), the benches of K237, K298, K299, K300, K309 and K310 end their window at 10:00Z (it was 08:00Z: calibrated on the entry), K293 starts its route C at 16:00Z.


**RAD rules of form A, and the floors of the free route** (SOG-0401 lot 3, 17 Nov 2026; case K332). NEVER A ROUTE LOST TO A DECIDED RULE: `plan.plan`, when the head route is not found (`ok` false, not a budget stop) and `rad.blocks` is not empty, assesses again with `_rad_fallback` — `engine.assess` then gives `generate` NO blocks and says the explicit prohibitions (`Interdiction RAD explicite … enfreinte : aucune route sans infraction`) and the findings of the whole flight (`Règle RAD enfreinte … : …`) as REVUE instead of ERREUR; the route is accepted (`computed`), the key stays on the request for the alternatives. `rad_flight.alternatives(text)`: a numbered list « 1. … 2. … » of at least two items in order (an OR); a sub-list « a. » / « i) », an exception, a gap in the numbers: None (not decided). `condition` takes the alternatives (true if one is true, false if all are false, else None). `split_parts(util, clock)`: the parts of a rule separated by « ---------- », one text and one time per part; `evaluate` and `static_blocks` judge each part (a rule is violated when one part is; the blocks of the search come from the ONLY / NOT parts at H24). `clock_ok` / `clock_active`: H24, « HH:MM-HH:MM » (UTC), « HH:MM-HH:MM (HH:MM-HH:MM) » (winter, then summer): read when both give the same answer, otherwise the rule stays to examine. `route_engine/fra_floor.py`: `floors(rad)` = `{ zone: FL }` for `ch` (LS) and `it` (LI), from the rows of « Annex 3B FRA LIM » whose DCT limit is « FRA » (the lower limit of the H24 slices: « ABV FL195 », « BTN FL195-FL245 »; None for the country if the rows disagree or a cell is not readable); `findings(legs, floor_of)`: the legs of an airway (not a SID, a STAR, a DCT or a procedure) of a zone with a floor that the PROFILE puts above it (the higher of the two altitudes of the leg: a climb that stays under the floor is not a finding, a climb through it, a cruise or a descent from above are), grouped by airway and zone; `unknown(legs, floor_of)`: the legs whose profile gives no altitude (« to examine »). A floor is plausible (FL100 to FL500) and read from cells of at most 4 000 signs (a rule text over 4 000 signs is not decided by `rad_flight.condition` either: `MAX_CONDITION`); a workbook with neither digest nor name is not cached. `engine.assess`: typed → ERREUR per run (at most 40, then a sentence), the legs `compatible: false`; search → one REVUE (the search keeps its shortest route). `fra_floor.CAP_SEARCH` (false): when true `plan.plan` posts `nav.fra_floors` and `Nav.routing_graph` caps the ceiling of the airway edges of those zones at the floor (a copy of the edge with `fra_floor_cap`; the cache key carries the floors); false by default: capping made the routes descend to FL195 over the Alps (+14 %). The answer: `fra_floors` = `{ applied, not_read, runs, unknown }` (plan and view). NOT read: Germany (EDUUFRAW FL245, EDUUFRAN FL285, EDUUFRAE FL315: three slices without contours), France and Spain (free route by sub-space).



**RAD: a segment rule applies to a portion** (SOG-0401, 17 Nov 2026; case K337). `rad_flight.target` (annex 2B with airway, from, to): the legs from `from` to `to` on the airway (the exact cover) when the flight flies the whole segment; otherwise `ctx['airway'](alias, p, q)` (`Nav.airway_portion`: the shortest chain of loaded edges from p to q following the directions of the airway; returns the edges in that direction and the same edges reversed, or None) gives the segment and the legs of the flight that lie in it are the target. The flight not on the airway: not concerned (decided); legs only against the direction or no chain: `UNSURE`: `evaluate` runs the rule with the rule applicable AND not applicable and decides it only if both give the same answer (always « no infraction »), otherwise it stays « to be interpreted ». The static blocks of the search (`Rad._blocks`, kind `segment`) still hit the leg that is exactly `from`–`to`: a route that flies a portion is found by `evaluate` and retried with the legs found as exclusions.

**RAD form B, 1st form: the sequence of a VIA group** (SOG-0401, 18 Nov 2026; case K347). `rad_via.read(spec)` → `[('node', {points}), ('conn', {airways | DCT}), …]` or None (a list of points `(A, B, C)`, two nodes in a row, a text that ends on a connector, an airway where a point is expected: not read, the old reading applies); `rad_via.match(spec, legs, aliases, known)` → True (the flight has consecutive points matching the nodes, joined by the connector: DCT = one leg `via` DCT, an airway = one or more consecutive legs on it, `*` = any point, a list = any of them), False (a node with none of its points on the flight, an airway no leg uses, a DCT with no direct leg: the group cannot hold), None (everything else: order, a point not in `known`, legs that do not chain, no legs, more than `MAX_STEPS` = 200 000 steps: the search is memoised on (node of the group, node of the flight)). `rad_flight.context` carries `legs`; `conjunction` calls `match` for a VIA group that `read` accepts, before the old checks.

**RAD: the requested level and the level at a point** (SOG-0401, 17 Nov 2026; case K335). `rad_flight.rfl_test(spec, fl)`: `RFL ABV FLnnn`, `RFL BLW FLnnn`, `RFL BTN FLnnn-FLmmm` against `ctx['fl']` (the level FILED, passed by `engine.assess` to `evaluate(…, fl)`); on a limit or without a level: None. `at_test(kind, level, point, ctx)`: `ABV / BLW FLnnn AT point` against `ctx['alt'][point]` = the altitude of the PROFILE at each point of the route (`context(legs, …)`: the end of each leg, the start of the first); a point not on the route: false; unknown or exactly on the limit: None. Both are atoms of `condition`'s conjunction (with DEP / ARR / VIA, numbered alternatives and one EXC); `static_blocks` has no level (never a static block). `plan.plan`: after the RAD retries, the decided infractions that remain on a CALCULATED route (`Interdiction RAD explicite …`, `RAD <id> (…)`) become REVUE (« Règle RAD enfreinte (aucune route sans infraction, la meilleure est rendue) : … ») under one first sentence and the status is recomputed.

**NAT route or free route: the threshold of 5 %** (SOG-0421, 17 Nov 2026; case K333). `sections._solve`, after the best NAT chain (`path`, its NM): `_nat_or_free(nav, g, start, end, ratio, info, path, tid, valid, meta)`. No comparison when `nat_nm <= great circle × NAT_COMPARE_MIN` (1.15: the search of the free route costs 3 to 8 s; on the 72 westbound flights it changed only the flights of Madrid, NAT at 1.17 to 1.25 times the great circle). Otherwise the free route is searched on a COPY of `info` (`_free`, the same searches as when no NAT route is valid; its steps are added to the timing whatever the outcome); if it is found and `nat_nm - free_nm >= NAT_MAX_EXTRA × free_nm`, `nav.sections_info` becomes the copy (`source: random`) with `nat_discarded` = `{ track (the label), key, nm, free_nm, extra_nm, extra_pct, max_pct }`, and `reason` says it; `nat_near` = `_near_nat(nav, free_path, tracks)`: the valid routes (the candidates of the flight) whose points, interpolated by longitude, are within `NAT_NEAR_DEG` (1°) of latitude of a leg created by the free route, sampled every degree of longitude: `[{ track, min_deg, from_lon, to_lon }]`. `engine.assess` writes two REVUE (« Route NAT X écartée : … » and « La route libre passe à moins d'un degré de latitude de la route NAT X … ») when `nat_discarded` and a free route are in the answer. A route that stays within the threshold is unchanged. **The links** (SOG-0450 C): `free_route._links` classes the high network once per `Nav` by latitude (`nav._high_by_lat`, checked against the network object itself) and keeps only the nodes whose longitude is within the reach implied by `sin²(d/2) ≥ cos φ1 cos φ2 sin²(Δλ/2)` (a margin of 2 %): the nodes and their order are those of the full scan. **The question asked** (SOG-0450 B): `_nat_or_free` gives `free_route.solve` a `limit` = NAT NM / (1 + `NAT_MAX_EXTRA`): the bound of the pairs is the smaller of the best route found and the limit (`cap()`), so a pair whose lower bound exceeds the limit is not tried while the answer is « no »; once a route within the limit is found the bound is that route and the search is the former one. A search made for a limit answers every question with a smaller or equal limit. **One free search per calculation** (SOG-0450, case K340): `_nat_or_free` keeps on `nav` (one `Nav` per calculation) the free search made for (start, end, ratio, avoided points, compelled points); another NAT route of the same calculation is compared with it, and a retry whose constraints include those of a search that already said « the NAT route stays » says it again without searching. A comparison made, and its NAT route kept, is not made again at the RAD retries of the same calculation (`nav._nat_kept`, keyed by the entry, the exit, the track and the NM): each retry restarts the search, and the comparison cost 3 to 4 s every time.

**Route check: the limit of the direct at the connection of an aerodrome** (SOG-0401 lot 3, 17 Nov 2026; case K331). `route_engine/dct_limits.py`: `rules(rad)` reads the lines `XX_LIM_AD` of the sheet « Annex 3A Conditions » of the RAD in force into CLAUSES `{ prefix | codes, kinds (arr / dep), nm, complex, other }` in the order of the text (a general limit of the country, then the exceptions that replace it: `ED**`, a list `(EDAC, EDDF…)`, a single aerodrome; « ARR/DEP », « Departures/Arrivals », « Other AD's » = all the others); a clause that carries a level, an hour or « except » (also in the text that follows it up to the next limit), or a cell whose struck text was removed (`strike_removed`), is `complex`: not decided. `limit_of(rad, icao, 'dep' | 'arr')` = `{ state: known | complex | unknown, nm, rule }`. `check(rad, legs, dep, arr, typed, procedures)` looks at the FIRST leg from the departure and the LAST to the arrival when it is a direct (`via` DCT, no `procedure_id`): N > 0 NM exceeded → ERREUR when `mode` is `validate` (a typed route), REVUE otherwise; 0 NM → `connection_points(procedures, ad, end)` (the last point of the SIDs read, the first of the STARs read; None when the base reads none): the point is one = within, is not = ERREUR (typed) / REVUE, None = « à examiner »; `complex` / `unknown` = « à examiner ». The leg of an ERREUR is `compatible: false` with the alert. The answer: `dct_limits` = `{ checked, not_applied }` (plan and view). Called by `engine.assess` after the remark on the missing SID / STAR. NOT read: annex 3B « FRA LIM » (the limit of the direct en route by airspace, needs the contours of the spaces: said in `not_applied`). The RAD parsers cache by workbook (`sha256`).

**Route answer: the RAD numbers, the whole list, the unknown spaces, the request** (SOG-0401 lot 1, 17 Nov 2026; case K317). `view.to_view` (the shape of the Fuel screen) has: `rad` = `{ title, start, decided (rad_summary.evaluated_conditions), review (review_conditions: the mentions « À interpréter » left), findings (the number of constats), total (rad_count or the length of the list), listed, cut (total > listed: the bound cut), unknown_spaces: [{ space, rules: [ids] }], items: [{ sheet, row, id, reason, assessment, validity }] }` (None without a RAD book; no `values`: the answer for EDDF → LFMN was 578 KB with them); `plan.MAX_RAD` = 1 500 (it was 100): the list is whole below it, above it `rad_count` and `truncated: ['rad']` of the plan and `rad.cut` of the view say it; `message` is `''` when alternatives exist (the phrase « N alternative(s) sans incompatibilité détectée » is gone: `plan.alternative_message` too) and « Aucune alternative trouvée… » when there is none; `request` = the request as the engine took it (`view.bounded_request`: 60 fields at most, strings cut at 600, lists at 60, no key that begins with `_`). `rad_flight.target`: an Annex 2B rule whose only target is `v[7]` and that is not in `ctx['known']` (the ids of the base and the routes used) is a SPACE: `ctx['unknown_spaces'][name]` lists the rule, its `assessment` becomes « À interpréter : la cible est un ESPACE (X) inconnu de la base (aucun polygone) : règle non appliquée » (so `review_conditions` counts it) and `rad_summary.unknown_spaces` carries them; there is no polygon branch yet because no airspace of the base has these names (checked: LSASFRA, EDUUUTA, LSAZACC, EDMMACC, LIMMACC, EDUUFRAW, LSAGACC, EDUUUAC, EDUUALP1L: 0 of 25 307). Measured on the real base (EDDF → LFMN, FL350): decided 94, review 437, findings 0, total 531 listed 531, 8+ unknown spaces (EDUUUTA cited by 19 rules, EDUUUTAW 4, EDUUUTAC 3…), the whole view 578 KB.

**The NAT source read again before the answer** (SOG-0394 lot C, 17 Nov 2026; case K314). What triggered a reading of the source until now: only the page (`AeroDb.natEnsure`: Carte on opening, the « Lire les routes NAT » button of Carburant) through the job `base/update` zone `nat`, when `natState().expired !== false` (NO route valid NOW), one job at a time, two tries at least five minutes apart. `route/compute` (tools/regen/route.py) never read it. Now `compute(job, args, ctx)` calls `_nat_before` first (before the key of the results cache, so that the new fingerprint of the NAT folder makes the cache miss): `route_engine/nat_refresh.before(data, request, ctx)` → `decide` (files alone: the aerodromes' positions from the aeroindex of the base, the flight crosses the Atlantic (`WEST_LON` -35 / `EAST_LON` -15), `plan.depart_of` gives an hour, no track of the direction of the flight, of `nat_tracks.json` or `nat_past.json`, whose [from, to] meets [departure, departure + great-circle distance / speed]) and, if `needed`, `refresh(data, sense)`: `aero_fetch.nat(data, timeout=CONNECT_S)` in a thread joined for `BOUND_S` (a late write is atomic and its result ignored), at most one try per `MIN_GAP_S` (300 s) and per sense, failure included (memory of the launcher process), `ctx['lan']` → nothing is read; only a context with `nat_refresh: True` reads (tools/launch.py sets it on the jobs: a case or a tool never reads the source). The engine reads the file when it loads its context; `plan.fingerprint` covers `sia/zones/nat`, so the same request sees the new file. The answer (`_nat_attach`, on a copy: the cached result is never modified): `sections.nat` = `{ sense, messages (the ids of nat_tracks.json), fetched_at (its date), age_s, refresh, why?, seconds?, tracks_in_sense, valid_at_passage, text? }` and `timing.nat_refresh` (seconds, counted apart from the search budget; absent when no reading was tried); when nothing covers the passage `sections.reason` gets the sentence of `text` (« …n'a aucune route vers l'est valable à ce passage ; la source n'en publie pas encore » / « Relecture de la source impossible (…) » / « …relue il y a moins de cinq minutes » / « …relue que depuis le poste de l'utilisateur »). `why` of a `skipped`: `has-routes` (a route covers the passage), `no-hour`, `delay`, `lan`, `off`. Jobs are started from the workstation only (`public='poste'`): `lan` is for a launcher that says so. `route_matrix.py` calls the engine, not the job: the network stays refused there. SOG-0403 (R1): `nat_refresh.decide` judges the need like the engine: the window of a system of the direction is compared with the hour of the PASSAGE of the 30°W (`MERIDIAN_GATE` = 53°N 30°W, great circle from the departure at the speed of the flight; `decide` says `passage`), not with the duration of the flight; case K314 (6). Security review (17 Nov): `refresh` gives `why` among `timeout | source | format | keep | error` (`nat_refresh.WHY`: one fixed sentence each; `delay`, `off`, `lan` for what is skipped), the detail (type and text of the exception, the text of the errors of aero_fetch: they may carry a path) goes to `nat_refresh.LOG` (the console of the launcher by default), never to `sections.nat`; `_positions` and `state` refuse a file over `MAX_POS_BYTES` / `MAX_NAT_BYTES` and catch `RecursionError` / `MemoryError` (`_pos` remembers the failure with the date of the file); `aero_fetch.nat` writes under `_NAT_LOCK`.

**NAT messages kept: the engine reads them** (SOG-0394 lot A, 17 Nov 2026; case K313). `nat_layer.add` reads `data/sia/zones/nat/nat_past.json` (`nat_layer.read(path, MAX_PAST)`: the same bounds as `nat_tracks.json`, 2 MB, a letter, points, levels, dates; 120 routes) after the file of the day; a kept route whose key (letter, start, direction) is already in the file of the day is left out. A kept route gets the key `<letter>~<start>` (`meta.track`), `meta.letter`, `meta.source = 'kept'` and `meta.label` (« A (gardée du 10/11) », the name used in the sentences); the edges keep the route name `NAT<letter>`; it is listed in `nav.nat['kept']`, `nav.nat['points'][key]` holds its points and `nav.nat['ends']` its two ends (entries and exits of the free route). `nat_layer.check`: for a kept route the hour is ALWAYS checked (the stale pass of the message of the day does not apply: K237 is untouched) and `nat_layer.unknown_hour` sets it aside when the request gives no hour. `plan.plan` starts every request with the kept routes in `nav.nat_rejected` (closed to every search, the whole search too); `sections.solve` replaces that set by the ones it has set aside, so a kept route judged valid at the passage is open. A route set aside has `code` (`time`, `level`, `direction`, `network`, `rad`), its sentence with the label, the fields of SOG-0391 and, for a kept route only, `letter` and `source: 'kept'`. `directions`, `issued` and `stale` of `nav.nat` (and the phrase « Aucune route NAT vers l'est dans le message du… ») count the last publication only. `sections.taken` = `{ track (the letter), key, source ('last' | 'kept'), from, to, issued, points: [{ id, lat, lon }] }` for a track of the day taken; the REVUE remark of the route says « Route gardée d'un message précédent, valable à l'heure de son passage » for a kept one. Measured on the bench of K237 (KJFK → EGLL at FL370, the kept eastbound A of 01:30-08:00Z, the westbound A of 11:30-19:00Z in the file of the day): at 04:30Z the kept A is taken (entry ENTRA 07:05Z); at 10:30Z it is set aside for the hour (13:02Z) and a free route is made; without an hour it is set aside; the whole search at 10:30Z with the kept routes open would take it (3 029.5 NM): closed, no route.

**Points on the limit of a FIR: the flag `lim: 'fir'`** (SOG-0417 (a), 17 Nov 2026; case K328). `AeroSources.geojson` (`shared/aero_sources.js`): a Feature `sog: 'point'` whose property `remarks` matches `/\bFIR\s*BDRY\b/i` gives a point `{ id, type, lat, lon, lim: 'fir' }`; a point without it has no `lim`. The flag is of a CLOSED vocabulary (`fir` for now; the text of the remark is never copied into the base). `AeroSources.merge`: when the point of a second source has a twin (same id, within the distance of `near`) already in the base and it carries `lim`, the twin takes it. It goes through the compilation (`sia_base.js`) as a field of the point; the consumers that read `id`, `lat`, `lon` ignore it. Written by the compilation of the zone: the Portugal zone must be rebuilt to get it (the points of the other zones have it where their source says « FIR BDRY »: Ireland, Sweden, Iceland use the same reader of the remarks). The remark is read only when it is a text, its first 400 signs (an untrusted input: never converted with `String()`). The engine does not read it yet.

**NAT: a route is written under its name only when it is followed whole** (SOG-0414, 17 Nov 2026; case K325). `nat_layer.read` keeps the NAR of the message (`nar`: names of at most 7 signs, 12 at most). `nat_layer.add` notes the published points that `nav.ids` does not know (`missing`; `nav.nat.skipped` keeps saying `A: JANJO inconnu`): when there are some, `meta.partial` = `{ from, to (the first and the last PLACED point), missing, exit_missing (the last published point is missing), nar }`, and the body edges of the route have the route `DCT` (not `NATx`: the text of the route writes the points one by one, like a route out of its window, SOG-0403 R2); the links of entry and exit are unchanged. `oriented_chains`, `engine.assess` (`nat_legs`) and `view.nat_of` recognise such a leg by `nat.partial`. `engine.assess` adds ONE review per route: « Route NAT <lettre> suivie de <from> à <to> seulement : sa sortie <X> n'est pas dans la base | <points> ne sont pas dans la base ; les routes nord-américaines <NAR> du message ne sont pas suivies ; écrite point par point, jamais sous son nom. » (`issue_kind`: `NAT`), and the summary « Routes NAT du … : NATx » is not said for a route that is all partial. `sections.taken` gains `written` (`name` | `points`: points for a partial route and for a route out of its window) and `partial`. The NAR of the message are not read as airways (the base does not define them: only N260B-N271B); the fallback of the X-Plane data (Q-0079) is the way to place the exits and the NAR.

**NAT routes: the search of the entries stops at the three cheapest** (SOG-0412, the time, 17 Nov 2026; case K324). `sections._solve` asks `_europe(nav, g, start, entries, info, need=EUROPE_NEED)` (`EUROPE_NEED` = 3, only when there are more entries than that). `Nav._search` with `goals` notes a goal at its first pop and returns when `need` are reached; the goals come out of the queue in the order of their cost (a goal has the heuristic 0), so the three reached are the three cheapest. `info.europe` = `{ goals, reached, expanded, seconds, truncated, stopped }` (`stopped`: the search was stopped by `need` with entries left). The candidates whose entry was not reached because of it are `untried`: not `network` (« son entrée n'est pas atteignable ») and not in the skeleton; if no skeleton came out (every reached route is refused for its hour or level) or if no chain came out of them (`best is None`), the search is made again for all the entries (`while True`), and the loop goes on with the full result; otherwise they are `rejected[tid] = ('bound', « non essayée … »)` (closed to the whole search like the others). The approximation: an entry left aside could have given a slightly shorter route than the three cheapest (the total adds the length of the route and of the exit side, which differ little between the routes of one day); measured on the real base the chosen route is the same one (EIDW → KJFK, KLAX; EGLL → KJFK; LEMD → KBOS).

**NAT links from the nodes that reach the level of the route** (SOG-0412, 17 Nov 2026; case K323). `nat_layer.add` joins the first and the last point of a route to the nearest nodes (`LINK_NM` = 900 NM, `LINK_NODES` = 3) of a network, by DCT arcs `nat_link` with the band of the route (`lower` = its lowest level × 100). The network is `main_network(nav, floor_ft)` with `floor_ft = max(HIGH_FT, lower)`: the nodes of the airway edges (not the routes, not the links) whose `upper` reaches `floor_ft`, in a connected component of at least `MIN_COMPONENT` nodes (one quarter of the largest on a small bench). It is computed once per `floor_ft` in an `add`. Before, `floor_ft` was `HIGH_FT` = 24 500 ft whatever the route: the nodes of airways with a ceiling of 25 500 ft were « high », were the nearest to the entries of the routes of EGGW → KJFK, and a flight at FL340 reaches them at 19 500-25 500 ft, never at the band 34 000-40 000 ft of the link. An entry that is itself in the network of its floor has no link. The free route (`free_route.gates`, `nat_layer.main_network(nav)`) keeps `HIGH_FT`. Measured on the real base, EGGW → KJFK FL340 of 17 Nov: six goals, six reached (one before), 4 783 states (32 364), 0.16 s (0.8 s); the entries BALIX, GOMUP, SUNOT, BILTO, PIKIL, RESNO are linked from BAGSO, RIMVU, LUSOD, BOFUM, LIFFY (250 to 390 NM).

**Route control: the two writings of a NAT point; the old-cycle remark** (SOG-0411 A and B, 17 Nov 2026; case K322). `Nav.keys_of(ident)`: an identifier that the base does not know but whose coordinates are readable (`engine.coordinate`: `5100N05000W`, `5230N01000W`, `4959N08712W`) is looked up among the nodes named by a NAT coordinate (an identifier with a slash: `51/50`, `5230/10`, `5130/30`) before a new node is made: the same position (0.15 NM) is the same node, so `nav.path(nav.graph(None, 'NATF'), current, target)` finds the portion of the track from the node of the track. The remark `Cycle de navigation périmé pour <ZONE> : <cycle>` (`engine.assess`, a cycle at least 28 days before the flight) gathers the parsed legs by (zone, cycle) and says them: `source de N260B-N271B (NICSO → ALLEX)` (one entry per route, from its first point to its last, four at most then `et n autre(s)`), then `; le cycle courant de la zone est <cycle>` when the zone has another current cycle. The legs of a NAT track and its links carry the date of their message as their cycle, so a track of the day is never the old cycle: the remark is about the airways of the zone NAT (the NARs) read in a file of an older cycle.

**NAT messages kept: nat_past.json** (SOG-0394 lot B, 17 Nov 2026; case K312). Writer: `aero_fetch.nat(data)` (`nat_past(folder, tracks)` BEFORE the replacement of `nat_tracks.json`). File: `data/sia/zones/nat/nat_past.json` = `{ source, tracks: [{ id, route, east, west, nar, by, from, to }] }`, the same shape as the tracks of `nat_tracks.json`, sorted by start then letter. Content: the routes of the old `nat_tracks.json` and of the old `nat_past.json` that the new publication does not carry (key = letter + `from` + direction `east`/`west`/`both`; a route of the same key that is still published is not kept twice, the old file wins over an old kept copy), removed when the end of their validity (`to`, else `from`) is more than three days old (`NAT_PAST_DAYS`), 120 at most (`NAT_PAST_MAX`, the oldest end first), the routes to come included. Untrusted input (`nat_clean`, `nat_read`): at most 1 MB and 400 routes read, a letter `[A-Z]{1,2}`, 2 to 40 points of the form of the engine (`nat_layer`), levels `\d{3}` from 100 to 600, dates of the exact form `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` (a route with no date at all is dropped), `nar` and `by` checked, every other field dropped. Write: atomic (`write`), not rewritten when the content is the same; if it fails (`OSError`) `nat()` returns the failure (`NAT (FAA) : les routes retirées n'ont pas pu être gardées`) and does NOT replace `nat_tracks.json`. Nothing else reads it yet: the page (`shared/sia_base.js` reads `nat_tracks.json` by name; a `.json` of the same folder goes through its GeoJSON branch and is reported « format inconnu » or empty, to settle with the page's session) and Carte, Carburant and `natState` do not see it; the engine reads it in lot A.

**Route check: levels outside the band of a leg** (SOG-0401 point 4, 17 Nov 2026; case K319). The adapted vertical profile (`level_strategy: 'adapt'`, the default of the engine) clamps the planned altitude of a leg to the ceiling of its segment (`flight_profile.build`): it stays so and it no longer hides. (1) `flight_profile.band_findings(legs, fl, window)` lists the CRUISE legs whose band `[lower, upper]` (ft, numeric) does not contain the level filed (`fl` × 100): a leg that is not a SID, STAR or approach link, has no `data_gap` and no vertical reason of its own (a floor above the level was already an ERREUR through `vertical_errors`), and overlaps the cruise part of the profile (`profile.cruise_nm` = `[end of the climb, start of the descent]` in NM, None when the level is never reached) or follows, on the same airway and the same band, a leg already said (the ordinary descent does not excuse the end of an airway that was already clamped). Consecutive legs of one airway, one band, one zone and one cycle make ONE run. (2) `engine.assess`, for a route that is TYPED (neither `mode: 'calculate'` nor a `_path` of the search): one issue `ERREUR` per run, « Tronçon M858 CANNE–SRN : niveau FL350 hors de la bande FL130–FL165 de la voie, au-dessus du plafond FL165 (cycle 2026-10-01, zone it) » (`Tronçons A1 P1–P2–P3` for several legs, the chain of points cut after 8; « sous le plancher FLxxx » when the band is above the level), at most `MAX_BAND_ISSUES` = 40, then ONE ERREUR sentence with the number of legs and runs left; the legs get the alert (« Niveau FL350 au-dessus du plafond FL165 de la voie (bande FL130–FL165, écart 18500 ft). ») and `compatible: false`; as for any ERREUR, `status` is « ERREURS DÉTECTÉES » and the view's alternative counts the faults, the segments are `state: 'error'` with a `ceiling` (or `floor`) reason and the issue has `kind: 'ceiling'` (or `'floor'`). A route written by the SEARCH (`mode: 'calculate'`, or an alternative re-read from its path) gets ONE issue `REVUE` « Profil adapté aux plafonds : n tronçons de croisière hors de la bande du niveau FL350 (voies …) » and is accepted as before: the search is not changed. (3) The level changes of the profile, `profile.changes` of `flight_profile.build`, copied to the result as `profile_changes` (and `profile_changes_total`) and to the view as `profile_changes` / `profile_changes_total` at the top level (the HEAD route only): `[{ point, end_point, from_ft, to_ft, via, kind, reason, limit }]`, in the order of the route, 60 at most (`flight_profile.MAX_CHANGES`, `plan.MAX_PROFILE_CHANGES`, `view.MAX_CHANGES`). A change is a leg of the cruise part (not a SID / STAR) whose planned altitude moves by more than 1 ft AND passes below the natural altitude of the level filed (a ceiling brings the level down); consecutive legs in the same direction are ONE change: `point` = the start of the first leg, `end_point` = the end of the last, `from_ft` / `to_ft` = the planned altitudes there, `via` = the airways of the run (separated by a space). `kind` = `ceiling` (a descent forced by a ceiling AHEAD: `limit` = `{ via, from, to, ft }`, the leg whose ceiling is the altitude reached), `release` (the climb back after a ceiling: `limit` = the leg whose ceiling is the altitude left), or `profile` (no leg explains it; `limit` None). `reason` is a French sentence of the engine (« Le plafond FL315 de Y112 MOMUK–RAVED impose de descendre de FL350 à FL315. »); the screen composes its own sentence in its language from `kind`, the levels and `limit`. A climb or descent of the ordinary profile (free departure, free arrival, TOC, TOD) is NOT a change: a route inside its bands has `profile_changes: []`. Measured on EDDF → LFMN (the exact route, FL350, 200 kt): 6 ERREUR for 10 legs, 4 changes (UTABA → MOMUK L607 FL350 → FL315; RAVED DCT FL315 → FL350; KELIP → CANNE Z651 FL350 → FL165; SRN → VOG M858 FL165 → FL195).

**Route text: the level changes are written in the route** (SOG-0401 point 4, lot j, 17 Nov 2026; case K321). `primary.route` and the `route` of each alternative of a route written by the SEARCH (`mode: 'calculate'`, or an alternative re-read with its `_path`) carry the changes of `profile_changes` as level groups `POINT/Fnnn` (`nnn` = the new planned level `to_ft` / 100, three digits, F010–F660; the form of the fuel page, `frame/lib/route_text.js`: `NTS/F280`). (1) `engine.assess`, once the profile is built, takes `profile.changes` (kinds `ceiling`, `release` and `profile`), `engine.level_marks` maps each change to the leg where it STARTS (its `point` = the start of that leg; a change at the departure or at a point that starts no leg has no group; two changes at the same point: the last), and `engine.written_route` rewrites the text from the legs exactly as `Nav.format_path` writes it: a point inside an airway portion stays in the text and the airway is repeated after it (`B1 SITET/F195 B1 KEPAD`); a point of the route text is not replaced, the group is added to the token; the departure and the arrival never carry a group. The text is rewritten only if the same rebuild WITHOUT groups gives the text the search wrote (else a REVUE says « Les changements de palier du profil ne sont pas écrits… »), and when `profile.changes_total` exceeds the 60 changes kept, a REVUE says the text carries the first 60. A route with no change has exactly the text it had; a TYPED route (`mode: 'validate'` without a `_path`) keeps its own text. (2) The REVUE « Profil adapté aux plafonds : n tronçons… » ends with « ; la route porte les changements de niveau (POINT/Fnnn). » when the text was rewritten. (3) The reader: `engine.split_level('SITET/F195')` = `('SITET', 195)`; `assess` takes the group off the word before resolving it (so `plan.py` `via` and the connector test see the plain point), keeps it in the text returned (`route` is the text as read, groups included), refuses a level outside F010–F660 and a group on an airway, a procedure or `DCT` (ERREUR, one sentence each), and ignores a group on the first or the last word (the filed level of the whole flight is the level field). The groups are used for ONE thing: `flight_profile.band_findings(legs, fl, window, levels)` judges each cruise leg against the level in force there (`engine.leg_levels`: the level of the last group put on a point at or before the start of the leg, the level of the request before any group), so a route filed FL195 on a ceiling FL195 and back to FL350 after it is not an ERREUR; the vertical profile is still planned from `fl` and `level_strategy` (a group does not move it). (4) The view: the `route` of each alternative carries the groups, `profile_changes` is unchanged (point, end_point, from_ft, to_ft, via, kind, reason, limit); the text through `view.to_request` / `check` round-trips (the same legs, the same `profile_changes`, the same text).

**NAT: the previous system, out of its window** (SOG-0403 R2, 17 Nov 2026; case K320). The user's rule: « il y a toujours une fenêtre nats ouverte avec des routes valides : si ce n'est l'active, c'est la précédente ». `sections._solve`: the routes of the direction whose window contains the passage of the 30°W are the candidates (R1); when there is none and the request gives an hour, `sections._previous_system(nav, g, meta, east, a, hour, eta_of, passage, chains, rejected, when, avoid)` groups the routes of the direction (`direction` of the direction or `both`) that have a chain by window `(from, to)`, returns nothing if one of these windows is over the passage (its routes are refused for another reason: R2 does not apply), takes the system whose `to` is the latest not after the passage (`relation: 'previous'`), else the one whose `from` is the nearest not before it (`'next'`), copies the edges of its routes (body and `nat_link`) with the band open (`lower` 0, `upper` 66 000: the list of levels does not bind), the body with `route: 'DCT'` (the route is written point by point: the points of the message, never « NATx ») and the meta `out_of_window: true`, and adds them to a copy of the graph; the time check of the second stage is skipped for these routes; the `rejected` and `when` entries of the chosen routes are removed. `oriented_chains` recognises such an edge by its meta. The REVUE remark (`engine.assess`) says « Route NAT hors de sa fenêtre : système du … (de … à …), route X suivie point par point, sa liste de niveaux ne lie pas » (the remark and the NAT summary of an alternative look at `nat.out_of_window`, not at the name). `sections.taken` (source `last` | `kept`) gains `window` (`in` | `out`), `levels_binding`, and for `out`: `system` = `{ sense, from, to, relation }` and `out` = `{ at, point, near }` (`nat_layer.time_fields`: the hour at the point `30W` | `entry`, and `near` for a gap of at most 60 minutes). With no hour of departure nothing is judged: the rule of the message of the day (K237) and the refusal of a kept route without hour stand. The free route of the permanent gates only runs when no route of the direction has a chain (R7). `tools/route_matrix.py`: a DCT leg of such a route is `nat_out`, explained.

**Free route: the gates follow the flight; the points on the limit of a FIR** (SOG-0417 (b), 17 Nov 2026; case K329). `free_route.gates(nav, high, level_ft, a, b)` (`a`, `b`: the nodes of the departure and the arrival; without them the old order and the cut at `MAX_GATES`): the candidates are the coordinates of `GATE_WEST` (50°W, 34-58°N) and `GATE_EAST_COORD` (15°W, 38-60°N), the named points of `GATE_EAST` on the levelled high network, and the nodes of `nav.fir_keys` (the points of the base with `lim: 'fir'`, kept by `Nav` at the load of `points`) inside `FIR_BOUNDS` = (20, 62, -35, -8), sorted by id and cut at `MAX_FIR_POINTS` = 120. They are ranked by their distance to the great circle of the flight (`_cross`: asin(|p · n|), n the unit normal of `a × b`), rounded to 0.1 NM, then by id, and on each side of `WEST_LON` (35°W) `MAX_GATES // 2` = 20 gates are kept: the flagged ones first, at most `MAX_FIR_KEPT` = 6 (the nearest), the permanent ones fill the rest (the nearest first): a file that flags points along the great circle of the flight cannot chase the permanent gates. Deterministic for a given flight. The flag is an UNTRUSTED datum (the file of a zone can be written by a pilot or come from a repository): a point outside the bounds is ignored, a forged flag can only add a gate (a DCT link to the network like the others: it can re-route, never break the calculation nor open a closed route: a gate gives no airway and no track). `Nav.fir_keys` is empty for a base compiled before SOG-0417 (a): the gates are then the coordinates and the named points. The flag itself: « Points on the limit of a FIR: the flag `lim: 'fir'` » (SOG-0417 (a)).

**Free route: permanent oceanic gates** (SOG-0396, 17 Nov 2026; case K316). Before: `free_route.ends(nav)` (the two ends of each route of the message: `nav.nat['ends']`) was the only source of entries and exits. Now `free_route.solve(…, skip)` takes `ends(nav)` PLUS `gates(nav, high, level_ft)` (constants `GATE_EAST`, `GATE_WEST`, `MAX_GATES` = 40) MINUS `skip`: (1) `point`: a node of the high network (`nat_layer.main_network`) whose position is in one of the bands `GATE_EAST` (lat 42-62, lon −15.6..−14.4; lat 43-48.5, lon −9.6..−7.9) and that touches a route (not a NAT route) whose band contains the level of the flight (`_levelled`: a point that is only on lower routes is not an entry at this level: measured, BANAL, EKROL, LUPOV of the base qualify and the Biscay points ETIKI, REGHI… did not on the high network of the cycle 2026-10-29); (2) `coordinate`: NAT coordinates `lat/50` for lat 40..58 along 50°W (`GATE_WEST`) and `lat/15` for lat 44..60 along 15°W (`GATE_EAST_COORD`, added after the measure: only 2 or 3 named points qualify on the 15°W line), created in the graph (`nav.add`) and joined by `_links` (the three nearest nodes of the high network within 900 NM, the DCT of simulation that says « simulation »); the coordinates come first (19 + 17), then the named points by id, the total is cut at `MAX_GATES` = 40 (a case can set it to 0 and run without gates: K303). `sections._opposite_ends(nav, g, start, end, info)` gives `skip`: the node keys of the ends of the routes whose direction is opposite to the flight, whose list of levels for that direction contains the level, not stale unless kept, and in force at the estimated passage at that end (departure + great-circle distance / speed; with no hour of departure the set is empty: the validity cannot be checked; the aerodromes' DEP/ARR are never gates). The answer: `sections.free.gates` = `{ entry, exit }` and the same `gates` on the section of the free route (`sections.sections[].gates`) with `track` (an end of a route of the message), `point` or `coordinate`. A graph without `edges` (a fake of a case) has only its ends: the cases of the free route (K294, K302, K303, K304, K306-K308) are unchanged. Measured on the real base, read only, one request each (NOT a pass of the matrix), message of the day alone: EDDF → KBOS 3 313.6 NM in 4.8 s (entry « 49/15 », exit « 48/50 »), LEMD → KBOS 3 024.6 NM in 3.2 s, LFPG → KJFK 3 226.5 NM in 4.0 s: the three origins that had no route with the prototype made of the 50°W coordinates and the named points only (72 → 108 of 144; the 36 left were Europe → America from these three origins: the named points near 15°W are north or on lower routes, and a free route from a gate whose arrival band did not meet the band of the free route explored one state). The matrix at the criterion has NOT been replayed (on the ergonomics session's word). Case K310 now expects the free route by the gates (entry « 49/50 »). `sections.free.diagnostic` = `{ entries, exits, reached (the ids of the entries that the search of the departure side reached), pairs: [{ entry, exit, ok }] (60 at most) }` and the matrix summary counts the free routes that failed by kind (no gate, no entry reached, no pair resolved). Links of a gate (`free_route.solve`): the three nearest nodes of `nat_layer.main_network(nav)` (airways with a ceiling of at least `HIGH_FT` = FL245) and the three nearest nodes of `main_network(nav, LOW_FT)` (ceiling of at least FL285, the floor of the band of the free route), without doubles: a node of an airway that stops at FL255 is reached only from below and never takes a link of the band FL285-FL600 (the same defect as the links of the NAT routes, SOG-0412); keeping the first set keeps the routes that went by a node reached by a DCT from a high airway (K303, K305); `nav._nat_high_band` caches the second set. Case K326.

**Route engine, SOG-0391 (a): the hour of the ocean entry as fields** (17 Nov 2026; case K310). Each `sections.rejected[]` of code `time` has `at` (the hour of the passage of the entry of that track, « YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MMZ »: the real distance of the section of the departure side when the track got as far as its search, the great circle when it was set aside before any search: an ESTIMATE at the speed of the flight, 450 kt when the request gives none), `entry` (the id of the entry point whose passage is meant; of the last option tried when a track has several entries) and `from` / `to` (the window of the message, ISO UTC, None when the message gives none): the other codes carry none of them. `sections.entry` = `{ point, time, nm, speed_kt }`: the hour estimated at the entry of the ocean of the route TAKEN (the end of its first section: the track of the day chosen or the free route created) and the distance it comes from; absent when no route is taken or when the request gives no hour of departure (`sections.depart.time` is then None and the hours are not checked). `nat_layer.time_fields(meta, eta, entry)` builds the fields. `sections.direction` = `east` | `west`: the direction of the flight as the engine took it (the one it set the tracks of the other direction aside for), absent when no NAT track comes into play (the screen no longer guesses it from the aerodromes). A route set aside for the LEVEL (`code` `level`) carries the same `at`, `entry`, `from`, `to` as one set aside for the hour when they are known: `at` is the estimated passage at the entry of that track nearest to the departure (the hour is NOT judged there, only said) and `from` / `to` the window of its message; the screen can then offer « recompute at FLxxx » only if one of these routes would be valid at the hour of its passage (a level of `levels`). Measured on the bench of K237 (KJFK → EGLL, FL370, track A valid 01:30-08:00Z): at 04:30Z the track is taken, `entry` = ENTRA at 07:05Z; at 10:30Z A is set aside for the HOUR (`at` 13:02Z by the great circle) and the free route created says `entry` = ENTRA at 13:05Z (the real section).

**Route engine, SOG-0389 (a): the levels as fields** (16 Nov 2026; case K309). The answer's `sections` (the view carries it as `sections`) has `fl` (the level taken, ft/100) and `fl_basis` (`request` | `default`); each `sections.rejected[]` of code `level` has `levels` (the list of FLs that the track offers in the direction of the flight: `meta.levels_east` / `levels_west`), the other codes have no `levels`. `view.to_request(args)` puts `fl_basis: 'default'` in the request when `args.fl` is absent or null (the level is then the engine's FL180) and `'request'` otherwise; `plan.check_request` accepts `fl_basis` as `request` or `default` only; `plan.plan` keeps it on the Nav (`nav.fl_basis`, reset at each plan) for `sections._solve`. The sections are those of the REQUESTED level (not of a retry at a lower level). For the screen (Features SOG): a flight whose `fl_basis` is `default` was NOT given a level; a flight with rejected tracks of code `level` can show « FL370 not offered: FL350 to FL400 » from `levels` instead of reading the sentence. Measured on the real base, KABQ → EGLL at 21:35Z: FL340, 360, 380, 390, 400 → track V (4 825.1 NM); FL300, 320, 330, 410, 430 → the nine tracks set aside (five by the direction, four by the level), a free route (4 635.2 NM); no level → FL180, `default`, no route.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) d1 ter: the budget of the free route in states** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K308). `Nav.search` is now a wrapper of `_search` that adds the `expanded` of each search to `Nav.states_total`; `free_route.solve` measures the difference (`spent()`; for an engine without the counter, the sum of the `expanded` of the searches seen) against `FREE_STATES` (60 000) before each search and each pair, then against `FREE_S` (3 s) and the bound of the phase: `report['stopped']` is `bound`, `end`, `states` or `time`; a cut with a chain keeps it and `sections._free` says `sections.free.cut` and appends « Route libre coupée par son budget d'états » or « … par le temps de sécurité » (« : elle peut être plus longue que la meilleure ») to `sections.reason`; a cut with no chain gives `NO_STATES` / `NO_TIME`; `sections.free.states` is the number of states used. The extra searches stay bounded by `EXTRA_STATES` (7 000) and by what remains of the budget. A pair is estimated with the REAL America section of its exit once one pair of that exit has been resolved (`tail_real`: the last section of the resolved chain + the lead) instead of the great circle (`bound - tail(o) + tail_est(o)`); a pair that cannot be shorter than the best chain is skipped (`continue`, no resolve).
