Measured on the real base, read only (second-run distance as the reference): KBOS → EHAM « out » 74 tries, 96 697 states, 5.6 s → 15 tries, 23 538 states, 2.5 s, 3 119.6 NM (0.0); KJFK → EHAM 3 397.0 (0.0), 27 807 states; KORD → EHAM 3 872.3 (−27.1); KBOS → EDDF 3 244.6 (0.0), 20 708 states, 2.6 s; EDDF → CYYZ « out » 3 574.1 (0.0), 16 917 states, 2.5 s; EGLL → KJFK 3 047.5 (0.0), 20 790; EIDW → KJFK 2 839.0 (0.0), 9 175, 3.2 s; LEMD → KORD « in » 4 003.7 (0.0), 28 076, 3.8 s; LFPG → KLAX « in » 5 293.8 (−18.3), 7 460, 1.7 s; CYYZ → EDDF « out » 3 605.0 (−29.8), 19 424.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) d1 bis (d): the extra search in states** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K307). `Nav.search(…, max_states=None)`: the bound of the search in states, `(max_states or 100000)`, checked every 128 states with the time bound (a cut search with goals returns what it reached); `sections._europe(…, max_states)`; `free_route.solve`: the first European search has no extra bound, each following one is called with `max_states = EXTRA_STATES` (7 000) and for goals only the entries of `remaining` whose `pack_bound` (the great circle: a lower bound in NM) is below the best `total_nm` (none: the loop ends on the bound). `EXTRA_SEARCH_S` (a time) is gone; `FREE_S` (3 s, the whole free route) stays as a safety net and is still a time. Measured on the real base, read only (the second-run distances as the reference): with 1 500 states, 5 000 and 7 000 — EDDF → CYYZ / KLAX « out » +76.1 NM with 5 000, 0 with 7 000; EGLL → KJFK « out » +72.6 with 3 000, 0 with 5 000; KBOS → EHAM « out » +32.0 NM with every value (the free route stops on `FREE_S` after 51 tries); times with 7 000: EDDF → CYYZ « out » 3.05 s, EGLL → KJFK 2.21, EIDW → CYYZ 3.62, EIDW → KJFK 3.38, LEMD → KBOS « in » 3.65, LEMD → KORD « in » 4.03, LFPG → KLAX « in » 2.76, KBOS → EDDF « out » 4.89, EDDF → KSFO « out » 2.65, KBOS → EHAM « out » 4.48.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) d2 A: the RAD finding on a point rule** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K305). `rad_flight.evaluate`: when the rule's target is a POINT (Annex 2B, point in column 7, no route in columns 4-6, not the departure or the arrival: `point_rule`), the finding adds ONE `{ kind: 'point', point, id, sheet, row }` to `search_exclusions` (the legs that are procedure legs still add their procedure exclusion, the other rules their segments); the engine already reads a `point` entry of `_rad_avoid` as a block of every edge that touches the point (`point_rules`) and as `graph.rad_avoid`. Measured on the real base, read only: LEMD → KBOS 7.4 → 4.2 s, one retry (the retry excludes DELOG and passes by ETIKI), the same route KORUL DCT ETIKI DCT REGHI, 3 267.2 NM; LEMD → CYYZ 3.6 s; LEMD → KORD 3.7 s; LFPG → KLAX 4.0 s.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4) d1: one search to all the entries** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K304). `free_route.solve`: `remaining = list(rank)` (the entries by lower bound); `europe(g2, start, [eff(i) for i in remaining])` is ONE `Nav.search(goals=…, need=NEED)`: it stops as soon as two goals are popped; the pairs (entry reached, exit) are tried in the order of their bound as before (L5 4 d3); when a chain succeeded the loop ends; when none did, and two entries were reached (the search stopped at `need`), the entries that were not reached form the next search; fewer than `NEED` reached means the whole reachable space was seen: the others cannot be reached and the loop ends. `CHUNK` (the packs of five) is gone; `sections.free` carries `searches`. Measured on the real base, read only: EIDW → KJFK « out » 7.4 → 2.1 s (one search 0.16 s, 13 goals, 2 reached, 1 197 states, the same route DOGAL … NICSO; before, a later pack with ONE reachable entry took 4.5 s and 14 376 states), EIDW → CYYZ 7.5 → 2.5 s, EIDW → KLAX 7.5 → 1.4 s, LFPG → KLAX 6.3 → 3.4 s, EDDF → KSFO 5.2 → 2.3 s, KBOS → EDDF « out » 5.0 → 4.1 s, CYYZ → EDDF 3.2 s, EGLL → KJFK « out » 1.9 s.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4): final run of the matrix** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026, 22:21 to 22:30, code of `702c89991`, the whole matrix, same conditions as the first run; detail in `data/local/route_matrix_full4/`). 144 requests: **144 with a route**, 0 none, 0 proposal, 0 error. Seconds: median 1.8, 90th percentile 2.7, **maximum 4.0**, none over 5 s, none over 20 s. Sources: 84 free routes created, 60 NAT tracks (E 24, V 24, Y 7, T 5); no direct of more than 200 NM without a reason outside a FRA zone. **Distances against the second run: 107 identical, 37 shorter (−9.6 to −29.8 NM), 0 longer.** `sections.free.cut`: 0 of the 84 free routes were cut (neither `states` nor `time`); all 84 stopped on the bound; the most states of a free route 38 325 (the budget is 60 000), the most tries 17. The 31 « steps cut » of the summary are the extra searches stopped by their bound of 7 000 states (by design). The estimate of the America section of an exit (not a strict lower bound) cost no route. Route: first run 110 / 34 proposals, second run 144 with 17 over 5 s, final run 144 with the longest at 4.0 s.

**Route engine, lot L5 (4): second run of the matrix, after the fixes** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026, 21:12 to 21:23, code of `bb9c6884b`, the whole matrix once, same conditions as the first run; detail in `data/local/route_matrix_full/`). 144 requests: **144 with a route**, 0 none, 0 proposal, 0 error (first run: 110, 0, 34, 0). Seconds: median 2.2, 90th percentile 6.1, maximum 8.2, **none over 20 s**, no search cut by its bound; **17 over 5 s**: EIDW → CYYZ / KBOS / KJFK / KLAX / KORD / KSFO « out » (6.2 to 8.2 s), LEMD → CYYZ « in » and « out » (7.7, 7.1 s), LEMD → KBOS « in » and « out » (6.2, 6.2 s), LEMD → KORD « in » and « out » (7.8, 6.9 s), LFPG → KLAX « in » and « out » (6.3, 6.1 s), LFPG → KORD « in » (6.2 s), EDDF → KSFO « out » (5.2 s), KBOS → EDDF « out » (5.0 s). Sources: 84 free routes created, 60 NAT tracks (E 24, V 24, Y 7, T 5). No direct of more than 200 NM without a reason outside a FRA zone (64 routes carry one inside a FRA zone, published). Where the time goes (read, not corrected): (1) EIDW « out » (6 requests, one European search of 4.7 to 6.6 s): the free route searches the entries by a pack of five with `need = 2`; from Dublin the entries nearest to the path are not reachable and a search with an unreachable goal explores the whole space (L5 2 bis measured the same on EIDW → KJFK, 4.8 s); (2) LEMD and LFPG (10 requests, no step over 0.5 s): the RAD retries — LEMD → KBOS is 7.4 s: the head route 2.4 s, then two RAD retries of 2.6 and 2.9 s (the first for LE2666 and LF2096, the second for the segments that the first retry brought), each a graph and a sections search; LFPG → KLAX one retry; (3) EDDF → KSFO and KBOS → EDDF « out »: three European searches of 1.1 s (packs) and a graph of 1.4 s. The five defects of the first run are fixed (1, 2, 3, 4: K299, K300, K302, K303) and listed above.

(The table of the second run is replaced by the one of the final run below: the requests are the same.)

**Route engine, lot L5 (4): the matrix of flights** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; note § 13.3; case K298). `tools/route_matrix.py` (standard library only) runs 144 requests on the user's REAL base, read only: six aerodromes of Europe (EIDW, EGLL, LFPG, EHAM, EDDF, LEMD) × six of North America (KJFK, KBOS, KORD, CYYZ, KLAX, KSFO), both directions, two departure times. « In » = the estimated time at the entry of the ocean is the middle of the window of the message of that direction (departure = that time minus the estimated flight to the entry at 450 kt: the hour of a NAT route is read at the passage of the entry); « out » = twelve hours before (Europe → America) or after (America → Europe) the same day. One process, one request after the other, low priority, packs of 12 (a pause and a `STOP` file between two packs, resumable), no network (connections refused in the process), results cache off, graph caches emptied before each request (the worst case: the bound of 20 s must hold with a graph to build), the load of the context measured apart (0.90 s and 0.84 s, once per day of flight). The detail of each request, route text included, goes to `data/local/route_matrix/detail.jsonl` (never committed) and one line per request to `progress.txt`; `--summary` writes `table.md` (the table below) and the summary. Nothing was corrected during the pass.

Run of 16 Nov 2026, 20:04 to 20:15, 11 minutes, on the cycle 2026-10-29 and the NAT messages CZQX1117/26 (east, 17 Nov 01:00-08:00) and EGGX1116/26 (west, 16 Nov 11:30-19:00):

- 144 requests: **110 with a route**, 0 « none », **34 « proposal »** (the answer carries no route, only partial proposals), 0 in error.
- Seconds (requests with a route): median 1.4, 90th percentile 2.1, maximum 2.3, none over 5 s. The 34 proposals take 10.6 to 14.5 s: **34 over 5 s, none over 20 s**; 6 requests hit the bound of a search (all « budget »).
- Sources of the 110 routes: 62 free routes created (`random`), 48 NAT tracks (E 18, V 21, Y 5, T 4). Proofs: 62 `c+r+c`, 48 `c+p+c`.
- Directs over 200 NM: none without a reason outside a FRA zone; 50 routes carry a DCT of more than 200 NM inside a FRA zone, where the direct is published (not a defect; counted).
- Longest route: LEMD → KSFO 7 513 NM, 2.0 s.

**Defects, listed and not corrected** (to be ordered by the ergonomics session):

1. **Every request that has EHAM as an end is a « proposal » (24 of the 34).** Cause found and checked: the base has EHAM at an elevation of **−10 ft** and the vertical profile is judged infeasible (« Profil vertical impossible avec les altitudes AD… », an ERREUR of the control): the route itself is found (EHAM → KJFK at 13:30: 3 408 NM, NATE), and with the elevation set to 0 the same request has no error. The engine then retries at FL350, 330, 310… and every attempt gives the same error, 10 to 14.5 s in all. Any aerodrome below sea level is concerned.
2. **Eastbound « out » to EDDF and LFPG: 8 « proposals » (`no_chain`, 12-13.5 s)**: all the east tracks are set aside by the hour (said), the free route is created for the other destinations but its three tries fail for these two; EGLL, EIDW and LEMD answer. Not examined further.
3. **LEMD → KBOS westbound, 2 « proposals »**: a free route is created (REGHI 48/10 50/20 50/30 49/40 48/50 NICSO) and the control reports RAD LF2096 (Annex 2B, line 4948, « COMPULSORY FOR TFC ») as an ERREUR: the RAD rule is not examined; it is the same one as in L5 2 bis.
4. **The answer's `sections` and `timing.sections` describe the LAST attempt** (after the retries at lower levels: FL290 for a request at FL370), not the requested level: the reason of the first failure is lost, and the screen would say « FL270 non proposé vers l'est » for a flight at FL370. The reason code of the table is the one of the last attempt for the same reason (`no_valid_track` for the EHAM westbound, which in fact have a route and an ERREUR).
5. **The retries at lower levels** (up to six attempts) are what takes the 10-14 s of a request that has no route: when the first attempt failed for a reason that is not the level (a control error, a track set aside by the hour), the next ones cannot do better.

(The table of the first run, 144 rows, is replaced by the one of the second run below: the requests are the same.)

**Route engine, lot L5 (3): `timing`, the bounds, the default** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; note § 13.4; case K296). `plan.plan` returns `timing` (and `view.to_view` carries it): `{ context_s, tracks_s (nav.nat['seconds']), graph_s (nav.graph_seconds: 0 when the graph is kept), skeleton_s (sections.solve: the time outside the searches), sections: sections_info['steps'], total_s }`; a step: `{ name, seconds, bound_s, hit, track?, shared?, goals?, reached? }` (`sections._step`, `sections._bound`: min(SEARCH_CAP_S, DEADLINE − now)). `engine.GRAPH_MIN_S` (module variable, 5.0 for an oceanic flight, 0 otherwise, posted by every plan): `Nav.routing_graph` refuses to BUILD (not to read the kept graph) when `perf_counter() + GRAPH_MIN_S > DEADLINE`. `plan.plan`: `nav.sections = bool(request['sections'])` when the request has the key, else `crossing and mode == 'calculate'`.

**Route engine, lot L5 (1 bis): one search to all the entries** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; note § 13; case K295). `Nav.search(g, start, end, …, goals=[nodes])` → `{ goal: { path, cost, band } }` (`end` ignored, no waypoints; `last_search.goals` when all are reached, `truncated` when the budget cut: the partial answer is returned, no exception). `sections._europe(nav, g, start, entries, info)` makes it; `info['europe'] = { goals, reached, expanded, seconds, truncated }`; `sections._resolve(…, first=found[entry])` starts its first section from it (its entry in `sections[]` carries `shared: true` and the states / seconds of the one search). The skeleton bound of a track is now the REAL European cost + the lower bound of the America side + the cost of the track; the hour at the entry is checked with the real distance before anything is resolved; a track whose entry is not reached is set aside (`network`). Measured: see the note § 13 « Done (L5 1 bis) ».

**Route engine, lot L5 (2 bis): the free route by the entries that are reached** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K297). `free_route.solve(nav, g, start, end, ratio, resolve, europe)`: the links of ALL the ends are added once on a copy of `g`; the entries are ranked by their best great-circle bound and searched by packs of `CHUNK = 5` through `europe(g2, start, pack)` (= `sections._europe(..., 'europe_free', NEED)`, `Nav.search(goals=pack, need=2)`: stops at two reached; `info['europe_free']` says goals / reached / states / seconds); the pairs (entry reached, exit) are ranked by the REAL European cost + the estimate of the rest, at most `TRIES = 3`; `resolve(g2, start, end, entry, exit, legs, first)` = `sections._resolve(..., created=True, first=...)`; an exit whose America section failed is dead for the next pairs. `Nav.search` gains `need`. Measured: see the note § 13.2 « Done (L5 2 bis) ».

**Route engine, lot L5 (2): the random route** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; note § 13.2; case K294). `free_route.interior(a, b)` → `[(lat, lon, id)]` (one point per multiple of 10° of longitude strictly between the two ends, from a to b, the latitude of the great circle rounded to the whole degree, `id` = « 54/30 »); `free_route.ends(nav)` = the first and the last node of each route of the message (`nav.nat['ends']`, set by `nat_layer.add`); `free_route.solve(nav, g, start, end, ratio, resolve)` → `(chain, None)` or `(None, reason)`: the pairs (entry on the departure side, exit on the other) by lower bound, `TRIES = 3`, simulation links added on a copy of `g` (`nav._nat_high` caches `nat_layer.main_network`), the points created with `nav.add` (the same node for the same id and place), edges `route: DCT, origin: random, lower 28500, upper 60000, _cost = nm × ratio`. `sections.solve` calls `sections._free` when no track is valid or no chain succeeded; `sections._resolve(..., created=True)` names the sections by the side of the ocean (`europe`, `amérique`: the flight can go either way) and the middle one `route libre` (`proof: created`). **The answer**: `sections.source: 'random'`, `sections.free: { source: 'random', label: 'route libre, créée', ok, entry, exit, points: [id], nm }` or `ok: false, reason: « aucun point d'entrée océanique connu : aucune route du jour n'en donne »`; the legs of the view: `origin: 'random'`; the review: « Route libre océanique, créée : … Non publiée : autorisation océanique non vérifiée ». **Measured, real base, read only**: EIDW → KJFK at 03:00: 1.9 s, `DOGAL 54/20 53/30 51/40 48/50 NICSO`, 2839.0 NM (before: no route at 15 s); KJFK → EIDW: 1.9 s, 3001.3 NM; EGLL → KDEN: 3.2 s, 4663.7 NM; EIDW → KJFK at 12:00: still the track E, 3022.7 NM, 2.0 s.

**Route engine, lot L5 (1): the validity of a NAT track for this flight** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; note § 13.1; case K293). `nat_layer.check(meta, fl, east, eta)` → `None` or `(code, sentence)`: `direction` (the track goes the other way), `level` (the requested level is not in the list of the track for that direction), `time` (the estimated time at the entry is not inside `from`–`to` of the message). The metadata of each track (`nav.nat['meta'][id]`) now carries `levels_east`, `levels_west` and `levels` (their union, used by `Nav.level`: an edge of a track is refused at a level that is not in its list, whatever the whole search or a section). `plan.depart_of(request)` gives the departure (the hour of the request, UTC; a date with no hour gives none: `(None, 'none')`, the hour of the messages is NOT checked and `sections.depart` says `{ time: null, basis: 'none', text: « heure de départ non donnée : validité horaire des routes NAT non vérifiée » }`; `nat_layer.check(meta, fl, east, eta)`: the track is valid when the estimated time at the entry is inside the window of the message, a stale message (`meta.stale`) is not checked, a route with no level list and no window is valid at any level and hour) and `nav.depart`. `sections.solve` sets aside, before anything is searched, the tracks that fail on direction or level, then those that fail on the time estimated at the entry (departure + great-circle distance / speed), and again, for each track resolved, on the REAL distance of the European section; a track that is neither a candidate nor set aside says `network` (its entry or its exit is not linked to the high network at this level). **The answer** (`sections` of the view): `rejected: [{ track, code: direction | level | time | network, text }]`, `depart: { time, basis: request | now }`, and, when no track is valid, `chosen: null` and `reason` (« Aucune route NAT valable pour ce vol : la recherche entière suit. »); `Nav.nat_rejected` keeps the whole search that follows from taking a track that was set aside (so, with the option, a request at 03:00 never uses a track valid from 11:30). **Measured, real base, read only**: EIDW → KJFK at 12:00: gate E, the same route; at 03:00 (the message is valid from 11:30 to 19:00): tracks D and E « non valide à 0341Z (valide de 1130Z à 1900Z) », the tracks R to Z « sens contraire », no gate, the whole search follows WITHOUT a track and reaches its bound (15 s, no route): that is the case that the random route of § 13.2 (the next point) is for. Not the default of a request without the option (the whole search of the engine without `sections` does not check the hour yet: it will with (3), when `sections` is the default of a flight across the ocean).

**Route engine, lot L2b (3): the option `sections`, gates, skeleton, lazy resolution** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; design note § 3–4; case K290). **Request**: `sections: true` (boolean; `route/compute` through `view.EXTRA`, `plan.check_request`). `Nav.generate` calls `sections.solve(nav, g, start, end)` before the whole search when the option is set, there is no mandatory point and no `require_fra`; `None` means « not applicable » and the whole search follows as without the option. **Applicable** when the departure is west of 35°W and the arrival east of 15°W or the reverse (the same test as `plan.crosses_ocean`) and when `g` (already filtered by the flight level) carries at least one track of the day in the direction of the flight (`oriented_chains`: a track going one way is kept if it is the way of the flight; a both-ways track is taken in the way of the flight; a chain that branches is dropped). **Skeleton**: for each track, `lower bound = ratio × (distance(departure, entry) + distance(exit, arrival)) + the exact cost of the published legs` (`ratio` = the smallest cost per NM of the graph, so the bound never exceeds a real cost); the tracks are resolved in the order of their bound and the loop stops when the bound of the next exceeds the best cost found. **Sections**: each one is `Nav.search` — the European section and the American section are searches (`proof: computed`), the track is a search on the sub-graph made of its legs only (`proof: published`; same bands, same costs, one path); `search` has a new argument `gate`: an end that is a GATE (the middle of the flight) asks no arrival level and no arrival descent; the final band `(low, high)` and the `cost` of a search are in `last_search` (`band`, `cost`) and the next section starts with `initial_band` (a section that starts at a gate is in cruise: no free climb; one that ends at a gate has no free descent). **Answer**: `plan.sections` and `view.sections`: `{ candidates: [{ track, lower_bound }], tried: [{ track, cost }], chosen, cost, sections: [{ name, from, to, nm, cost, expanded, seconds, proof }] }` (null without the option). **Measured on the real base, cold, FL370**: EIDW → KBOS 9.3 s → 2.0 s, EIDW → KJFK 8.3 s → 2.1 s, SAME routes and distances (2 850.1 / 3 022.7 NM; the chain: NAT E, NICSO N260B–N271B, ALLEX Q822…); LFOE → KTPA 2.2 → 3.3 s and EGLL → KDEN 2.2 → 2.6 s, the same routes; LFPO → KLAX (no route on this base) unchanged. For EIDW → KBOS the five tracks of the day were all resolved (the bound of each, 2 626–2 661 NM, is far below the best real cost, 2 880 NM): 260, 5 and 16 states for the three sections of the best chain (15 ms, 0 ms, 8 ms): the cost of the flight is the context (1 s) and the graph (1 s), not the searches. **What the option is not**: the best flight BY a track, not the best of all flights (a flight with no track, or one that a track makes longer, is not in its space); that is why it is a request option and not the default until the user has seen it on EIDW → KBOS and KJFK.


**FAA published routes, lot L2b (2): the route of each route, the conditions, the fixed sentence** (SOG-0354, 16 Nov 2026; case K286). `faa_pfr.read(blob)` now returns for each preferred route: `segments` — the TRACÉ read in `PFR_SEG.csv` joined by (origin, destination, type, number): `[{ seq, value, type: FIX | NAVAID | AIRWAY | STAR | DP | RADIAL | FRD, country? }]` in the order of `seq` (the file gives them out of order); `conditions` — `{ hoursUtc: [from, to] (HHMM), jet, prop, single, rnav1 }` (flags only when the aircraft text says so), the raw texts remaining in `aircraft`, `alt`, `dir`, `area`, `hours` (the engine must treat a non-empty text that it does not interpret as a restriction); and, for a coded departure route, `navEqp` (1, 2 or 3: the meaning is the FAA's, `CDR DATA LAYOUT.pdf`), `coordReq` and `play`. **Measured on the 29 Oct 2026 subscription**: 13 197 routes, **12 631 with their route** (NAR NON-COMMON 617 of 617, H, L, HPD, SHD, SLD all, TEC 5 634 of 5 657, **NAR COMMON only 222 of 765**: the 543 others are the pair (coastal fix, inland fix) and the designator `N503B`… with NO route between them: the file does not carry the North American Route itself for them — the points between are not in the NASR); the segment values are 12 signs at most, 120 of them with a dash (`IMEBA-STAR`), 74 181 rows in 1.1 s with the rest. **Conditions seen in the file**: `HOURS` 22 formats (`1100-0300` for 799 routes), `AIRCRAFT` 276 texts (`RNAV 1 - DME/DME/IRU OR GPS`, `TURBOJETS`, `JETS ONLY`, `SINGLE ENGINE ONLY`, `PROPS ONLY`), `ALT_DESCRIP` 235 (`10000`, `PQ60`, `J100M80`, `110-170 INCL`), `ROUTE_DIR_DESCRIP` 115 (`WESTBOUND`, `EASTBOUND`, `WATER`, `DEEP OCEAN`, `IAH EAST FLOW`…), `SPECIAL_AREA_DESCRIP` 1 755 (lists of airports); the North American Routes carry no hours, no aircraft and no altitude, only a direction. **Security touch-ups** (Sécurisation, 15:4x): `_rows` turns `csv.Error` (a field over 131 072 signs), `zipfile.BadZipFile`, `zlib.error`, `EOFError`, `RuntimeError` (an encrypted zip), `UnicodeError` and `OSError` into ONE `ValueError('<file> : fichier illisible')` WITHOUT the text of the exception (it could quote a content of the file); `faa_nasr.inner_entries(blob)` reads the number of entries from the end-of-directory record and `faa_nasr.rows` refuses more than `MAX_INNER_ENTRIES` = 500 BEFORE `zipfile` parses the directory (the inner zip has 114 entries today); true for `convert` too. Still a reader: no file written, no network, no verb.

**Route engine: the bounds of a flight across the Atlantic** (SOG-0354, decision of the ergonomics session 16:06 on the question (a) of the lot L2b 1). **Criterion**, decided BEFORE the search (no route is known yet): `plan.crosses_ocean(dep, arr)` = one end west of 35°W and the other east of 15°W (the very test of `nat_layer.hint`); the direct distance alone is not used (a flight of Europe of 1 500 NM keeps its 5 s); a CHECK (`mode: validate`) keeps its bounds. **Bounds** (`plan.budgets(request, ocean)`): `head_s` 12 (5 otherwise; first 10, widened to 12 because the route of EIDW is found at 8.5–9 s), `search_s` 12 (6; `engine.SEARCH_CAP_S`, put back to 6 at the end of the computation), `total_s` 20 (15). Inside the 20 s: the RAD retries of the head route only before the head bound (each retry rebuilds a graph, ~4 s, and a search); when the head route is refused, an attempt (graph + search, 6.5 s) is not begun if it could not end before the end of the alternatives window (16 s), and the fallback proposals (`recovery.propose`, 0.5–3 s) keep the last seconds; the alternatives of a GOOD head route keep their own 8 s (`alternatives_budget`). **For the screen** (Features SOG): the response of `route/compute` carries `plan.budget` and, in the view, **`budget: { ocean: bool, head_s, search_s, total_s }`** (`ocean: true` = « cherche plus longtemps : jusqu'à 20 s »; the field is null never: it is always present). **Measured** (real base, FL370, the form of the screen; before → after): EIDW → KBOS 15.0 s, a fallback proposal → 16.0 s, a ROUTE of 2 850 NM; EIDW → KJFK 15.0 s proposal → 15.7 s, a ROUTE of 3 023 NM (the NAT E track and NAR N260B–N271B); LFOE → KTPA 5.6 → 4.6–5.8 s, 4 734 NM, 3 alternatives, unchanged; EGLL → KDEN 6.1 → 4.4–4.9 s, 4 578 NM, 3 alternatives, unchanged; LFPO → KLAX (no route on this base) 8.5 → 17 s, still a proposal. **Margin**: the route of the two EIDW flights is found at ≈ 8.5–9 s into the 10 s head bound (context 1.9 s + search 6–7 s): four runs in four were routes; one run under load made a proposal: a margin of 1 to 1.5 s, which a head bound of 12 s would widen. Case K289 (the real-flight proof is timing-sensitive and skipped without the base).

**Launcher jobs: the pure reads run beside the job that holds the place** (SOG-0384, 16 Nov 2026). `Jobs.start` accepts one job at a time (`409 busy`); `READ_ONLY` only says which jobs do not replace the « last work ». The Data screen asks `base/status`, `base/cycle` and (since the libraries line of SOG-0376) `pdf/status` at the opening: the second start was refused and the screen said « état non lu ». **Measured, real base, read only**: `base/status` (84 zones, default zones, personal declared or not) 0.44 s before `1e8ac887f` and 0.69 s after (0.54 / 0.53 s with six personal sources): `datedBy` per zone costs nothing visible; `pdf/status` 0.15 s cold (the versions read in a child process); reproduced: `pdf/status` then `base/status` → the second `409 busy`; `base/status` then `pdf/status` → `409 busy`; `base/cycle` then `base/status` → `409 busy`. **Fix**: `regen_jobs.PARALLEL` = `base/status`, `base/cycle`, `base/coverage`, `base/last`, `aircraft/registry`, `route/fra-points`, `ground/state`, `pdf/status`: `Jobs.start` starts them as jobs of their own (their id, a thread, `view(id)` and `cancel(id)` find them in `Jobs.reads`, the last 32) WITHOUT taking `cur` WHEN a job holds the place (a read alone behaves as before: it is the current job and the last-job file keeps it): they run beside a job that writes or a read that holds the place, are never written to `tools/.regen_last.json` nor to the persistent journal, have no minimal gap, and `running()` (the job that holds the place, used by the upload to say busy) never counts them. A suspension of the starts (`held`: the update of the suite) still refuses them. **Cap** (Sécurisation): `Jobs.MAX_READS` = 6 reads RUNNING at once (the screen starts three at the opening); the seventh gets the usual refusal `409 busy` (with the job that holds the place, `reads`, `max_reads`), and starts again once one has ended (the last 32 are kept to be polled by their id). A job that writes (build, update, pickup, fetch…) keeps the place and a second writer is still refused. **For the page** (Réglages): nothing to change; a read that fails is an `error` job with its sentence; no read is refused 409 because of another job any more (so no retry loop is needed). Case K288.

**Route engine, lot L2b (1) of SOG-0354: the corridor, measured, and what the search really costs** (16 Nov 2026). **The corridor was built and not kept.** `Nav.search` first searched, for a flight of 1 200 NM or more without a mandatory point, inside a corridor around the great circle (a point enters if distance(start, P) + distance(P, end) ≤ 1.15 × the direct distance, then 1.35), and kept the result only when it was PROVEN optimal (a path that leaves the corridor is at least `1.15 × direct` long, so it costs at least `ratio × 1.15 × direct`, `ratio` being the smallest cost per NM of the graph: a corridor result at most that is not beatable), else the whole search as before. Measured on the real base: **no gain**. LFOE → KTPA: the corridor at 1.15 found the route but could not prove it (cost 4 788 against a bound of 4 511), at 1.35 it was proved, with the SAME 13 426 states expanded as the whole search (the A* with an admissible heuristic already stays inside the corridor): 0.7 s lost to two extra searches. EIDW → KJFK: the corridor at 1.15 expanded 6 656 states in 3.4 s, exactly the rate of the whole search, and found nothing in its bound. So the number of EDGES per state is not what costs. **What costs** (cProfile of the one search, graph of 19 306 nodes, 77 639 edges, average degree 4, hubs up to 60): about 27 edges relaxed per expanded state (the search favours hubs) and, above all, **≈ 38 non-dominated altitude labels per (node, milestone) bucket**: 6.3 million turns of the dominance test for 162 000 relaxations (1.0 s of 3.5 s), the loop itself 1.5 s, `edge_band` 0.5 s. A cache of the two altitude bands per edge per search gave nothing (each edge is relaxed about twice). Quantizing the bands (a coarser label, 500 or 1 000 ft, inward) was tried: it LOSES the routes (LFOE → KTPA and EGLL → KDEN no longer found): not kept. **Kept**: the dominance test as a plain loop with its thresholds computed once (the same test, the same expanded states, the same routes; ≈ 30 % faster: 15 063 states in 9.6 s → 6.9 s). **The finding for the user's complaint** (« toujours des tracés directs entre Dublin et JFK »): the route EXISTS and the search finds it — EIDW → KJFK and EIDW → KBOS with the bounds raised (a search of 12 s, a head route of 10 s): `EIDW BAML1H BAMLI DCT DOGAL NATE 54/50 DCT NICSO N260B-N271B ALLEX Q82…`, in 16 s with the alternatives — by the NAT E track and a NORTH AMERICAN ROUTE (N260B–N271B) already in the base; the fallback proposal is only the bound of the head search (5 s, 6 s per search: SOG-0371) cutting it at 6 000 states of the 15 000 it needs. A real route in the cold 8 s of the note § 8 needs a search that expands far fewer labels: the skeleton (lot L2b 3), or a policy decision on the bounds (10 s for the head of a long flight), not a micro-optimisation. Table after L2b (1) (FL370, the form of the screen; before L2a → after L2a → now): EIDW → KBOS 15.0 → 8.1 → 7.4 s; EIDW → KJFK 15.0 → 7.9 → 7.9 s; LFPO → KLAX 15.6 → 9.7 → 8.5 s; LFOE → KTPA 7.8 → 6.7 → 5.6 s; EGLL → KDEN 7.1 → 6.9 → 6.1 s.

**Route engine, lot L2a of SOG-0354 (16 Nov 2026): the profile, and what was cut.** Profile (cProfile, EIDW → KJFK, real base): of 25 s under the profiler, **20 s were `recovery.propose`** — the line `g[u]=[… not any(engine.block_hit(block, a, b, route) for block in blocks)]` ran 41 589 477 times (every edge of the graph times every block of the RAD), 11.8 s in the generator and 4.7 s in `block_hit`. The blocks are now indexed ONCE by `recovery.usable_graph` (a set of the points of the `point` blocks, a set of `(via, start, end)` triples of the others): the same graph as the old formula, a case compares them on a random graph and counts that `block_hit` is no longer called (K287, red before: no such function). Second, `engine.date_in(text)` (a regular expression and a date) was called 623 604 times per computation, 1.2 s: memoized (`lru_cache`, a pure function, immutable dates). **What was NOT cut, and why**: the per-state cost of the ocean search (≈ 500 µs per state, 1 500 states/s) is not an expensive state but a wide one: ≈ 50 edges relaxed per expanded state (300 000 geodesic heuristics, 1.2 s; 2.1 million label-dominance checks, 0.6 s; the loop itself 1.3 s); caching the two altitude bands of an edge per search was tried and gave nothing measurable (reverted). A real gain on that search needs fewer edges per state (the corridor of the design note § 5.2: the graph admits only points close to the gates), which is lot L2b, not a micro-optimisation. The searches that fail (3 to 4 s truncated, « not found ») still spend their bound: they are the head route, bounded at 5 s. **The table of § 5 again** (FL370, the form of the screen; before → after; this workstation, no other load): EIDW → KBOS 15.0 → 8.1 s (recovery 9.9 → 3.0 s), EIDW → KJFK 15.0 → 7.9 s (10.0 → 2.7 s), LFPO → KLAX 15.6 → 9.7 s (9.0 → 2.8 s, the second graph build 4.5 s), LFOE → KTPA 7.8 → 6.7 s, EGLL → KDEN 7.1 → 6.9 s; the same routes (4 734 NM and 4 578 NM). The remaining 2.7–3.0 s of `recovery.propose` are the nodes pool scans (137 000 nodes read for each of the up-to-14 aims of each leg) and the assessments of the proposals: not touched.

**Route engine, lots L0 and L1 of SOG-0354 (design note `docs/technique/V3_ROUTE_GATES.md`)** — facts, 16 Nov 2026, on the user's real base, read only (results cache off, nothing written). **L0, the table of § 5** (FL370, the form of the screen: alternatives and proposals asked; times of this workstation; « states » = states expanded by `Nav.search`):

| Flight | Total | Context + NAT layer | Graph build | Searches (big ones) | Fallback proposal (`recovery.propose`) | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EIDW → KBOS | 15.0 s | 0.9 s + 0.15 s | 0.8 s | 438 calls, 4.2 s: 3.26 s / 5 376 states TRUNCATED, 0.94 s / 2 816 states TRUNCATED, 436 tiny (126 not found, 0.00 s together) | 9.9 s | proposal |
| EIDW → KJFK | 15.0 s | — | 0.8 s | 2 calls, 4.4 s: 4.05 s / 6 144 states TRUNCATED, 0.35 s / 1 664 states TRUNCATED | 10.0 s | proposal |
| LFOE → KTPA | 7.8 s | — | 1.0 s (the second build, for the alternatives, is cached) | 6 calls, 3.1 s: 0.5–0.7 s each, 13 400–18 600 states | — | route, 4 734 NM, 3 alternatives |
| EGLL → KDEN | 7.1 s | — | 1.2 s | 13 calls, 2.4 s: 0.4–0.5 s each, 9 500–14 600 states | — | route, 4 578 NM, 3 alternatives |
| LFPO → KLAX | 15.6 s | — | 1.1 s + 4.2 s (the second build) | 3 calls, 0.9 s: 0.84 s / 21 617 states, not found | 9.0 s | proposal |

Reading of the table: (1) where the 15 s go on the failing ocean flights: the search itself is cut by its bound at 3–4 s having expanded only 5 000–6 000 states (1 500 states/s, twenty times slower than on the European flights, 30 000 states/s: the ocean graph is the expensive one: the NAT edges and the free connectors), then **`recovery.propose` takes 9–10 s, 60–65 % of the answer**; (2) the graph build is 0.8–1.2 s, except 4.2 s for the second build of LFPO → KLAX; (3) the number of calls to `Nav.search` is not the cost (438 calls in EIDW → KBOS, 4.2 s of which 4.2 s are the two big ones); (4) **LFPO → KLAX is no longer a route** (the design note's table, 14 Nov, said a plausible chain in 2.4 s): it is a budget at 15 s — bisected: the code of 14 Nov (`329847a19`) gives « no route found » in 39.6 s, so it is the base (the US zone is the 1 Oct build, the Canadian zone only the DAH), not the engine of this week. No behaviour was changed by L0. **L1, the FAA published routes** (`tools/faa_pfr.py`, case K286): the full subscription of 29 Oct 2026 carries `PFR_BASE.csv` (13 197 rows: NAR 1 382, TEC 5 657, H 4 443, L 916, SHD 703, HPD 73, SLD 23), `PFR_SEG.csv` (74 181 rows, a segment per row with its type FIX / NAVAID / AIRWAY / STAR / DP), `PFR_RMT_FMT.csv` (1.6 MB) and `CDR.csv` (41 916 coded departure routes: code, origin, destination, departure fix, route string, centres, length). **What `NAR_TYPE` really holds**: `COMMON` (765 rows, 762 designators `N101B`… `N5xxB`) = a pair (one coastal fix, 47 distinct; one inland fix, 52) with the DESIGNATOR of the North American Route and a direction (`ROUTE_DIR_DESCRIP`), no route string; `NON-COMMON` (617 rows) = from an inland fix to an aerodrome (`DESTINATION`), with the ROUTE STRING (`LARIE Q220 RIFLE Q167 ZIZZI KNUKK ATR LAFLN SPISY2`); the origin / destination countries of the NAR rows: US→US 461, CA→US 375, CA→CA 297, US→CA 249 (so the Canadian side is the same file). **Positions**: every point named by the preferred and North American routes (1 902 five-letter points and fixes) AND all the 275 points of the NAR segments have a position in `FIX_BASE.csv` / `NAV_BASE.csv`; `FIX_BASE` carries **359 Canadian fixes** (COUNTRY_CODE CA, of 70 060) and `NAV_BASE` 30 Canadian navaids; the NASR knows only 6 Canadian airways (`AWY_SEG_ALT`, COUNTRY_CODE CA): the Canadian part of a route string that uses a Canadian airway has points located but no airway of its own. So the « named, not located » level of the note § 6 is **not needed for the points**: the points named by the published routes are located; what is missing is the Canadian airway between them. The reader is **not wired to the engine** (no verb, no output file: where the published sections live is for L2).

**`aircraft/registry`: the published file as a fallback** (SOG-0341 c): `origin` (`'local'` | `'published'`) on the answer; with no export of its own the verb reads `data/registry/fr_immat.json` through `registry_fr.load_published` (the same `rebuild` as the publication: five text cells, bounded, cleaned, k-anonymity recounted, source and date not trusted). Case K285.

**`base/status`: `datedBy` and `deposited` per zone** (SOG-0368, Réglages asked on b740841c9): `zones[zone]` carries `datedBy: 'file'` and `deposited: 'AAAA-MM-JJ'` when the zone is built on a hand deposit (`zones/<cc>/avians_<cc>_enr_dep_AAAAMMJJ.geojson`, the most recent date; a deposit has no edition, the date is the date of the deposit), judged on the files the build read (`rec.inputs`; the files present when there is no record); both absent for a zone built on an edition of the site. Same names as the `steps.download` of `base/cycle`. Case K283.

**Optional libraries: one file of bounds, a script that installs, a verb that says** (SOG-0376, 16 Nov 2026; opinion of Sécurisation before the code, all conditions kept). The page SAYS, the script INSTALLS: the launcher never runs pip (a launcher or a page that installed code taken from the network would have a power it does not have). **`pdf/status`** `{}` (read only) now answers `{ ok, packages: [{ name, version, minimum, maximum, state }], groups: [{ id: 'pdf' | 'ocr', packages: [name], ready }], ready: { text, vectors, ocr }, script, python, error? }`: `script` is the NAME of the script of the platform (`installer_bibliotheques.cmd` on Windows, `.sh` elsewhere; it replaces `command`, which no longer exists), `error: 'bounds'` says that the bounds file was refused (nothing is then ready and no PDF is read). **For the page** (Réglages): the line of the Data screen is composed from `packages` (a package `old` or `missing`: its name, `version`, `minimum`) and `script`: « Lecture des PDF : prête » when `ready.text` and `ready.vectors`, else the package, the version read and the minimum, then « lancez <script>, dans le dossier de la suite, puis relancez le lanceur »; the group `ocr` is separate and not required. **The bounds file** `tools/requirements-optional.txt` (two groups; `# group: pdf`, `# group: ocr`), read by `tools/optional_libs.py` for `pdf_guard` AND for the script: each line `name>=min[,<max]`, the name in the closed list, anything else refuses the whole file. **The script** (`tools/install_libs.py`): `--user` (omitted in a venv), never elevated (refused as administrator or root), `python [-P] -m pip install --only-binary :all: --index-url https://pypi.org/simple --isolated --no-input` (`--allow-pip-config` lifts `--isolated`, never by default), an empty working folder, no `PYTHON*` variable, no `-I`, no `--require-hashes` (it would freeze the security fixes: this replaces the exact pin advised on 14 Nov for builder workstations), only the libraries `missing` or `old` are given to pip, one `new` (above its maximum) is left as it is (a version is never lowered), the question o/N defaults to no, `--dry-run`, the versions read again at the end. The failures say a FIXED sentence (`pdf_guard.HOW_TO`: « À faire : lancer <script>, dans le dossier de la suite, puis relancer le lanceur. ») built from names of the closed list and from no version read. Cases K268, K284.

**PDF libraries as an option: `pdf/status` and the bounded read** (Q-0053, 14 Nov 2026; opinion of Sécurisation). pypdf (texts) and pdfplumber (vectorisation, with pdfminer.six and Pillow) are an OPTION of the workstation; `pypdfium2` stays with the page. **The launcher never imports them** (`tools/pdf_guard.py`): `pdf/status` `{}` (read only) answers `{ ok, packages: [{ name, version, minimum, maximum, state: 'ok' | 'missing' | 'old' | 'new' }], ready: { text, vectors }, command, python }`, the versions being read by `importlib.metadata` in an isolated child (`python -I -c`, nothing of the libraries runs in the launcher) and compared as NUMBERS (pdfminer.six is a date). **Minima**: pypdf ≥ 6.21.4, < 7 (three denial-of-service advisories before); pdfplumber ≥ 0.11.10, < 0.12 (it asks for pdfminer.six 20260107, no advisory); **Pillow ≥ 13.0.0, < 14** (39 advisories before, all fixed; the workstation has 12.3.0, and pdfplumber asks only ≥ 12.2.0, so the resolver will not raise it by itself: the user must look first for what already uses Pillow); `command` = `python -m pip install "pypdf>=6.21.4,<7" "pdfplumber>=0.11.10,<0.12" "Pillow>=13.0.0,<14"` — **the launcher installs nothing**. For a builder workstation Sécurisation advises an exact pin with `--require-hashes` (hashes computed by `pip hash` on each wheel, never copied from a message). **The read** (`pdf_guard.read_text(data, path, first, count)`, for the readers of deposited PDFs; no verb hands it to the page): the PDF is under `data/local/upload/` or `data/sia/zones/<zone>/` only (real path, no link, name by pattern, ≤ 1 GiB); a library that is missing or too old refuses the read, said with the command; the child is `python -I tools/pdf_child.py` (the only place pypdf is imported), working folder = an EMPTY temporary folder, environment cleaned (SYSTEMROOT only), a hard timeout (120 s) that stops the WHOLE tree (a Job object on Windows, a process group elsewhere), memory limited (2 GiB: the Job object / RLIMIT_AS), at most 400 pages, its answer a single bounded file (4 MB) that is an UNTRUSTED input (JSON without a non-finite number, two closed fields, control characters removed, text bounded per page). **Review notes taken** (Sécurisation, 14 Nov): on Windows the Job object also allows ONE process (`ActiveProcessLimit`; two in a virtual environment, whose interpreter starts the base one as a child) and, if no Job can be made, the read is REFUSED (`failed`), never made without a memory limit or a tree stop; `pdf/status` keeps its answer 5 s (a page that calls it in a loop starts no process each time). Errors: `PdfError.reason` = `missing` | `version` | `path` | `timeout` | `failed` | `output`. Not done: the vectorisation read (pdfplumber) waits for SOG-0336 (beta 1); no reader uses the read yet. Case K268.

**Route computation: the budget of the alternatives** (SOG-0296, the cause; the user: « il faut traiter la root cause »). The screen's form asked for `alternatives: true` and waited for the whole search: LFOE → KDEN took 25 s with them, 3.0 s cold (0.7 s warm) without, the SAME route of head, and the search found no other alternative. **Request field `alternatives_budget`** (optional, a number from 1 to 25 seconds; screen's form and engine's form): the time the search of the ALTERNATIVES may hold the station, counted from the end of the primary calculation; **default 8 s** (it was the 25 s of the whole plan). A plan that is REFUSED keeps the 25 s (its proposals are its recourse). The message says which budget was reached (« Budget de recherche des alternatives atteint (8 s). Les propositions déjà contrôlées sont conservées… »). Measured on the real base, LFOE → KDEN FL370: default 13.7 s (3 s the primary, 8 s, then the search under way finishes), `alternatives_budget` 3: 3.9 s, 25: 25.7 s. **How the screen should use it** (decided by the ergonomics session: ONE answer then a SECOND request, no partial result of one job): first the request WITHOUT `alternatives` (the primary, 1 to 3 s), shown at once with « variantes en recherche… »; then the same request with `alternatives: true` (each has its own key in the kept results); a changed input cancels the second. Case K267.

**Route computation: the budgets are counted in STATES, the clock is only a safety** (SOG-0524, 19 Nov 2026, the relay: « les budgets se comptent en états »; it supersedes the seconds of SOG-0371 (5 s / 15 s / 8 s) and SOG-0354 L2b (12 s / 20 s) above, and removes SOG-0475's extension of the deadline and the rule « a graph is not begun when less than 5 s remain »). Measured: under a loaded workstation the 5 s of the head route and the 3 s of the free route cut the search, so the same request gave « CALCUL IMPOSSIBLE (budget de recherche atteint) » for a route that exists (ESSA → LEMG), or lost its alternatives. Now every phase has a CEILING on the counter of states of the Nav (`nav.states_total`, the sum of the states of all its searches): the head route and its RAD retries `HEAD_STATES` 250 000, the whole calculation `TOTAL_STATES` 750 000, a flight that crosses the Atlantic in calculation 600 000 / 1 000 000, the alternatives `alternatives_budget` (1 to 25, the SECONDS of the old contract) x `STATES_PER_S` 50 000 (400 000 by default); one search keeps its own 100 000 states; the free route keeps its `FREE_STATES` 60 000. Measured at rest: 450 to 13 500 states for the head route of the reference flights, 27 000 with the alternatives. The same request gives the same answer whatever the load. The clock stays as a WIDE safety (`GUARD_S` 120 s for the calculation, 120 s for one search, `FREE_S` 30 s for the free route): when it passes, the calculation is **interrupted** — `engine.Interrupted` (it is NOT a `ValueError`: no `except ValueError` takes it for a missing route), and the answer says it: the head route is returned with a REVUE « Calcul interrompu : le poste est trop lent » when only a later phase was cut (RAD retry, alternatives, fallback), and `primary.status` is `CALCUL INTERROMPU` with `error` « Calcul interrompu : le poste est trop lent (plus de 120 s sans conclure). Ce n'est pas une absence de route… » when nothing was computed; never « CALCUL IMPOSSIBLE », never « proposition ». The answer carries `budget: { ocean, head_states, total_states, guard_s, s0 }` (instead of `head_s`, `search_s`, `total_s`), `states` (the states this calculation used) and `interrupted`; the screen's view has `status: 'interrupted'` (rather than `none`) when it was interrupted and nothing is computed. `timing` (seconds) stays as information. Case K402 plays a slow clock (K278, K289, K296, K348 and others were redone: they asserted seconds).

**Route computation: the levels of a trip in one call — `route/levels`** (SOG-0529, for the level « Auto » of Carburant; case K406). `launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'route', verb: 'levels', args })`; `args` = the arguments of `route/compute` in the form of the screen (`adep`, `adest`, `date?`, `speed_kt?`, `climb_fpm?`, `descent_fpm?`, `terminal_speed_kt?`, `enable_fra?`, `via?`, `rwyDep?`, `rwyArr?`…) **without `fl` and without `route`**, plus `levels`: 1 to 12 integers from 10 to 660 (flight levels, **in the order of the caller's preferences**) and `first_valid?`: boolean (stop at the first valid level). Closed: anything else is refused (400). Each level is the HEAD route alone (no alternatives, no proposals) with the states budget of the head route (`HEAD_STATES`, 250 000, set at each level); measured: 30 to 43 states and 0.3 to 0.6 s for a level where a route is found, 57 to 1 344 states and 1 to 3 s for a level with none (LFBY → LFST), 700 to 13 500 states and 2 to 6 s for long flights; the context and the navigation base are shared between the levels, the routing graph is built once per level. Result `{ ok: true, levels: [{ fl, ok, valid, status, distance_nm, route, connector, reason, errors, states, interrupted }], first_valid: <fl or null>, stopped: 'first-valid' | 'end' | 'cancelled' | 'time', asked }` or `{ ok: false, reason: 'no-rad' | 'invalid' | 'failed', message }`. `ok`: a route was found; **`valid`: a route, a check WITHOUT error, no limit of the RAD at the connector exceeded and not interrupted** — a direct connector that the IFPS will refuse (ROUTE134 / ROUTE135) is never a valid route; `connector`: `'procedure'` (every end that has published procedures flies one), `'direct'` (an end has published procedures but none can be flown at this level: it is flown by a DCT connector of simulation), `'none'` (no end has published procedures: the direct connector is the only one); `reason`: ONE line for the screen (« route valide », « raccord direct de 64,2 NM à l'arrivée au-delà de la limite de 0 NM du RAD (LF_LIM_AD) : à examiner, jamais une route valide », « Aucune route trouvée sur le réseau disponible à ce niveau », the first error of the check); `errors`: at most three, 160 signs; `states`: the states used. The choice of the level stays in Carburant (`levelAuto`: the level that burns least among the valid ones; preferring a level where a PUBLISHED arrival can be flown to one with `connector: 'direct'`, and saying so); this call only says which levels have a valid route. **Bounded**: the job is a read-only SIDE job like `route/compute` (it starts beside `ground/fetch`; its view does not replace the last job that wrote); the cancellation of the job (`job.cancelled`, also a released job) and the time of the WHOLE call (`LEVELS_BUDGET_S`, 300 s) are tested BEFORE each level — over either, the levels already computed are given back with `stopped: 'cancelled'` or `'time'` and `asked` says how many were asked (a caller compares it with `levels.length`); the levels are computed in series under the solver lock.

**Route computation: three verdicts, never confused — `verdict`, `violations`, `unverifiable`, and the reasons as CODES** (SOG-0532 (3), SOG-0531; cases K481, K483). Every answer of `route/compute` (`plan.compute`: `primary`; and each `alternatives[]` of the view) carries **`verdict`**: `'violates'` (a rule that the engine KNOWS is broken: the issues of level `ERREUR`), `'unverifiable'` (nothing known is broken, but something could not be verified: it is NAMED in `unverifiable`) or `'conform'` (nothing is broken and nothing is left unverified); **`violations`**: `[{ code, values, text }]`, one per `ERREUR`; **`unverifiable`**: `[{ code, values, text }]`. A reason that counts for the verdict is `issues[]: { level, text, code?, values? }`: `code` is stable and `values` are the numbers and names (the screen composes the sentence in the language of the user; `text` stays the French sentence of the journals; an unknown code shows `text`). Codes in use: `rad_rule` {id, sheet, row} and `rad_block` {id, sheet, row} (a rule of the RAD that is decided and broken), `connector_limit` {ad, end, nm, limit_nm, rule, point?, no_network?} (the direct connector of an aerodrome is longer than the limit of the RAD), `space_dct_limit` (a direct too long in a London space), `no_conforming_route` {violations} (a review: no route avoids the known rules, the best is returned and says so); `other` for an error without a code yet. **A known rule that is broken is an ERROR, for a calculated route as for a typed one — there is no more demotion to a review** (before: « REVUE », the route stayed valid and « Auto » could keep it). When the retries with the exclusion find nothing conforming, the best route is still returned and the view marks it: `alternatives[0].faulty: true`, `alternatives[0].verdict: 'violates'`, **`status: 'faulty'`** (a new value beside `computed`, `proposal`, `none`, `interrupted`); a `faulty` route is never counted valid (`route/levels`: `valid: false`; each level row also carries `verdict`). The status text of the engine is `ERREURS DÉTECTÉES` when the verdict is `violates` (« CONTRÔLE PARTIEL — À VÉRIFIER » is only for a route that breaks nothing known). **For the screen** (Features SOG): show the verdict apart from the fixed wording; before « Copier pour Eurocontrol », say « N règles connues enfreintes — Eurocontrol refusera ce plan » when `violations` is not empty.

**Route computation: the rules of the RAD that were not verified, said without drowning — `not_verified`** (SOG-0532 lot 2; case K486). The answer (`primary.not_verified` and the root `not_verified` of the view, `alternatives[].notVerified`) is a list, **one entry per State** (the two first letters of the reference of the rule; at most 40 States, the State with the most rules first): `{ state, flown, spaces_only, items }`. **`flown`** = the number of rules of the annex 2B that the engine could NOT decide and **whose TARGET the route flies** (a point or a run of an airway of the route); a rule where the route appears only in a condition is not counted. **`spaces_only`** = the number of rules aimed at a space that the base does not know (no contour): **a count, never a list**. **`items`** (the list to open, at most 40 per State): `{ id, sheet, row, reason, reasons, target? }` — `target` (SOG-0532 lot 2 bis) is the element of the ROUTE that the rule aims at (the first leg it touches, « UL607 KOK–BUPAL »; absent when the rule has no leg), the sentence of the screen is « {target} — {reason} ({id}) »; the reference of the rule (the text of the rule is read in the workbook by its sheet and its row) and the reason as a CODE: `zone_state` (the schedule depends on the state of a zone), `schedule_airac` (by month of AIRAC), `schedule_dst` (with a summer time in brackets), `schedule_days` (by day of the week), `schedule_season` (summer, winter, holidays), `schedule_list` (a numbered list of schedules), `level_along_points` (a level along a run of points), `aircraft` (a type or a navigation equipment), `space_unread` (an aerodrome or a passage « in » a space), `unread` (no known form). Each State also has ONE line in `unverifiable` (and one review in `issues`): the code **`rad_unread`** with the values `{ state, flown, spaces_only }` (the screen composes the sentence); **the long review « Règles RAD NON appliquées » (SOG-0501 (c)) is not in the answer any more** (`rad.unknown_spaces` stays in the data of `rad`, for the pages that read it). The requests are bounded at the door (SOG-0532, Sécurisation): `speed_kt` and `terminal_speed_kt` from 50 to 1 000 kt, `climb_fpm` and `descent_fpm` from 100 to 10 000 ft/min (a speed of 0 divided by zero in the search). **`allow_levels`** (the line of the table above): absent, the level of the search is LOCKED (SOG-0532 (1)) and the profile is the `adapt` of the model, which writes a step only upward when a floor requires it; `true`: the legacy search (lanes below the level at cost × 3), a yoyo it writes is the error `level_yoyo`. The `values` of the codes carry names of the base and of the RAD (`rule`, `sheet`, `id`, `point`) that are not cut: the screen puts them with `esc` or `textContent`, never as raw HTML.

**Route computation: the values of the reasons that the sentences of the screen need** (SOG-0532 lot 2 bis; case K489, K484). `rad_block` and `rad_rule` carry **`target`**: the element of the ROUTE that triggered the rule, as a bounded string — a leg « UL607 KOK–BUPAL » (the airway and its two points; « KOK–BUPAL » for a direct) or a point; for `rad_block` the first leg of the route that the rule touches; for `rad_rule` the first leg it touches, and for a rule without any leg (« compulsory for the traffic via Y », the target X being absent from the route) the point Y of its condition that the route flies; **absent when none is known** (the screen then writes the sentence without it). `space_dct_limit` carries `{ from_point, to_point, nm, limit_nm, space }` (a direct too long in a London space: its two points, its length, the limit of the RAD in NM, the area). `level_yoyo` carries **`{ down_point, down_fl, up_point, up_fl, filed_fl }`**: TWO points, the point and the level of the descent (the LOWEST level written before the climb), then the point and the level of the climb. All the strings are cut (points at 40 signs, a leg label at 20 + 20 + 20); the pairs `(level, text)` of `space_dct.check` are unchanged unless `coded` is asked (the engine asks).

**The prefixes of the RAD that are NOT States** (for `{zone}` of `stale_cycle` and `{state}` of `not_verified` / `rad_unread`; measured on the annex 2B of the cycle of 29 Oct 2026, 45 prefixes). `state` is the first two letters of the reference of the rule; the workbook has no legend, the meaning below is read from the column of the control centres (ACC) of the rules. **`YX`** (276 rules): the **Maastricht Upper Area Control Centre** (the ACC `EDYYUAC`), a centre that covers parts of Belgium, the Netherlands, Luxembourg and Germany; its rules are not those of a State. **`NM`** (1 rule): the **Network Manager of EUROCONTROL** (rules that the NM publishes for the whole network). **`RE`** (2 rules): rules of a **cross-border group of centres** (the ACC columns name centres of France, Switzerland, Germany and Italy), not of one State. **`DS`** (2 rules, the references begin `DSEC`): rules **shared by several centres** (Germany, Maastricht, Denmark, Sweden); the workbook does not say more. **`GC`** (8 rules): the **Canary Islands**, a part of Spain (the ICAO prefix `GC`). **`EB`** covers **Belgium and Luxembourg** (the Luxembourg airspace is served by Belgian rules: the column of States reads `EB,EL`). A reference of **four letters before the digits** (`EBEG1004`, `LOED1028`) is a **bilateral rule** between two States (here Belgium and the United Kingdom, Austria and Germany): `state` keeps the FIRST one. Every other prefix of the list is the ICAO prefix of a State (`BI` Iceland, `ED` Germany, `EG` United Kingdom, `LE` Spain, `LF` France, `LI` Italy, `LS` Switzerland, `UK` Ukraine…). The screen puts a fixed label for `YX`, `NM`, `RE`, `DS` and shows the code in capitals for any prefix it does not know.

**Route computation: two more forms of the RAD are read** (SOG-0532 lot 3a; cases K490, K491). Nothing changes in the shape of the answer: rules that were `not_verified` with the reason `level_along_points` or `schedule_dst` are now DECIDED when the cell can be read with certainty, and a rule that is broken is a `rad_rule` error like any other (`target`, values). **L1, a level along a run of points** (`ABV FL245 AT (A UL607 B)`, `BLW FL105 AT (A DCT B DCT C)`, also behind `RFL`; the lists of points `(A, B)` were already read): the run is read like the group of a `VIA` (`rad_via.runs`: the first and the last point of each passage of the run on the flight); a run that the flight does not fly makes the atom false; otherwise the profile of the flight is read at EVERY point of the run, and the atom is decided only when the profile is on ONE side of the level along the whole run (the source does not say if the level is read at the first point, at one of them or at all of them: the conclusion is given only when it is the same in the three readings). A point exactly on the limit, an unknown altitude, a level that crosses the limit along the run, a run flown in the other direction, a point that the base does not know, more than 20 passages, a cell longer than `MAX_CONDITION`: the rule stays `not_verified`, never « conforming ». **H3, a schedule with a summer time in brackets** (`13:30-20:00 (12:30-19:00)`): if the two windows say the same for the passage, that is the answer (as before); if they differ, the window in brackets is read as the SUMMER window (the workbook has no legend; measured on the two cycles, 342 of the 574 simple cells are exactly the winter window one hour earlier in UTC, which is the local hour of a rule) and it is used only when it is EXACTLY the winter window one hour earlier (the start and the end, midnight included) AND the whole passage is of one season, more than 24 hours from a change of time (the summer time of the European Union: the last Sunday of March 01:00 UTC to the last Sunday of October 01:00 UTC). Any other cell stays `not_verified` (reason `schedule_dst`). The numbered lists (`1. … 2. …`), the months of AIRAC, the days of the week and the zone states are NOT read by this lot (lot 3b and the rest stay `not_verified`, named).

**Route computation: two more forms of the schedule are read** (SOG-0532 lot 3b; cases K492, K493). Same shape of the answer; same rule of doubt as lot 3a (never « conforming » on a cell that may be read two ways). **H2, a period in AIRAC cycles** (`AIRAC NOV - FIRST AIRAC MAR 05:00-17:00`, `LAST AIRAC MAR - AIRAC OCT H24`; the schedule after the period is `H24`, a window, or a window with a summer time in brackets): the workbook does not say what `AIRAC NOV` designates (the cycle that starts in November, the one in force…) nor whether the period stops at the start or at the end of the last cycle, so only the months that are on one side WHATEVER the answer are decided: inside, the months strictly between the month of the start and the month of the end; outside, the months from two months after the end to two months before the start (a cycle lasts 28 days); the month of the start, the month of the end and their two neighbours are `not_verified` (reason `schedule_airac`). The period AND the schedule must hold: a schedule that is false at the passage makes the rule not active whatever the month; a passage that changes month is decided only by that. Days of the week, holidays and any other text after the period are not read. **H6, a numbered list of schedules** (`1. H24 / 2. 04:00-22:00`, `1. A. … / 1. B. …`; one schedule per alternative of the condition): the workbook gives the pairing between the schedules and the alternatives only by position, and the sub-lists and the exceptions do not line up one to one, so NO pairing is guessed: the list is decided only when EVERY schedule of the list says the same for the passage (all active, or all not active: the answer then depends on no pairing). A list where one schedule says yes and another no, or with a schedule that is not read, stays `not_verified` (reason `schedule_list`). A cell longer than `MAX_CONDITION`, or a list of more than 60 items, is not read.

**The table of cruising levels of France: a file read once per cycle** (SOG-0530 lot A; case K494). The launcher reads the page **ENR 1.7** of the SIA eAIP (the table 1.7.5) with the page ENR 5.1, in the same update of the data (the source `enr51`; same host `www.sia.aviation-civile.gouv.fr`, the address computed from the AIRAC cycle by `aero_fetch.enr51_url(cycle, '1.7')`, never taken from a page; one page of 178 KB, `ENR17_PAGE_BYTES` = 4 MB at most) and writes **`data/route/levels_fr.json`** (NOT in `zones/fr`: `shared/sia_base.js` reads every `enr51_*` file there as a zone document). One file, which says its cycle; the file of the cycle in force is not asked again. Content: `{ schema: 1, zone: 'fr', cycle, source, tracks: { lower: ['000-179', '180-359'], upper: ['090-269', '270-089'] }, lower: { ifr: [a, b], vfr: [a, b] }, upper: [a, b] }` where `a` and `b` are the lists of flight levels (hundreds of feet, ascending) of the two halves of the magnetic track in the order of `tracks`: **below FL195** the IFR levels are 10, 30 … 190 for the tracks 000-179° and 20, 40 … 180 for 180-359°, the VFR levels 35, 55 … 195 and 45, 65 … 185; **in the UTA (195 < FL < 660)** the cut is 090-269° (210, 230 … 390, then 410, 450, 490) / 270-089° (200, 220 … 400, then 430, 470, 510). The page ends with « etc. » and the reader does NOT extend it: a level above the last one is outside the table. The parse is strict (`tools/sia_enr17.py`): every level is checked against the column of feet of the page (level × 100) and against its series; a page that does not have exactly this layout writes NOTHING and the update says « ENR 1.7 : … (mise en page changée ?) », so the engine has no table and says so (never a guessed one). The VFR lines are given, but **the height above which the VFR rule applies is NOT in ENR 1.7 nor in ENR 1.3** (they only say that VFR is forbidden above FL195): the engine is IFR only, and no VFR warning is made until that height is read from a source (question Q-0134 on ENR 1.1). The ENR 1.3 page adds that the table applies « unless otherwise stated » in ENR 3 (per airway segment), in the RAD (per direct) or in an ATC clearance, and the parity of the FRA cells: they are not read by this lot.




**Route computation: what the check could NOT verify is named — `unverifiable`** (SOG-0532 (6) and the Belgian cycle; case K483). The codes of the list `unverifiable` (and `issues[].unverifiable: true`): `connector_unknown` {ad, end, nm, reason, rule} — the direct connector of an aerodrome whose limit is not known (`reason`: `no_limit` = no limit read for the aerodrome, `conditional` = a limit with a level or a time that is not decided, `no_procedure_read` = a limit of 0 NM and no SID/STAR read for the aerodrome, `no_network_no_limit` = a fallback direct of an aerodrome without network and no limit read); `stale_cycle` {zone, cycle, current, routes} — a State whose data used are of a cycle 28 days or more before the flight (« non vérifiable pour cet État », the airways named); `network_gap` {from_point, to_point, nm} — the direct that the engine invents between two parts of the network (SOG-0476). A direct connector whose limit is unknown is **never counted conforming**: the verdict is `unverifiable`. **Belgium** (measured, 20 Nov 2026): the base of the workstation has NO Belgian airways for the cycle of 29 Oct (`be/2026-10-29/airways.json` is empty, like `points.json`): the airways come only from the older build of 1 Oct (145 segments, the X-Plane / OpenAIP era), because **no source of Belgian en-route data is read** (readers exist for `fr` SIA, `gb` NATS, `es` ENAIRE, `de` DFS, `ie`); it is not a reading defect but a missing source (a source to choose: the user's word if a host is added). Until then every route through Belgium is `unverifiable` with `stale_cycle` {zone: `be`, cycle: `2026-10-01`}. **Level written** (SOG-0532 (2)): nothing in the engine raises the level — the profile comes back to `fl × 100`; the FL350 of the refused plan was the level of the calculation (the plan was filed at FL330, a matter for the screen); a level group is written only when it changes the level (`engine.level_marks(…, filed)`: no more « POINT/F330 » for a flight already at FL330).

**Route computation: the level is CONSTANT by default — the lanes out of their extent are excluded, no more yoyo** (SOG-0532 (1)(5); cases K482, K484, K273, K358). Before, a lane whose ceiling was under the cruise level cost × 3 and could carry a flight at FL330 (`L9` F075–F245, `L607` F065–F195 in the refused plan): the vertical profile was « adapted », level groups were written down then up (`REMBA/F240 … ROPUV/F350`), and Eurocontrol answered PROF195, PROF407 and PROF326. Now, unless the caller asks for steps (`allow_levels: true`), **the search excludes** every airway segment whose extent does not contain the cruise level, **except in the natural climb and descent**, and there only if the natural profile stays inside the band of the segment over its whole length: at the far end of the segment the altitude (the distance from the departure, with a 15 % detour, at the climb rate) plus a margin of 1 000 ft is under the ceiling, and at its start the altitude is above the floor (the descent is symmetrical, towards the arrival). Two parts of the network that the excluded lanes no longer join are joined by the ONE direct of SOG-0476 (`no_network: gap`, said in a review: « la base n'a pas de liaison … »); a flight whose only way is a low lane therefore gets a route with that direct, not the low lane. **A descent followed by a climb written in the route (`level_yoyo`, PROF326) is an ERROR** with its code and values `{ point, down_fl, up_fl, filed_fl }`, for a typed route and for a written one; with `allow_levels: true` the legacy search (lanes below the level at cost × 3, steps written) stays, and a yoyo that it writes is that error (the view puts an alternative without it first). Measured on the 14 reference flights of the workstation: the own-check band errors went from 6 flights to 0; two flights (EGLL → LPPT FL350, EGLL → BIKF FL350) have no route at their level any more — the answer says so, a lower level is proposed.

**Route computation: the results are kept** (SOG-0296, refused by the user on 13 Nov 2026: « oui, il faut garder un cache, qui périme uniquement au changement d'airac, ou s'il passe par les nats »). The kept context above saves the READING (2 to 6 s); it does not save the SEARCH. Measured on the real base, LFOE → KDEN in the screen's form (`alternatives`, `proposals`), context reused: 26 s EVERY time, the same request again as long. `tools/route_engine/results_cache.py` keeps the RESULT of a request of the verb `route/compute` (the screen's form or the engine's) under `data/local/route-cache/<sha256 of the request>.json` (the workstation's local folder: never synchronised or published). **The key is every field of the request** (a request that differs by one field is another request). **What expires it**: the signature of the files it depends on (`plan.fingerprint` — `sia/base`, `proc`, `sia/zones/nat`, the RAD and FRA books: an AIRAC change, a rebuilt base, a procedure read again, an imported book, a new NAT message — plus a stamp of the engine's code); and, for a result that goes through the NAT, **the end of the UTC day of the calculation** (`until`). Nothing else: no fixed duration. A result that is not `ok` is never kept. **What the answer carries**, optional, nothing removed: `cache: { hit: true | false, computedAt: ISO UTC, until: ISO UTC | null }` — the screen says « du cache, calculé à {computedAt} » when `hit` is true (the page's wording and its « Recalculer » belong to Features SOG; the verb has no « fresh » field yet: the request is closed). **Bounds**: 64 files (the least recently used leave), 4 MB each, atomic write; a file found there is an untrusted input (size, JSON, version, key, signature, deadline checked, else ignored and the search redone; **audit 205**: a file nested deeper than 32 levels or that holds a non-finite number — `NaN`, `Infinity`, `1e999` — is refused, a `RecursionError` while reading is a file that cannot be read, and the pruning touches only the names this folder writes, 64 lowercase hex digits + `.json` or `.tmp`). Measured: 27.9 s, then the same request 0.0 s; another level 25.4 s. Not done: the search itself stays at ~26 s (the 25 s budget of the alternatives, `plan()` `_deadline`) the first time; the screen's « 34 s » is that plus the launcher's own start. Case K266.

**Route computation: the airway preference, the proposals** (SOG-0161). **`fra_preference`** (request field, −0.3 to +0.5): negative values favour the published airways (the cost of a free-route link is multiplied by 1 − preference: 1.10 for −0.10); the **default is −0.10** (it was +0.10 until 10 Nov, which could exhaust the search budget on long routes: DECISIONS). **`proposals: true`** (request field of the screen's form, optional): when no route is computed, the engine's fallback proposals (`recovery.proposals`: direct, pieces, regional FRA) come back as **`alternatives` with `proposal: true` and `incomplete: true`**, each with its `control` (`controlledPct` / `fallbackPct`…) and its segments' `control`; ordered by controlled share then distance, three at most. Without `proposals` an impossible plan has no alternative, as before.

**X-Plane points as a local layer of the page** (SOG-0153 e, 10 Nov 2026, decision of the user: « x-plane : tous les utilisateurs en auront, on garde »). `AeroDb.create({ …, xplane: { points: async () => [[id, lat, lon], …] } })` — **the frame gives the reader** (it owns the page's handle on the X-Plane folder, `FileAccess` `__xplane`, and `AeroSources.xplane('earth_fix.dat' / 'earth_nav.dat', text)`): when no published zone and no FRA book has an identifier, `coords` / `coordsAll` / `whereIs` answer from it with `{ kind: 'point', zone: 'xplane', local: true, source: 'xplane' }` (`distanceNm` with `near`). In memory only (the folder is read once, 30 s before a failed read is asked again), **never written, never exported**: the data is Navigraph / Jeppesen's (« Copyright (c) 2025 Navigraph, Datasource Jeppesen » on the first line of `earth_awy.dat`, `earth_fix.dat`, `earth_nav.dat`). **The existing source `xplane` has never written anything into `data/`**: the launcher's build refuses it (`xplane-in-page`, the folder is a handle held by the page) and the survey of the user's `sia/base` (every zone: airspaces, points, navaids, airways, aerodromes, vrp, meta) found no record marked `xplane` or `openair`. **To wire (V3 Frame, `frame/app.js`)**: `xplane: { points }` reading the two files of the granted folder (nothing without a grant); K135 gives the shape.

**`base/coverage`: the coverage table by country** (SOG-0174, read-only job, `tools/regen/coverage.py`). `{}` → `{ ok, zones: { <zone>: { cycle, parserVersion, counts: { aerodromes, navaids, points, airways, airspaces, fir, vrp }, procedures, ground, sources: { declared: [ids], present: [ids] }, has: { points, navaids, airways, airspaces, fir, procedures, ground } } } }`, one entry per zone of `sia/base/index.json`. All of it is **read from the files, never guessed**: the counts of the last published cycle of the zone (`fir`: the airspaces of type FIR or UIR), `procedures` and `ground` the aerodromes of the zone (by the ICAO prefixes of the index) whose `proc/manual` or `proc/<cycle>/index.json` file, or whose `proc/ground/<ICAO>.json`, is held, `declared` the sources the suite declares for the zone and `present` those with a file in `data/sia/`; the **licence and the credit of each source are the catalogue's** (`frame/aerodb/catalogue.js`, `lic`, `pub`, `credit`), which the page adds. 71 zones in about 1 s; no write, it does not replace the last job that wrote. **On the user's data of 10 Nov**: 56 zones have navaids, **5 have airways** (fr, gb, de, es, ie), **points: fr, gb, es** (ie and de: partly), **FIR: fr only** (parser 26 brings 32 countries), procedures: 9 aerodromes held, ground: 1; **Sweden, Denmark and the United States have neither point nor airway** (before LFV and the NASR are read: the next « Mettre à jour »). K137.

**A source « lue sur ce poste seulement » in `personal` is set aside, not a refusal** (SOG-0162 refused at the acceptance, 10 Nov 2026). `base/build`, `base/update` and `base/status` accept `xplane` (and `eurocontrol`, `eurocontrol_rad`) in `personal`: **the source is set aside and said** in the result as **`excluded: [{ source, why: 'local-only', message: '<source> : lu sur ce poste seulement' }]`** (build: also in its journal; update: from its build; status: from the arguments), **and everything else is done** (the other sources of the zone are built, the zones are given their state). The refusal `xplane-in-page` of `base/build` is gone: nothing of Navigraph / Jeppesen or of EUROCONTROL is ever built into the committed base (X-Plane is read by the page, in memory: the local layer above). A page that kept showing « La construction avec X-Plane se fait dans la page… » can drop that sentence: show `excluded[].message` as a note. K138 (the real headless browser: red before).

**`page-error` of a zone, what it is** (the seven zones cz, pl, ro, rs, si, sk, ua of 9 Nov 16:53): the build page reported an error for that zone and the launcher recorded `failed: { error: 'page-error' }` in `data/local/build/<zone>.json`; **those records have no message** (built before the message was kept: K46), and **all seven are stamped to the same second**, i.e. one pass of the page failing together (the pass of « Tout recalculer » whose 404 on `data/local/sources/` was fixed in `K117`). `failed` is a buildable state: «Mettre à jour» takes them again.

**`base/pickup` with `build: true`: « Prendre le fichier » chains the build** (SOG-0180, the verbs' side). `base/pickup { zones, dry?, zips?, personal?, build?: boolean }`: with **`build: true` and `dry: false`**, once the files are taken the job runs `base/update` **without download** (`fetch: false`) on **the zones whose file it took**, with the same `personal` as the box (a local-only source in it is set aside, see above), and gives its answer as **`update: { ok, reason?, message?, built, failed, excluded, after, nothing?, notBuildable?, waiting? }`** in the result. A build that fails makes the pickup `ok: false, reason: 'build-failed'` and **the files stay taken** (the page says « <zone> : prise faite, construction en échec : <raison> · Réessayer », and « Réessayer » is `base/update` on that zone). A dry run (the plan, the default) never builds; without `build` nothing changes. **What the page needs of the verbs for its one gesture**, all there now: `base/update` (download what changed → state → build the zones that need it, one result with `built`, `failed`, `after` per zone, `waiting` for the zones that wait for a file of Downloads with their `reasons`), `base/pickup { build: true }` for the button on the line of a zone that waits for a file (K139), `excluded` for the sources read on this workstation only (K138). K139.

**ILS, localizers, GLS and MLS of the AIXM 5.1 files** (SOG-0176, parser version 28). `AeroSources.aixm5` (NATS, ENAIRE) no longer skips the `Navaid` of type `ILS`, `ILS_DME`, `LOC`, `LOC_DME`, `GLS`, `MLS`: it writes, **in `navaids.json`, the record of the SIA's form** — **`{ kind: 'ILS', id, name, freq, ad, rwy, bearing, dme?, lat, lon }`** (`id`: the ident of the localizer, `IND`; `name`: « EGLL 27R » (ICAO of the aerodrome and designator of the runway direction); `freq`: the frequency of the localizer in MHz with two decimals, `'110.30'`; `ad`: the ICAO of the aerodrome; `rwy`: the designator of the runway direction; `bearing`: the axis in degrees (the true bearing of the runway direction, the magnetic one when there is none, rounded; the SIA gives its own); `dme: true` when the navaid has a DME; `lat`, `lon`: the position of the localizer) — **the Map and `procedures.js` read them unchanged**. **Two optional additions, absent from the SIA's records**: **`loc: true`** for a localizer alone (no glide path), **`sys: 'GLS' | 'MLS'`** for those systems (no frequency, `freq: ''`). `ad`, `rwy` and `bearing` are absent when the runway direction cannot be followed (an ILS the file does not link to a runway). The localizer and the DME of an ILS are not listed twice; a marker beacon is still not a navaid of the base. **The zones built before parser 28 have none of these: they are `parser-old` until « Mettre à jour »**; France keeps the SIA's 200. The glide path is not read (its frequency is the localizer's pair). K140.
