**`base/coverage`: the contract for the screen, in writing** (answer to Réglages, 11 Nov 2026; the screen reads everything as an untrusted input).
1. **Call**: `launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'base', verb: 'coverage', args: {} })`. **No argument is accepted** (`{ zones: … }` or anything else is a 400): it answers **for every zone of `sia/base/index.json`**, so the page filters, sorts and picks its columns itself.
2. **Answer** (`job.result`): `{ ok: true, zones: { <zone>: Zone } }`. Keys of `zones`: the zone identifiers of the index, **`^[a-z0-9_-]{2,12}$`**, at most about 80 (71 today; there is no other cap). `Zone` = **`cycle`** (string `YYYY-MM-DD`, or `null`: the index gives none), **`parserVersion`** (integer, or `null`: the `meta.json` is unreadable; 21, 23 and 28 are seen today), **`counts`** (integers ≥ 0, never `null`: `aerodromes`, `navaids`, `points`, `airways`, `airspaces`, `vrp`, `fir`; largest seen: 20 782 aerodromes, 9 230 airspaces), **`procedures`** and **`ground`** (integers ≥ 0: the aerodromes of the zone whose procedures, or whose ground layer, are held; largest seen 2 257 and 1), **`sources`** = `{ declared: [id…], present: [id…] }` (arrays of at most ten ids), **`has`** = booleans (never `null`) `points`, `navaids`, `airways`, `airspaces`, `fir`, `procedures`, `ground`, each « at least one record » of the count with the same name (`has.points` is **points only**, not navaids). **A count of 0 is « no »**, there is no « unknown » in a count: a zone with no readable file has zeros (« nothing found », not a guess). **Source ids: a closed list**, the keys of `SOURCES` of `tools/regen/base_sources.py`: `airnav`, `dfs`, `dircam`, `enaire`, `faa`, `faaclass`, `lfv`, `mora`, `nat`, `nats`, `navcan`, `openaip`, `sia`, `ukmil`, `xplane` — **there is no free text and no licence in the answer**: the **licence (`lic`), credit, publisher and `localOnly` of a source are the catalogue's** (`AeroDb.sources().SOURCES` → `frame/aerodb/catalogue.js`, by the same ids, fields `lic` and `pub` [fr, en, de, es], `credit`, `localOnly`); `xplane` is read on this workstation only (never in the base). An id the page does not know: ignore it.
3. **Speed and refusals**: about **1 s for the 71 zones** (it reads the JSON files of the last cycle of each zone), so « lecture en cours » for a second is enough. It is a **job like `base/status`**: **read-only** (it does not replace the last job that wrote, `READ_ONLY`), **but the launcher runs one job at a time**, so it is **refused with a 409 while another job runs** (a build, a pickup): ask it again when the job is over; it never blocks a build.
4. **A real answer, three countries** (user's data, 11 Nov 2026; `se` and `us` still at parser 23: not yet rebuilt):
```json
{ "ok": true, "zones": {
  "fr": { "cycle": "2026-10-29", "parserVersion": 28,
    "counts": { "aerodromes": 1872, "navaids": 424, "points": 4277, "airways": 1493, "airspaces": 2559, "vrp": 1121, "fir": 15 }, "procedures": 6, "ground": 1,
    "sources": { "declared": ["sia", "dircam", "openaip", "mora", "xplane"], "present": ["dircam", "openaip", "sia"] },
    "has": { "points": true, "navaids": true, "airways": true, "airspaces": true, "fir": true, "procedures": true, "ground": true } },
  "se": { "cycle": "2026-10-29", "parserVersion": 23,
    "counts": { "aerodromes": 217, "navaids": 84, "points": 0, "airways": 0, "airspaces": 380, "vrp": 272, "fir": 0 }, "procedures": 0, "ground": 0,
    "sources": { "declared": ["lfv", "openaip", "mora", "xplane"], "present": ["openaip"] },
    "has": { "points": false, "navaids": true, "airways": false, "airspaces": true, "fir": false, "procedures": false, "ground": false } },
  "us": { "cycle": "2026-10-29", "parserVersion": 23,
    "counts": { "aerodromes": 20782, "navaids": 1400, "points": 0, "airways": 0, "airspaces": 9230, "vrp": 0, "fir": 0 }, "procedures": 2257, "ground": 0,
    "sources": { "declared": ["faa", "faaclass", "openaip", "mora", "xplane"], "present": ["faa", "faaclass", "openaip"] },
    "has": { "points": false, "navaids": true, "airways": false, "airspaces": true, "fir": false, "procedures": true, "ground": false } } } }
```
(`sources.present` lacking `lfv` for `se` and `faa` having no NASR file yet is what « Mettre à jour » fixes; `declared` that lists `xplane` and `mora` for every zone is the catalogue's list: show `present` for « source », `declared` for « pas encore lue ».)

**Sources dropped by hand: the step-by-step** (SOG-0189, 10 Nov 2026; shape for the Données screen). `AeroCatalogue.stepsOf(id, cycle?)` returns, for a source the user takes from the Downloads folder, `null` otherwise:
`{ id, page: { url, home }, files: [{ name, url?, gives }] }` where **① `page.url`** is the DIRECT link of the page the file is on (resolved for the cycle: the SIA's product page of the cycle; the user's links to the letter for Sweden), **`page.home`** the front page of the site (**the fallback to show when the direct link no longer answers**: the screen checks, the catalogue does not), **② `files`** each file to take: **`name`** (the exact name; `<…>` is the part that changes with the cycle), **`url`** (the direct link of the file itself, when there is one: `AIP_OFFLINE.zip`, `MIAM_OACI_ENR 5.7.pdf`), **`gives`** (four languages: what it brings) ; **③** « Prendre le fichier téléchargé » is the screen's own button. Covered (`fetch.how = 'pick'`, in use): `sia` (zone fr), `dircam` (fr), `vac` (fr), `nats` (gb: the three sets AIP, OBS, UAS), `lfv` (se), `eurocontrol_rad` (RAD and FRA workbooks). Not covered: the sources the launcher downloads itself (no file to take); Naviair (no reader yet, see the licence request); X-Plane CIFP (candidate). K141.

**Denmark in the step-by-step; progress of `weather/world`** (SOG-0189 / SOG-0198, 10 Nov 2026). The source **`naviair`** (zone `dk`) is in the catalogue as a **candidate**: `fetch.how: 'pick'` (the user drops the PDFs, glob `EK_*.pdf`), `build.runtime: 'none'`, `publish: false`, `localOnly: true`, so `stepsOf('naviair')` gives the step-by-step and the line reads « à déposer ; lecture à venir »: **the launcher reads nothing of it** (the guard is unchanged: the terms, GEN 0.1 § 6, forbid reproduction without the written permission of the Danish CAA, `NAVIAIR_LICENCE_REQUEST.md`). Links to the letter of the user's note: page `https://aim.naviair.dk/en/` (lists the current files; home `https://aim.naviair.dk/`); `EK_ENR_3_2_en.pdf` (direct link given, the **AIRAC 06/24** edition: the step says so); `EK_ENR_2_1_en.pdf` (airspaces, name by the pattern of the others, found on the page: no direct link); `EK_AD_2_EKOD_RNP_RWY_24_2_en.pdf` (direct link given, procedures, page 2). K141.
`weather/world` (launcher job): the builder prints `@etape k/n` after each forecast time and the job reports `job.step('synoptique monde : échéance k sur n', k, n, 'étapes')` (it called `step` once, « 0 / 1 étapes » for 119 s); the progress lines are not kept in the job's log. The output is read as it comes (a full pipe could freeze the tool). K111.

**The list of the cycle: `base/cycle`** (SOG-0259, 12 Nov 2026, K179; contract for the Data screen). A READ-ONLY verb (`{ personal?: [source ids] }`; no write, no network, no file opened: only the NAMES of the first level of the launcher's download folders are listed, as `base/pickup` does). Answer: `{ ok, cycle: { inForce, next, ids: { inForce, next } }, items, next, unread }`. **`ids`**: the EUROCONTROL identifier of a cycle (« 2611 » = 29 Oct 2026, « 2612 » = 26 Nov). **`items`**, one per source of the catalogue: `{ id, label[4], regime: 'auto' (the launcher takes it) | 'hand' (waits for the user's hand) | 'local' (computed or read on this workstation), personal, zoneCount, zones? (omitted for the common sources), steps: { download: { state: 'done' | 'late' | 'missing' | 'waiting' | 'n/a', cycle?, at }, build: { zones, upToDate }, publish: { published, unpublished, unknown } }, why?, hand?: { link, expected: [name patterns], inDownloads: [names] } }`. **`late`** = taken but older than the cycle in force (distinct from **`missing`**: no file at all; **`waiting`**: no file held, one already in Downloads by its name); `why`: `older-than-cycle` | `newer-in-downloads` (a file of the cycle in force waits in Downloads) | `no-file` (hand) | `not-downloaded` (auto). A source published by editions (NAV Portugal) is never `late` for the cycle alone. **`next`**: `{ cycle, items: [ { id, state: 'in-downloads' | 'to-take' | 'will-be-taken', files } ] }` — what Downloads already holds for the NEXT cycle (by the names: SIA, NATS, EUROCONTROL RAD and FRA points), what is still to take by hand, what the launcher will take « when it is in force ». **`unread`**: `[ { id, files, reason: 'no-reader' | 'format', note } ]` — what the workstation sees in Downloads and the suite does not read yet (AIP Canada, Naviair, the XML of openAIP): a line like the others with its reason; a name that can be of two States is said as it is (`EK_`: Denmark OR Faroe; the content is never opened here). The ticks are set by the tool from the real state (the dates of the files, `base/status`, the publication per zone of SOG-0258).

**The view per zone: `base/cycle { zone }`** (SOG-0262, 12 Nov 2026, K182; contract for the country sheet). Same verb, same read-only rules; `zone` (one identifier, `^[a-z0-9_-]{2,12}$`) adds `zoneView` to the answer: `{ zone, known, inCoverage, state, sources, addWillAsk }`. **`known`**: false when the zone does not exist (nothing else is given). **`inCoverage`**: false for a country the suite does not cover yet. **`state`**: `{ state, builtCycle, builtAt, expectedCycle, publish, notices, reasons: [codes] }` (the same fields as `base/status`, null for a zone outside the coverage). **`sources`**: the items of the list, one per source that feeds THIS zone, each with its three steps computed for this zone alone (`build`: `{ zones: 1, upToDate: 0|1 }`, `publish`: one of the three counts at 1). **`addWillAsk`**: what adding the country will ask: `{ sources: [ids], auto: [ids], hand: [{ id, expected: [names] }], personal: [ids] (the personal-use tick), noReader: [ids] (files the pilot deposits by hand that the suite does not read yet) }`. `noReader` comes from the launcher (`NO_READER_ZONES` in `tools/regen/base_cycle.py`: Naviair for dk, fo, gl; AIP Canada for ca): a new source that is deposited and not read is said here, not discovered afterwards.

**What, where and file of a file taken by hand** (SOG-0262, 12 Nov 2026, K183; contract for the Data screen). `AeroCatalogue.stepsOf(id, cycle)` → `{ id, page: { url, home }, files: [ { name, file, url?, link?, gives[4], what[4], where[4], verified } ] }`. **`what`**: how the pilot names the file (« l'ISO de l'eAIP »). **`where`**: the path on the site, rubric by rubric, separated by ` › ` (« NAV Portugal AIS › AIS PRODUCTS › eAIP › eAIP downloads › Publications In Force › eAIP - ISO Disk (complete eAIP) »). **`file`**: the expected name (the `name`, except when this one describes more than it names). **`link`**: the page of THIS file when it is not `page.url`. **`verified`**: `true` when the path was followed on the site; `false` when the site refused or did not answer (never bypassed, never guessed): the screen then says `AeroCatalogue.PATH_TO_CHECK[lang]`. Not verified today: FAA NASR (three CSV, 403), UK Mil ENR 5.2, the FRA points file of EUROCONTROL, DIRCAM ENR 5.7, VAC. Naviair: `EK_ENR_*` are Denmark AND the Faroe Islands, `BG_ENR_*` Greenland; the same name is told apart by `where`.

**FAA NASR: one file; sources unavailable at the publisher** (SOG-0273, 12 Nov 2026, K184, K185; contract for the Data screen). (1) FAA: `AeroCatalogue.stepsOf('adds', cycle)` gives ONE file, `28DaySubscription_Effective_<AAAA-MM-JJ>.zip`, with `page.url` = `…/NASR_Subscription/<date>/` and `files[0].url` = `https://nfdc.faa.gov/webContent/28DaySub/28DaySubscription_Effective_<date>.zip` (both resolved for the cycle). `POST /__setup/base/upload` recognises it (`source: faa, zone: us, name: 28DaySubscription_Effective_<date>.zip, cycle`, `deposited: [FIX, AWY, NAV]`, `missing: []`) by the zip `CSV_Data/<DD_Mon_YYYY>_CSV.zip` + `FIX.txt` and the name that says the cycle; the three separate sets (`<DD>_<Mon>_<YYYY>_<FIX|AWY|NAV>_CSV.zip`) stay admitted and counted as before. The fetch of the zone reads the subscription in place (`tools/faa_nasr.py sub_blobs`), nothing is extracted. The shapefile of the airspace classes (`Additional_Data/Shape_Files/Class_Airspace.shp`, 390 MB) is in the same zip and is NOT read yet. (2) `unavailable`: the catalogue carries `unavailable: { since: '2025-11', note[4] }` on `dircam` and `ukmil` (`stepsOf` passes it through); `base/cycle` answers `unavailable: [ids]` and, for these items, `unavailable: { since }`, `steps.download.state: 'unavailable'`, `why: 'unavailable-at-publisher'`, and `next.items[].state: 'unavailable'`: the screen shelves them apart (neither « late » nor « to do »). `base_cycle.UNAVAILABLE` and the catalogue say the same (K185).

**NAT routes: validity and on-demand refresh** (SOG-0263, 12 Nov 2026, K186, K187; contract for the Data screen and the Map layer). `await AeroDb.natState()` → `{ present, count, from, validUntil (ISO UTC, the latest end of validity of the tracks), expired (true: none is valid any more; null: unknown), current (tracks valid now) }`: read from the zone already open, nothing asked to the network; the row says « valides jusqu'à <validUntil> » or « périmées ». `await AeroDb.natEnsure()` → the same state plus `refreshed` (a refresh was attempted) and `ok` (it succeeded; absent when nothing was asked): when `expired !== false` and the launcher is the workstation's own (not paired), it asks `regen/job { set: base, verb: update, args: { zones: [nat], personal: [], fetch: true } }` ONCE (concurrent calls share the job; no new try within 5 minutes after a failure), then drops the open zone and reads the new state. No timer anywhere: call it when the NAT layer is displayed or the row is opened. The « Ouvrir » button of the row goes (raw JSON); a file taken by hand stays possible when the site cuts. The A → B computation does not read the tracks.

**Archive of FRA workbooks; DIRCAM; France files not read yet** (SOG-0273, 12 Nov 2026, K188; contract for the Data screen). `POST /__setup/base/upload` of a zip whose members are `eurocontrol-airac-<cycle>-fra-points-….xlsx` (at least two, no `[Content_Types].xml`): only the members of the cycle in force and of the next are taken, posted under their own names in `data/local/upload/` (read by `route/import-rad`), the archive is dropped; answer `{ ok, source: eurocontrol_rad, kind: 'fra', name (the first), names: [all taken], size }`; an archive with none of the two cycles: `unrecognised`, the message names the two cycles. DIRCAM: the ENR 5.7 PDF is parsed BY THE PAGE (it writes `miam-enr-5-7-tracks.xml`), not by the launcher (no PDF reader in the standard library): the catalogue points at `info_aero_dircam.zip` (member `PDF/MIAM/MIAM_OACI_ENR 5.7.pdf`), and taking the member out of the 1.3 GB archive is to be done where the PDF is read (page side, File slice + central directory) — not done. `base/cycle.unread` gains `aip_fr_datasets` (`ObstaclesDataZone1MFRANCE_*.xlsx`, `AD.2.10_MIL*.zip`, `LF_Amdt_*_AIP_en.pdf`, reason `no-reader`).

**Built cycle, and unavailable sources in `base/fetch`** (12 Nov 2026, K189, K190; contract for the Data screen). `base/status.zones[z].builtCycle` is now the newest built folder NOT later than `max(expectedCycle, cycle in force)`: a later folder (built early) is ignored until its cycle comes. `base/fetch` answers `unavailable: [{ zone, source, error, since }]` (absent when empty) for a source of the list `UNAVAILABLE` (UK Mil today; the same list as `base/cycle.unavailable` and the catalogue); its line in the view has `state: 'unavailable'` (new value, with `error`); it is not in `failed`, `ok` is not lowered, the zone is `done`.

**Last results: `base/last`** (12 Nov 2026, K191; contract for the country sheet). Read-only, no argument → `{ ok, results: { 'base/pickup' | 'base/fetch' | 'base/build' | 'base/update': { job, state: 'done' | 'cancelled' | 'error', ended (epoch s), ok, reason, args, result } } }`, `results: {}` without journal (or a broken file). `result` is the verb's own answer, trimmed: lists capped at 50 (a final `{ more: n }` counts the rest), texts at 300 characters, secrets and the data folder path removed, keys starting with « _ » dropped. Written by the launcher at the end of each of the four jobs into `data/local/last_results.json` (one entry per verb, the last job replaces the previous one); never committed.

**`base/cycle.local`; `natEnsure` busy** (12 Nov 2026, K185, K186). The sources of regime `local` (computed or read on this workstation: MORA, X-Plane) are no longer in `items`: `local: [ { id, label[4], at (ISO UTC of their latest file or null), personal, zoneCount } ]`. `await AeroDb.natEnsure()` adds `busy: true` and `running` (the launcher's `running` of the 409: set, verb, …) when the launcher already runs another operation: `refreshed: false`, no failure counted, the next call may try again at once. **Read again** (16 Nov 2026, SOG-0372, K277): after the job, the zone's paths and `sia/base/index.json` are revalidated (`reader.revalidate`, else `evict`) and the list of published cycles dropped BEFORE the answer — the launcher writes outside the data layer; a call during a running job waits for it and answers the new state with `joined: true`; a call refused because the last try failed less than 5 minutes ago carries `retryAt` (ISO UTC).

**DIRCAM ENR 5.7 PDF** (SOG-0273, K192). `POST /__setup/base/upload` of a `%PDF` named `MIAM_OACI_ENR 5.7.pdf` (any « (n) » suffix) → `{ ok, source: 'dircam', zone: 'fr', name: 'MIAM_OACI_ENR 5.7.pdf', size }`; the file is kept in `data/local/upload/`. The reading is on the PAGE side (pdf.js + `AarTracks.parseEnr57` → `miam-enr-5-7-tracks.xml`, written to `data/sia/` by the page; `file_source` routes that name to `dircam`): the page takes the member out of `info_aero_dircam.zip` (`zipmember.js`), parses it, writes the XML — the launcher reads nothing of the PDF.

**`base/coverage.procEmpty`** (SOG-0264, K194). Per zone, `procEmpty: [ICAO]`: the aerodromes whose procedure record (`data/proc/manual/<ICAO>.json`) holds NO procedure; they are not counted in `procedures` and `has.procedures`, and not in `procSources`: the hatching of the map is not drawn for them. The country sheet can say « enregistrement sans procédure : à réparer ».

**`proc/eaip-fetch` with no procedure read** (12 Nov 2026, K196). The answer stays `{ ok: false, reason: 'failed', message, warnings, stats }` and gains `runwaysKept: true` when the runways of the AD 2.12 page were written into `data/proc/manual/<ICAO>.json` (with `procedures: []`, the charts, pages, formats and the index entry at 0/0/0). The aerodrome then has runways in the base but no procedure; the screen can say « pistes lues, aucune procédure ».

**`AeroDb.approachSummary(icao)`** (12 Nov 2026, K163; contract for Fuel / Features SOG, SOG-0248). `await AeroDb.approachSummary('LFST')` → `{ coded, chartOnly, published, none, sia }`. The list shown for an aerodrome = the coded approaches of `procedures(icao, { doubt })` PLUS one line « carte seulement dans l'AIP » per entry of `chartOnly(icao)` (`{ name, runway, url }`, not selectable as a coded procedure). Say « aucune approche » ONLY when `none` is true (an SIA aerodrome whose file lists no approach chart and has no coded approach); outside the SIA (`sia: false`) the charts are not listed: say nothing, never « none ».

**RF (arc) legs** (12 Nov 2026, K200, K201; contract for the Map, the profile and Fuel). A procedure point of an arc leg: `{ leg: 'RF', id (end point), lat, lon, center (id), radius (NM), nm (arc length), turn: 'L' | 'R', arcGaps? }` preceded by the points of its arc: `{ leg: 'RF', id: null, lat, lon, alt: null, arc: true }` (one every 5° at most, in flight order, from the previous point to the end point): draw and measure the path through ALL points; a point with `arc: true` has no name and is never a fix of the route text. `arcGaps` (text list) says that the printed radius or arc length does not fit the positions: the record is then in doubt. New doubt code `rf-arc` (« branche en arc RF : tracé en cours ») for a record whose arc could not be developed yet: hidden unless `{ doubt: true }`; aerodb develops it when it can resolve the centre, and then serves it as `check`.

**French aircraft registry** (SOG-0270, 12 Nov 2026, K202, K203; contract for Features SOG, the aircraft creation screen). (1) `POST /__setup/base/upload` of the registry CSV (recognised by its header, whatever its name; `export.csv`, `export (1).csv`) → `{ ok, source: 'registry_fr', rows, skipped, date, size }`: the five-column derived file is written to `data/local/registry/fr_immat.json`, the original is NOT kept; errors: `unrecognised` / `too-large` with a code in the message (`line`, `headers`, `size`, `rows`, `empty`, `read`), never a line of the file. (2) `aircraft/registry` (read-only verb): `{ reg?: 'F-BHHH' | 'FBHHH', type?: 'cessna 172', limit?: 1..200 }` (reg or type) → `{ ok, source, date, rows: [{ reg, maker, model, serial, base }], count, truncated }`, or `{ ok: true, rows: [], absent: true }` without registry. The type is a LABEL (maker + model), a SUGGESTION: match it to our types by the local type base, show an unknown type as it is, never guess; `maker` is EMPTY when fewer than 10 aircraft of the file carry it (amateur builders are persons: say « constructeur non précisé »); `base` (aerodrome d'attache) is empty for 55 % of the rows: « où ? » is answered only when it is there. The screen cites « Registre français des immatriculations, DGAC, données du <date> » and says that the type is a suggestion. UC1 (types → registrations, France first), UC2 (registration → type), UC3 (« c'était quoi ? »). (3) `base/cycle.local` has `{ id: 'registry_fr', label[4], at }` when the file exists. No owner name or address exists anywhere in what the launcher keeps or answers.

**RF arcs: what the consumers must use and check** (12 Nov 2026, K204). `AeroDBProcedures.fixesOf(points)` = the NAMED FIXES of a procedure (never an arc point, never an `id` null): use it for the points list, locks, time over zone, the FMS / PLN export and the route text. `AeroDBProcedures.pathOf(points)` = every positioned point in flight order, arcs included: use it for the Map, the profile and the distances. Arc points carry `arc: true`, `id: null`, `leg: 'RF'`, the `phase` of their leg and no altitude. To check on the consumer side: (BUGS SOG, Map and profile) the polyline of a procedure goes through ALL the points (an arc is a curve of 20+ points), no label on an arc point, no snapping to it; (Features SOG, Fuel) distances and time over zone follow the path (the arc length is the printed `nm` of the RF point, already counted by following the arc points), the FMS / PLN / route export lists named fixes only and does not emit a fix for an arc point (an RF leg may be exported as its end point), the procedure list shows the `rf-arc` doubt in words while the arc is not drawn.

**`steps.why` and `partNamesOf`** (12 Nov 2026, K206; contract for the country sheet). `AeroCatalogue.stepsOf(id).why` = `[fr, en, de, es]` — why this source is taken by hand, only what is KNOWN (SIA, DIRCAM, UK Mil, NAV Portugal, FAA today); no `why` key for a source without a known reason (show no line). `AeroCatalogue.partNamesOf(zone)` = `[[fr, en, de, es], …]` in display order for a zone that is not in one piece (« PT · 3 zones : continent, Madère, Açores »; also ES, NL, DK, NO, US) or `null` (one piece, or parts not named: never a guessed name). Synchronous, data only.

**`base/cycle.datasets`** (12 Nov 2026, K207; contract for the Data screen header and « Tout mettre à jour »). `datasets: [ { id: 'procedures' | 'weather' | 'nat' | 'vfr' | 'aircraft' | 'sheets', label[4], state: 'up-to-date' | 'todo' | 'n/a', why?: 'absent' | 'older-than-cycle' | 'old' | 'expired', at (ISO UTC of the file or null), … } ]`, no network. What each set knows of itself: **procedures** — `cycle` (newest CIFP cycle), `counts: { cifp, eaip }` (aerodromes), `todo / older-than-cycle` when the CIFP cycle is older than the cycle in force and no aerodrome is read by hand; **weather** (the synoptic) — `run` (GFS run), `at` (build), `todo / old` beyond 12 h; **nat** — `validUntil`, `todo / expired` once all their messages have ended (the page's `AeroDb.natEnsure()` takes them again on demand); **vfr** — `counts.aerodromes`, `generatedAt`; **aircraft**, **sheets** (performance sheets) — only the date of their file (`n/a`: no due date). `n/a` means « exists, no due date »: count it as done in « n done on m ». A FAILED last operation of a set is not here: it is in `base/last` (pickup, fetch, build, update); the sets of aircraft, vfr, proc and weather are not yet covered by that journal. The first line « Cycle AIRAC 2611 » stays `cycle.ids`.

**Publishing a zone: its state, and « Publier »** (SOG-0258, 12 Nov 2026, K178; contract for the Data screen). **A) State.** `base/status` zones carry **`builtAt`** (ISO UTC: the last successful build of the zone on this workstation, from `data/local/build/<zone>.json`; null if none) and **`publish`**: `{ state: 'published' | 'unpublished' | 'unknown', commit (the SHA of the last commit that touched `sia/base/<zone>/`), at (its date), reason? }`, or `null` for a zone with no build commit. **Published** = that commit is an ancestor of the remote branch the last synchronisation saw (`@{u}`; no network call), **unpublished** = that commit is not sent, or the zone changed since (uncommitted), **unknown** = no repository or no known remote branch (`reason: 'no-repo' | 'no-remote'`): nothing is then said published. Four git calls for all the zones. **B) « Publier »** is the verb **`base/publish { zone, dry? }`** (`dry` true by default): the **plan** `{ ok, zone, state, commit, ahead: [zone ids], commits, otherFiles, pending, nothing }` — `ahead`: the OTHER built zones not published, which leave with this one (their ids: they are zones of the base); `commits`: the commits not sent; `otherFiles`: the NUMBER of the user's files (outside `sia/base/`) committed and not sent; `pending`: the number of files the synchronisation will commit (outside `sia/base/`); never a file name of the user; `ok: false` with `reason` `bad-zone` | `no-repo` | `no-remote` | `not-built`. With `dry: false` it is the **synchronisation of `data/`** (the « Synchroniser » gesture: commit with the trigram of the page, merge, push) and answers `{ ok, zone, state (after), sync: { ok, pushed, committed, received, conflicts, refused, message?, cause? } }`; `reason: 'no-author'` without a trigram. **Said as it is: one push carries EVERY unsent commit — the other zones and the user's data; there is no isolation of one zone** (true isolation is a separate decision of the user, Q-0025). The screen shows the plan before the real run and asks the accord.

**The running operation: « busy » says which one, one can stop it, a silent one is released** (SOG-0245, 12 Nov 2026, K175; contract for the Data screen). *Vitals* of a job (added to its view while it runs, and to every « busy » refusal): `seen` (its last sign of life, epoch s: a step, a log line, a beat), `silent` (seconds since), **`alive`**: `'alive'` (a sign within 120 s) | `'quiet'` (no sign for 120 s: say « silencieuse depuis … ») | `'stuck'` (no sign for 300 s: « ne répond plus ») | `'gone'` (its thread is no longer there), `cancelling` (an arrest was asked). **Every refusal « busy »** carries `running` = `{ id, set, verb, args: { zones?, icao?, action? } (zones cut at 12), started (epoch s), step (the text), done, total, unit, seen, silent, alive, cancelling }`: the job start (`409 { reason: 'busy', job, running }`) and the upload of a file (`POST /__setup/base/upload` → `409 { reason: 'busy', running }`). The screen names the operation from `set/verb` (the launcher gives ids, the screen has the words ×4), shows `started`, `step`, `done / total`, `alive`, and offers: **« Voir »** (back to the follow of that job: `GET /__setup/regen/job?id=<id>` gives its view with the vitals), **« Arrêter »** (`POST /__setup/regen/job/cancel { id }` — cooperative: the verb stops at its next check) and, for `stuck` or `gone`, **« Libérer »**: `POST /__setup/regen/job/release { id }` (this workstation only, like the others) → `200 { ok: true, job, released: true }`, or `409 { reason: 'alive', silent }` (the job gave a sign: cancel it first), `409 { reason: 'not-running' }`, `410` (no such job). The launcher checks before freeing: the job must be `stuck` or `gone`; the processes the verb declared (`job.track(proc)`: the build's browser, the world forecast tool) are killed; the job ends `cancelled` with `result.reason = 'released'`; what the abandoned thread writes later is dropped; the slot is free at once. The thresholds are `QUIET_S` 120 and `STALE_S` 300 (`tools/regen_jobs.py`). A verb that waits long without steps calls `job.beat()`.

**Envoi d'un fichier choisi à la main** (SOG-0199, verb side; the screen is Réglages', the client is `ctx.launcher.upload` of V3 Frame). `POST /__setup/base/upload`: **this workstation only** (header `X-SOG-Setup`, same origin; a paired device or the relay: 403), body = the BYTES of the file as they are (`application/octet-stream`, no multipart), **`X-SOG-Name` = the chosen file's name, ENCODED** (`encodeURIComponent`; the launcher decodes it, keeps its last component only, never a path: a HINT, the content decides). The size is checked against `Content-Length` BEFORE any read (**1.5 GB**: above, 413 `too-large` and the connection is closed); the body is streamed to `data/local/upload/.recv-….part` (never in memory), hashed on the way; refused at once, before the body, while a job runs (409 `busy`) or a merge waits in `data/` (409 `merge-pending`). **Recognised by the content** (`tools/base_upload.py`): a zip by its member list (never extracted): `sia` (`XML_SIA_*.xml` / `AIXM4.5_all_FR_OM_*.xml` → `export_xml_bd_SIA_AAAA-MM-JJ.zip`, zone fr), `nats` (`EG_AIP|OBS|UAS_…_AAAAMMJJ.xml`, DIFF/DELTA ignored → `EG_<jeu>_DS_AAAAMMJJ_XML.zip`, zone gb), `lfv` (cover page + ENR 4.4 of `tools/lfv_eaip.py` → `AIP_OFFLINE.zip`, zone se); a JSON by its first sign and its hint name `<cc>_<asp|apt|nav|rpp>.<geojson|json>` → `openaip`. The file is put under that canonical name in **`data/local/upload/`**, which `downloads_dirs()` lists beside Downloads: **`base/pickup` then finds it like any download** (plan, then take, then build: the upload builds nothing). **Answer**: `200 { ok: true, source, zone, name, size, cycle?, replaced, sha256 }` (`replaced: false`: the same file was already there, nothing rewritten) or `{ ok: false, reason, message, expected? }` with `reason` in a CLOSED list: `too-large` 413, `empty` 400 (also an upload cut short), `type` 415 (ni zip, ni JSON, ni PDF, ni XML), an EUROCONTROL workbook (SOG-0237: `RAD_AAMM_v<n>_<m>.xlsx`, `eurocontrol-airac-AAMM-fra-points-<text>.xlsx`, kept under that name, then `route/import-rad`) answers `source: 'eurocontrol_rad'`, `kind: 'rad' | 'fra'`; for an FAA NASR set the answer also carries **`deposited`** and **`missing`** (the sets of FIX, AWY, NAV already there for that cycle, and those still to send); `unrecognised` 422 (**`expected`: the ids of the sources, `sia, nats, lfv, openaip`**), `busy` 409, `merge-pending` 409, `disk` 507; the client keeps `reason` and `message` on a 4xx (the frame forwards `expected` too, or the message lists it). PDF, XML alone, xlsx (VAC charts, RAD, DIRCAM, Naviair) are not taken by this first lot: `unrecognised`. K146.

**A deposit read for want of network is dated** (11 Nov 2026, K166): when the download fails (the FAA's refusal, UK Mil's cut TLS) the layer reads what the user chose by hand in `data/local/upload/`, whatever its age (the folder is never cleaned: the user's decision). The line of the source in the result of `base/fetch` then carries **`deposited`** (`AAAA-MM-JJ`: the date of the file; the oldest of the three NASR sets) and the job log says « fichier déposé à la main le … (le réseau n’a pas répondu) »: the screen says « déposé le … » and a stale deposit is never taken for a reading of the day. A reading that reached the network carries no `deposited`.

**Old deposits are removed at the end of a successful build of their zone** (11 Nov 2026, the user: « nettoyer les vieux fichiers », K172; `tools/upload_cleanup.py`, called by `base/build` for each zone that is built, no verb of its own). A dated deposit of the zone (`fr`: `export_xml_bd_SIA_AAAA-MM-JJ.zip`; `gb`: `EG_<AIP|OBS|UAS>_DS_AAAAMMJJ_XML.zip`; `us`: the NASR sets `JJ_Mon_AAAA_<FIX|AWY|NAV>_CSV.zip`) is removed when **(a)** a later deposit of the same source was read by the build (its cycle is after the file's and not after the cycle read), or **(b)** its cycle is past and the cycle in force is built for the zone. **Never** the file the build read, never a later one, never an incomplete NASR set (FIX received, AWY and NAV awaited), never another name (UK Mil's PDF, the Swedish eAIP, the EUROCONTROL workbooks and the openAIP exports are replaced under their own name, never piled up). The cycle read is the one the zone was built with (`fr`, `gb`) or the `issue` of `faa_nasr.geojson` (`us`); OBS and UAS of NATS always keep their newest. The job log says each removal (name, size) and the zone line of the job carries **`cleaned: [{ name, size }]`** when something went. Deposits are SOURCES; compiled data is never purged.

**The personal-use tick is held by the launcher** (SOG-0238, K147, K173; DECISIONS 11 Nov 2026): `data/local/personal_use.json` `{ certified, at, migrated? }`, written only by the launcher through `POST /__setup/personal` (strict body `{ certified: true | false }`, or `{ certified: true, migrated: true }` once when the file does not exist; this workstation only: local network and relay 403; one console line per change) and read by `GET /__setup/personal` → `{ certified, at }`. `proc/lnm` reads this file and ignores the `personal` of the page (accepted, no effect): `not-personal` without the real tick, whatever `fetchLnm` passes. **Contract for the Data screen (Réglages)**: at each click on the box, `POST` the new value; at opening, `GET` and show the launcher's value; once, if the cache of the page says certified and the launcher has no file (`GET` gives `certified: false` and no `at`), `POST { certified: true, migrated: true }`. Limit: a script of the same origin can call the write route too (DECISIONS). `base/fetch` and the other jobs still take `personal: [sources]` from the page: to do.

**`AeroDb.lnmAvailable()`** (SOG-0210, asked by Features SOG, K170): a synchronous boolean, true when the launcher is there (`job`, `info()` answers), is not a paired device (`lan` false) and declares at least one Little Navmap base (`info().lnm` non-empty). Never a path nor a base name; the « personal use » box stays with the caller (`fetchLnm` answers `not-personal`). Fuel shows « Lire depuis Little Navmap » only when it is true.

**`AeroDb.fetchLnm(icao, { personal, signal, onProgress })`: the Little Navmap layer lives in aerodb** (SOG-0210, with Features SOG: Fuel's procedures card, its estimate (`CF.loadProcedures`) and the Map all read the procedures through aerodb, so the records must be there, like the X-Plane points). It runs the job `proc/lnm` (see below) and keeps `_procedures` **in the page's memory only** (never written, never exported, never committed); `procedures(icao)`, `procedure(id)` and `runways(icao)` then serve them with **`status: 'lnm'`, `lnm: true`, `local: true`, `source: { kind: 'lnm', ref: <database file name> }`** (rank `lnm`, level with X-Plane's: after the official, `check` and OFP readings; a procedure the published file also has keeps its place and lists the Little Navmap one among its `sources`); `on('change', fn)` receives **`{ procedures: icao, lnm: true }`**. Returns `{ ok: true, icao, count, notice, base }` or `{ ok: false, reason }`, `reason` ∈ `not-personal` (**no `personal: true`: no job is started**), `bad-icao`, `no-launcher`, `paired`, `busy`, `cancelled`, `no-lnm`, `not-found`, `no-procedures`, `unreadable`, `failed`. **`personal: true` is the user's single personal-use box** (Réglages, SOG-0162): the caller reads it (`cache/data/personal.json`, `{ certified: boolean }`, `keptPersonal` of `modules/data/model_sets.js`) and never sends `true` by default; aerodb does not read the box itself. `forgetLnm(icao?)` drops the records (of a terrain, or all), `lnmState()` says `{ ICAO: { count, base, notice, at } }` (to mark « lues de Little Navmap sur ce poste »). K151.

**`AeroDb.chartOnly(icao)`: the approaches that exist as charts only** (SOG-0232 / SOG-0216, for Fuel's « carte seulement dans l'AIP » line). `[{ name, runway, url }]` (France, SIA only; `[]` otherwise): the IAC charts of the terrain's `charts.items` (file `proc/<cycle>/<OACI>.json` or `proc/manual/<OACI>.json`) that **no approach record carries**, tested with `procedures(icao, { doubt: true })` (read, in doubt or from another source) through `chartFor`; `name` from the chart's file name (`ILS LOC RWY 05`, `FNA` dropped), `runway` the first runway, `url` = `charts.base` + `Cartes/<OACI>/<file>` (composed here, like `chartUrl`, never elsewhere). **The `doubt` field of a record** (`procedures(icao, { doubt: true })`): a string of codes separated by a comma, from the closed list `unread` (lines of the table not read), `name`, `repeat` (a point repeated outside a hold), `zigzag`, `unresolved` (points without position), `geometry` (printed course or distance against the positions). K163.

**DIRCAM precoding pages (SOG-0203, LFOE).** The military terrains of the French AIP (DIRCAM) publish their RNP approaches as « PRECODING RNP RWY nn » pages that the SIA coding-table reader recognises (`sia-code`, fingerprint `3021d8b493`) but whose rows do not print the empty cells: `IF OE401 2200 2200 220 / RNP APCH RNAV 1`, `TF IOE04 329 329.9 5.0 2100 2100`, `TF RW04 Yes 039 039.8 5.0 -3.0 / 15`. `proc_sia._dircam_leg` reads such a row (only when the column reading fails, only for approach pages, never a row that has a dash) by PATTERNS: point, `Yes` (overfly), `L` / `R`, magnetic course followed by true course, distance (one decimal, after a course), vertical angle (`-3.0 / 15`), integers of 500 and more are the altitudes (mini, maxi), below 500 the speed; a TF without course or distance, a word it does not know, three altitudes: not read (the row stays « illisible », said). The two pages of LFOE: 12 and 10 rows read of 12 and 10 (8 and 7 before), 5 procedures. K148. **Warnings for the screen**: `fetchProcedures` returns `warnings` (the strings of the file: « page NON lue, format à vérifier : 8 tronçon(s) lu(s) sur 12 lignes de tronçon: moins de 85 % », …) with a failure; « 2 avertissements » without their text is the screen's.

**`base/status`: `download` per zone** (SOG-0180, Réglages: « sources du cycle déjà présentes »). `zones[id].download = { done: bool, cycle: 'AAAA-MM-JJ' (the AIRAC cycle in force), at: ISO UTC | null (the OLDEST of the last file dates of the zone's downloadable sources), sources: { <id>: { present: bool, at: ISO UTC | null } } }`. The sources counted are those the launcher downloads (`base/fetch`: openaip, faa, faaclass, enaire only with `personal: ['enaire']`, ukmil, dfs, airnav, navcan, nat): not the hand-dropped (sia, nats, lfv, dircam), not the local-only (xplane) nor the computed (mora); openAIP is left out when the launcher knows it has no export of the country for this cycle (`tools/.openaip_absent.json`) or the zone has no country code. **`done`**: every counted source has a file written since the start of the cycle in force (a zone with no counted source: `done: false`, `sources: {}`). Read only, no network; the files are those of `data/sia/` attributed by `file_source`. For `base/fetch`, the real shapes of a source answering 403 are in the view `zones.<z>.sources.<s> = { state: 'failed', error: 'http', status: 403 }` and the result `failed: [{ zone, source, error: 'http', status: 403 }]`, `reason: 'partial'` even when nothing was taken (read `fetched` / `unchanged`). K158.

**Little Navmap as a fallback for the procedures of ONE terrain: job `proc/lnm`** (SOG-0210, 10 Nov 2026, decision of the ergonomics session; the gesture « Lire depuis Little Navmap » is Fuel's). `launcher.job('regen/job', { set: 'proc', verb: 'lnm', args: { icao, personal: true } })`: **`personal: true` is the user's personal-use box, mandatory** (the data are Navigraph / Jeppesen's: personal licence); the request is closed (`icao`, `personal`; a path, a file name or any other field: 400). The job opens, READ-ONLY (`mode=ro&immutable=1`: never copied, no `-wal` / `-shm`), the `little_navmap_<kind>.sqlite` of the workstation (the launcher's own folder `lnm_db_folder`; navigraph first, then xp12, msfs24, msfs, p3d, fsx; the logbook, track, user and online databases are skipped) which knows the terrain and has procedures. **Result** `{ ok: true, icao, source: 'lnm', base (the file's NAME), count, notice, _procedures: [records] }`: `_procedures` is for the page only (never kept in `tools/.regen_last.json`), the records are in the common schema (`id` `ICAO.KIND.NAME.RWnn[.TRANSITION]`, `kind` SID | STAR | APP, `type` for an approach, `runways`, `transition`, `points` with `phase` main | trans | missed, `alt` min | max | at | between, `turn`, `crs`, `nm`, `kt`, `vpa`, `over`, `rnp`) plus **`status: 'lnm'`, `lnm: true`, `source: { kind: 'lnm', ref: <file name> }`**; **the page keeps them in memory** (a local layer like the X-Plane points), marks them « lues de Little Navmap sur ce poste », and never exports nor commits them: **the job writes nothing** (not in `data/`, not in `data/local`). A SID is the rows of `approach` with suffix `D`, a STAR suffix `A`, the others approaches; a transition record is the transition's legs followed by the procedure's own, as the CIFP records are. **Refusals** `{ ok: false, reason, icao }`, closed list: `not-personal`, `no-lnm` (no database on the workstation), `not-found` (the terrain is in no database), `no-procedures` (in a database, without procedure), `unreadable` (not SQLite, unknown schema). Read-only job (`READ_ONLY`: it does not replace the last build); a local-network device cannot start any job (`regen/job` is a `poste` route). On the user's workstation (Navigraph database): KDEN gives 917 records (252 SID, 561 STAR, 104 approaches, 1.3 MB) in 0.1 s. K147.

**One job at a time, and the one exception (SOG-0206, 10 Nov 2026).** The launcher runs ONE job at a time (409 `{ reason: 'busy', job }` for a second). The page starts `ground/fetch` (about 30 s) by itself after the procedures of a terrain: a route check asked then was refused and the screen said « n'a pas répondu ». **`route/compute` now starts beside a `ground/fetch`** (`regen_jobs.SIDE` / `BACKGROUND`: a computation only reads the base and the procedures; the ground read only writes `proc/ground/`): one beside at a time, never beside another job, followed (`GET regen/job?id=`) and cancelled by its own id. `RE.run` (`modules/fuel/route_engine.js`) returns `{ ok: false, reason }` when the job gave no result: `busy` (409), `offline`, `stopped` (cancelled / interrupted), `failed`; the screen says it with « Réessayer ». K145.

**Why `AIP_OFFLINE.zip` was refused** (SOG-0189 item 4): not the name (the launcher's rule `^[\w .()+-]{1,150}$` accepts it, `AIP_OFFLINE*.zip` is the pattern of `base/pickup` for `lfv`) but the **page**: `PICKUP_DEST` of `modules/data/model_sets.js` listed `sia` and `nats` only, so `zipSendable` was false for any `lfv` entry and the page filed it under « blocked », whose text says « nom de fichier à renommer ». One line added (`lfv: 'data/sia/zones/se/'`); the verb accepts the zip as it is. L16.

**VFR points: « en partie » (SOG-0191, 10 Nov 2026).** *Survey: where the information was lost.* (1) `vfr_import.plan` wrote `source.cycle` from `src.get('cycle') or src.get('sourceAirac')`, but the compiler puts the cycle in `source.airac` (and `compiled.sourceAirac`): every published terrain had `source.cycle: null` (checked on the user's data: 28 of 28, and the 418 of 23 Oct), and `sia/vfr/index.json` had **no cycle at all**. (2) The page's row reads `index.cycle`: `null` → `cycleDateOf` null → `staleLevel` null, « never late »: **« à jour »**. (3) **The worst one**: `vfr/build` compiled the charts of the cache and `plan` **removed every published terrain the compilation did not carry**: the build of 10 Nov 2026 (11:28, commit `088db87` of the data repository) on a cache of 28 charts took `sia/vfr/` from 418 terrains to 28 (390 files deleted; recoverable from `2b7e819`, not restored by this session). The stopped download and the build were both « right » for the code; nothing remembered that the cache was partial.
*What is written now.* `sia/vfr/index.json` gains **`cycle`** (the OLDEST cycle among the terrains: a set is up to date only if all its parts are; `null` as soon as a part has no known cycle), **`cycles`** `{ 'AAAA-MM-JJ': n }`, **`unknown`** (terrains of a cycle never written: those published before), **`complete`** (false = compiled from an incomplete cache) and a `cycle` on each `aerodromes[]` entry; `<OACI>.json` has `source.cycle`. `vfr/fetch` writes `data/local/vac/<cycle>/_state.json` (`{ cycle, expected, absent[], at }`: the charts the SIA answers 404 for, remembered so that they are not « missing ») and its result gains **`remaining`** (charts still to take; 0 = the cache is complete); **a new « Mettre à jour » resumes where it stopped** (the charts already in the cache are not asked again). `vfr/build` compares the cache with the aerodromes of the base of France (`sia/base/fr/<cycle>/aerodromes.json`): **complete** (nothing missing) replaces the set as before (a withdrawn terrain goes); **incomplete**, or no base to prove it, **keeps** the published terrains it does not cover, with their own cycle, and says so: `complete: false`, `expected`, `missing`, `summary.compiled`, `summary.kept`, `summary.cycles`, `summary.unknown`; the commit message says « en partie ».
*For the screen* (Réglages). `AeroDb.publication().vfr` = `{ cycle, at, count, cycles: { 'AAAA-MM-JJ': n } | null, unknown: n, partial: bool }` (`AeroDB.vfrParts(index)` is the pure reader). **`partial`** = more than one cycle, or undated parts beside dated ones, or `complete: false`: show **« en partie à jour : n en 2611, m en 2610 »** (`cycles`, counts by cycle; `unknown` as « m de cycle inconnu »), never « à jour ». `cycles: null` = an index written before this change: `partial` is false, say nothing more than today. K142.

**X-Plane fallback network** (SOG-0474 continued, Q-0108; cases K354 to K356). Launcher: `GET /__setup/xplane` → `{ ok: true, configured: bool, state: 'ok' | 'missing' | 'not-xplane' | 'unreadable' | null, cycle: '2610' | null (the folder), zones: n | null, built: '2610' | null (the cycle of the network built, 19 Nov 2026) }` (this workstation only, `_lan()` → 403; the state is RECALCULATED at each GET, the folder being revalidated; never the path); `POST /__setup/xplane { path: string }` (strict body, ≤ 260 signs) → 200 `{ ok: true, configured: true, state: 'ok', cycle }` or 400 `{ ok: false, reason }` with ONE code among `bad-body`, `too-long`, `refused` (control character, UNC, relative, `..`, link or junction, not a local drive), `not-a-folder`, `not-xplane`, and 403 `not-personal` (the tick of the LAUNCHER); `{ path: '' }` clears. The path is kept in `data/local/xplane.json` (never served, never synchronised). Verb `xplane/build` (args `{}`; the page starts it by the job route like the others): `{ ok, zones, cycle, counts }` or `{ ok: false, reason }` (`not-personal`, `not-configured`, `missing`, `not-xplane`, `refused`, `unreadable`, `too-big`, `nothing`, `no-zones`); it writes `data/local/xplane/net_<cycle>.json` `{ schema: 1, source: 'X-Plane (Navigraph), personnel', cycle, zones: { <zone>: { points, airways } }, counts }` for the zones of the region of the route (`REGION` of `tools/regen/xplane.py`) whose base has fewer than 20 segments. The result of a route that uses it: `legs[].src = 'xplane'`, every NEW point of the overlay marked `local: 'xplane'` in `legs[].start / .end` and in `points` (the page writes it by name, without position: `xpStrip`, case F255) and a REVUE « Réseau X-Plane (Navigraph), personnel, cycle …, non officiel, à vérifier ». The line of the zone in Data says « X-Plane, personnel, cycle 2610, local » (Settings). Nothing of it is ever published.
